# Changelog

Full release history for the Loomwright plugin. This file is the complete record and the only one — `CLAUDE.md` and `README.md` point here rather than carrying their own per-release summaries.

> Entries are historical: the version and count claims in each describe **that** release, not the current state. The authoritative current version/counts live in `plugin.json`, `hooks.json`, and the `agents/`/`commands/`/`skills/` directories.

**v15.90.0 — A `cmd:`/bare Executable Acceptance bullet now runs only behind a content-keyed human stamp, never merely because the run happens to be interactive (red-team-hardening item 05, "cmd: valve by provenance"):** `run-ground-truth.sh` previously gated `cmd:`/bare shell execution on a single boolean, `--no-cmd`, that Supervisor set only when `NON_INTERACTIVE == true` — but `skills/autonomous-loop/SKILL.md` forwards `--non-interactive` to the inlined `/supervisor` invocation ONLY when the OUTER `/autonomous` call itself received `--non-interactive-fallback`, so a default, interactive `/autonomous "goal"` run executed a machine-authored brief's `cmd:` bullets with full shell privileges and zero human review — the exact gap a `skills/self-heal-advisory/SKILL.md` code comment had been falsely claiming closed. New `scripts/exec-acceptance-hash.sh <brief>` prints `sha256:<hex>` of the whitespace-normalized, newline-joined list of `cmd:`/bare Executable Acceptance bullets ONLY (`corpus-task:`/`qa-executor:` excluded), or `none` when that filtered list is empty; both it and `run-ground-truth.sh` source ONE shared classification/hash definition, new `scripts/exec-acceptance-lib.sh`, so the two can never silently diverge. `run-ground-truth.sh` now tracks per-line source provenance (`--check` | `--brief` | `--checks-file` | the JSON fallback) through its check-resolution pipeline and gates execution on a NEW content-keyed comparison: a `--brief`-sourced `cmd:`/bare bullet executes only when the brief's `## Configuration` section carries a stamp line `- **Executable Acceptance Approved:** sha256:<hash>` whose hash matches the CURRENT bullet list; absent or stale (edited-since-stamped) records `unverified`/`cmd_unapproved` and executes nothing — a **BLOCKING mutation control** splices out the real hash-comparison block and confirms the stale-stamp case then WRONGLY executes, proving the gate is genuinely load-bearing. `--no-cmd` always wins regardless of stamp validity; `--check`/`--checks-file`-sourced bullets are COMPLETELY unaffected by the gate, even in a mixed invocation alongside an unstamped `--brief` in the same run. `agents/plan-reviewer.md` Criterion 14 now escalates an unstamped/stale `cmd:`/bare bullet to a dedicated **NEEDS_HUMAN** Decision Matrix row (a valid stamp keeps the existing LOW/visibility-only behavior); the "M3 graduation" forward note describing this exact behavior as a future milestone is retired — it is now the shipped behavior. `agents/launch-pad.md` Phase 6 gains action 2a: on the interactive path, `AskUserQuestion` quotes the flagged bullets verbatim with `approve-and-stamp` / `strip-cmd-bullets` / `discard`; on the non-interactive path (`--non-interactive`/`--non-interactive-fallback`, a NEW Launch Pad flag), the bullets are auto-stripped — never silently kept — and the additive `LAUNCH_PAD_RESULT` field `cmd_bullets_stripped_non_interactive: true` records it (`validate-launch-pad-result.py` accepts it as a fifth allowed key; no `schema_version` bump). The `self-heal-advisory/SKILL.md` safety-valve comment is corrected to state the true `NON_INTERACTIVE` condition and name the stamp mechanism by script name. Docs: `docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` (`## Executable Acceptance` section + `LAUNCH_PAD_RESULT`), `skills/supervisor-readiness/SKILL.md` (`## Executable Acceptance` authoring convention). Tests: new `test-exec-acceptance-hash.sh` (8 cases — cmd-only/corpus-only/mixed classification, whitespace normalization, absent-section, determinism+sensitivity, usage errors, shared-lib cross-check); `test-run-ground-truth.sh` grows 19→28 (valid/absent/stale stamp, `--no-cmd`-wins-over-valid-stamp, `--check`-only unaffected, mixed `--brief`+`--check` provenance, and the BLOCKING mutation control). Counts unchanged: 14 agents, 24 commands, 42 skills, 39 hooks (two new plain scripts, uncounted, like `automate-helpers.sh`).**

**v15.89.0 — The review drain's scoped CI-check wait is now a single blocking script call, and a drain that silently dies mid-wait is detected and recorded instead of being mistaken for a completed run (red-team-hardening item 04):** `skills/review-heal/SKILL.md`'s §"Wait-For-Settled-Checks" and §"Termination-only severity floor" were `sleep(poll_interval); re-read statusCheckRollup` pseudocode the MODEL itself executed — under `claude -p` (a headless, non-interactive session) the model's habit was to start a background poll and end its turn ("I'll wait for the background poller to notify me…"), but turn-end under `-p` IS process exit: the drain died mid-wait, and `dispatch-pr-review.sh`'s wrapper trap then salvaged and removed the worktree/lock exactly as if the run had completed normally. Measured on this checkout: 35 dispatch markers, only 4 reached `READY`, 5 reached `ESCALATED`, and 26 had no decision at all — five 2026-09-17 runs all ended mid-wait. New `scripts/wait-for-checks.sh <pr_url> --sha <sha> --bound <seconds> [--interval <s>] [--required-only | --review-check-pattern <glob>]` mechanizes §U2 (required-check discovery, fail-closed spirit) + §U2.5 (the scoped wait) exactly: foreground, BLOCKING, bash 3.2/BSD-safe (bound tracked via `$SECONDS`, no `timeout` binary), ALWAYS exits 0, prints exactly one final `SETTLED sha=<sha> required=<green|red> review_producing=<settled|elapsed>` / `ELAPSED …` line, and a rollup reported for a DIFFERENT sha than the one being waited on is NEVER treated as settled (made explicit and tested, closing a gap that was only implicit in the prior pseudocode). Every `sleep(poll_interval)` block in `review-heal/SKILL.md` (0 remaining) is replaced by a `wait-for-checks.sh` call, each immediately followed by *"Never run this wait in the background and never end the turn while waiting — under `claude -p` ending the turn ends the process and the drain dies with no result"* — the same sentence added to `agents/review-pr.md`, and `background_wait` added to the skill's Anti-Patterns list (`review-pr`'s token budget re-measured, no raise needed — 2,873 tokens of headroom remained). **Death detection:** `dispatch-pr-review.sh`'s wrapper trap now checks, after the runner process exits, `grep -q 'REVIEW_HEAL_RESULT' "$_log"` — the SOLE mechanism (BLOCKING mutation-control-proven) deciding DIED vs completed. Absent ⇒ writes `.supervisor/review-dispatch/<hash>.died` (tab-separated `ts`/`pr_url`/`exit_code`/`last_log_line`/`attempt`, via `printf`), appends `DRAIN_DIED` to the run log, and fires `notify-desktop.sh` + `send-webhook.sh --gate-type drain_died` best-effort (both fail-safe, never block the trap). **Bounded re-dispatch:** the dispatcher's own `①` existing-marker-wins guard now treats `<hash>` + `<hash>.died` (attempt 1) together as "not dispatched", allowing exactly ONE automatic re-dispatch by whichever caller invokes the dispatcher again (the `PostToolUse[Bash]` hook backstop or Supervisor step 5.5 — `hook-dispatch-on-pr-create.sh` needed no code change, since PR-idempotency lives entirely in the dispatcher it calls); a SECOND death writes `.died` with `attempt=2`, which the SAME guard now treats as genuinely dispatched/exhausted — a third dispatch is refused, bounded, never an unbounded retry loop. **Surfacing:** `session-resume.sh` gains a "Drains that died without a result" SessionStart heading (bounded to 5, inside the existing 8 KB cap); `status-line.sh` gains a `⚠ drain died` cell; `/automate`'s RECONCILE (`skills/automate-loop/SKILL.md` §4) treats a `.died` marker for the current item's PR as `owned_drain_result: died` and PARKs with `pause_reason: drain_died` — explicitly NEVER `awaiting_merge`, since a died drain never produced a `READY`/`ESCALATED` verdict to park on. Docs: `docs/HOOKS.md` (PostToolUse dispatch row), `docs/ARCHITECTURE_CONTRACTS.md` (dispatch-shape table), `docs/PITFALLS.md` (new "dispatched ≠ completed" entry naming the concrete `.died` marker to check). Tests: new `test-wait-for-checks.sh` (8 cases — pending→green, pending→red, wrong-sha→right-sha, never-settles bounded-exit, `--review-check-pattern` scope, `--required-only` scope, unusable-`gh` degrade, bad-usage degrade — all via a scripted `$GH` stub, never a network call); `test-dispatch-pr-review.sh` grows 6 cases (37–42): a no-result stub writes `.died` with all 4 fields + `attempt=1` plus `DRAIN_DIED`; a result-producing stub writes no `.died`; the bounded re-dispatch sequence covers BOTH the allowed-first-retry case (attempt 1 → automatic re-dispatch → attempt 2) AND the blocked-second-retry case (attempt 2 → third dispatch refused, no third launch); and the **BLOCKING mutation control** — neutralizing the trap's `REVIEW_HEAL_RESULT` grep in a scratch copy (`if true; then`) and re-running the no-result case confirms the `.died`-marker assertion now FAILS, proving death detection is genuinely driven by that grep, not merely asserted. `test-session-resume.sh` and `test-status-line.sh` each gain fixture-driven coverage of their new surfaces. Counts unchanged: 14 agents, 24 commands, 42 skills, 39 hooks (`wait-for-checks.sh` is an uncounted plain script, like `automate-helpers.sh`).**

**v15.88.0 — The `/automate` trusted auto-merge gate (`automate-helpers.sh gate-eval`) now resolves its own six merge conditions from live ground truth instead of trusting a model-authored `ctx.json` (red-team-hardening item 03, closing the audit's condition-6-is-only-true-of-the-script gap):** the gate previously validated only the SHAPE of every value the loop wrote (`has()` + `type == "boolean"`, exact-string checks) — none of them re-derived the value, so a `ctx.json` carrying `"high_risk": false` with zero `classify-risk.sh` runs merged a genuinely high-risk diff, and the same gap existed for `head_sha`/`base`/`review_decision`/`unresolved_human_thread`/`protection_enforceable`/`checks_green`/`rubric_satisfied`. `ctx.json` now shrinks to exactly `{drain_result, termination_reason, ready_sha, trust_unprotected, review_heal_result_path, supervisor_result_path}` — the six things ONLY the loop/drain can know — and every former gate-owned key is REFUSED if present, regardless of the value it carries: `PARK: ctx_carries_gate_owned_key`, checked before any condition is evaluated. Condition 2 (head SHA/base) is cross-checked against a live `gh pr view --json headRefOid,baseRefName,statusCheckRollup`; condition 3 (reviewDecision) against a SEPARATE `gh pr view --json reviewDecision` call (kept apart so a reviewDecision-specific read failure parks on its own reason rather than being masked by condition 2's) plus the review-threads GraphQL query verbatim from `review-heal/SKILL.md` §U1(b) — an unresolved thread now blocks unless its first-comment actor is exact-listed in the red-team-hardening-item-01 trusted-actor set (`~/.claude/loomwright/trusted-actors.json`; absent file ⇒ nobody trusted, same fail-closed posture as `wrap-external-text.sh`, no `bot_author_re` fallback at this final merge gate); condition 4 (branch protection) via `gh api repos/<o>/<r>/branches/main/protection` (404 ⇒ unprotected, any OTHER error ⇒ `PARK: protection_unreadable`, never silently either); condition 5's required-check discovery reuses that SAME protection payload cross-referenced against the combined `statusCheckRollup` read, and its rubric check is now a FILE READ — the gate itself parses `rubric_score: N/M` out of the file named by `supervisor_result_path` (N==M ⇒ satisfied, no such line ⇒ `"na"`, unreadable ⇒ `PARK: supervisor_result_unreadable`) — never a caller-asserted value; condition 6 (`high_risk`) is now computed by the gate ITSELF via `"$(dirname "$0")/classify-risk.sh" main <live head_sha> --root <root>` — no `ctx.json` input feeds it at all — still with NO override of any kind (owner decision R5 unchanged). A new condition-1 cross-check reads the artifact the loop's DRAIN step now writes VERBATIM (`.supervisor/automate/<run_id>.review-heal-result.md`) via a bounded grep/awk extraction (`result_block_parser.py` is confirmed library-only, no CLI) and PARKs (`PARK: review_heal_result_unreadable` / `PARK: drain_result_mismatch`) if the loop's `drain_result`/`termination_reason` claim disagrees with what the drain actually wrote — the drain's self-report about its own outcome is corroborated, never trusted blind. Every PARK reason from the pre-hardening gate remains reachable (`drain_not_ready`, `sub_floor_not_merge_eligible`, `head_sha_moved`, `base_not_main`, `review_decision_blocking`, `review_decision_unreadable`, `unresolved_human_thread`, `unprotected_branch`, `checks_not_green`, `rubric_unsatisfied`, `high_risk_diff`, `merge_command_failed`, `ctx_unreadable`); five new reasons cover the self-resolution surface. `skills/automate-loop/SKILL.md` §10 rewritten around the self-resolving gate and the shrunk `ctx.json`; the four "loop contract (load-bearing)" paragraphs that instructed the LOOP to pre-resolve `review_decision`/`unresolved_human_thread`/`rubric_satisfied`/`high_risk` into ctx are deleted — those conditions are gate-internal now. `skills/automate-loop/SKILL.md` §6 (RUN + DRAIN steps) gains the two artifact-write requirements (`<run_id>.supervisor-result.md`, `<run_id>.review-heal-result.md`) the gate reads back. §11's five-surface `gh pr merge --squash` executor enumeration is UNCHANGED — this hardens the existing sole executor, it adds no second merge path (verified: `grep -rn "gh pr merge --squash" loomwright/ | grep -viE "no |never |not "` still resolves to the same five files). Docs: `docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` §SUPERVISOR_RESULT's `risk_classification` paragraph now points at the gate's own `classify-risk.sh` invocation (the Phase 4.5 field is advisory-only; the gate never reads it), `commands/automate.md`'s `--auto-merge` row, and `CLAUDE.md` §"Failure-Mode Invariants" all gain the one-sentence self-resolution note. Tests: `test-automate-helpers.sh` §E rewritten wholesale against an extended `$GH` stub (now answers `pr view --json headRefOid,...`, a separate `pr view --json reviewDecision`, `api graphql`, and `api repos/.../branches/main/protection`, each independently controllable via marker files) plus a scratch-copied harness carrying a stubbed `classify-risk.sh` (the gate's sibling-lookup convention, mirroring the existing `brief-repair`/reconcile-jobs.sh precedent) — every pre-existing PARK reason re-exercised, every new one covered, and a **BLOCKING mutation control**: neutralizing the `classify-risk.sh` invocation inside a scratch copy of the gate (`if false && [ -r "$risk_bin" ]`) makes the all-green case stop merging, with an instrumented stub proving the call log shows classify-risk.sh was NEVER invoked — plus a positive control (the same instrumented stub against the un-mutated gate DOES get called and DOES merge) proving the mutation result is caused by the deleted invocation, not the instrumentation. Counts unchanged: 14 agents, 24 commands, 42 skills, 39 hooks.**

**v15.87.0 — Telemetry consent and the webhook destination move to user scope, keyed by repo, so a repo-relative file can at most REQUEST — never grant — where anything is sent (red-team-hardening item 02, closing the audit's second FATAL):** reproduced live 2026-09-21 — a scratch repo containing only a planted `.supervisor/telemetry-consent.json` = `{"telemetry":"always_allow","telemetry_repo":"attacker/sink"}` made `send-telemetry-core.sh --dry-run` resolve `TARGET_REPO=attacker/sink` with `WOULD_EXIT=0`: a cloned repo granted its own consent and chose its own destination for the user's `gh` identity to post to; the same file class (`.supervisor/config.json`'s `webhook_url`) chose where every gate/pause notification POSTed. New `scripts/resolve-egress-config.sh` — fail-safe, always exits 0, `KEY=VALUE` output — is the SOLE reader of the new user-scope store `~/.claude/loomwright/egress.json` (`{"schema_version":1,"repos":{"<slug>":{"telemetry":"always_allow"|"no","telemetry_repo":"o/r","webhook_url":"https://…","webhook_url_sha256":"…"}}}`, keyed by a repo slug derived from `git remote get-url origin` normalized to `owner/repo`, else `local:<toplevel-basename>`); it prints `TELEMETRY=`/`TELEMETRY_REPO=`/`WEBHOOK_URL=` from that entry ONLY — a repo-relative `.supervisor/telemetry-consent.json`/`config.json` is still read, but only as `REPO_REQUESTED_TELEMETRY_REPO=`/`REPO_REQUESTED_WEBHOOK_URL=`, informational and never assigned to the resolved values; `LOOMWRIGHT_TELEMETRY_REPO`/`LOOMWRIGHT_WEBHOOK_URL` process-env remain the highest-precedence override for the VALUE (never for consent itself — there is no env override that can grant). An unreadable/malformed user-scope file, or no entry for this repo's slug, resolves every value to empty and still exits 0 (fail CLOSED, never a fabricated default). `send-telemetry-core.sh` and `send-webhook.sh` both now call the resolver instead of reading their repo-relative files directly (`grep 'CONSENT_FILE="${PWD}'` returns 0 hits); a repo's request that does not byte-match (webhook: sha256-match) the user-scope entry is logged — `repo_consent_ignored slug=<slug>` / `repo_webhook_ignored slug=<slug>` — and otherwise ignored, never silently honoured and never silently posted. `/telemetry enable` now writes the user-scope entry (timestamped backup first, jq-deep-merge, abort-without-any-write on parse failure); `/telemetry status` reports both the user-scope entry and any repo-requested values with `honoured: yes|no`, and offers a ONE-TIME human-confirmed import of a pre-existing repo-relative request via `AskUserQuestion` — hooks and every other code path NEVER auto-import, which is the standing invariant this whole feature protects. `/setup webhook` gains the same guided write (backup-first, jq-deep-merge) for the webhook URL and may still mirror it into `.supervisor/config.json` for the local run-view UI, now documented as inert on its own. Docs updated in lockstep: `docs/TELEMETRY.md` (consent model, resolver contract, precedence tables), `docs/HOOKS.md` (both emitter rows), `skills/telemetry/SKILL.md`, `skills/autonomous-loop/SKILL.md` (`--notify` resolution + fail-loud-on-misconfig), `commands/autonomous.md`, `skills/review-heal/SKILL.md`, and `CLAUDE.md` §Telemetry. Tests: new `test-resolve-egress-config.sh` (55 cases — slug derivation incl. `git@`/`ssh://`/no-origin/not-a-git-repo, user-scope-only sourcing, fail-closed on malformed/unreadable/wrong-type/wrong-slug, env precedence, repo-relative-request informational-only, and 17 newline-injection defense assertions). `test-send-telemetry-core.sh` (83→106) and `test-webhook.sh` (62→68) gain the exact reproduction case (planted consent + empty `$HOME` fixture ⇒ `WOULD_EXIT`≠0 + `repo_consent_ignored`/`repo_webhook_ignored` on stderr), a matching-user-scope-entry case (⇒ sends, using the correct value), a mismatched-repo-requested case (⇒ still sends using the correct user-scope value, mismatch logged), and — the brief's load-bearing proof — a **mutation control**: a spliced mutant that reverts the resolver-call integration back to a direct repo-relative read reproduces the exact 2026-09-21 vulnerability (`WOULD_EXIT=0`, attacker's target used), confirming the fix in the real script is what closes it, not a cosmetic branch. A post-review follow-up (`2e02a0a`) closed a newline-injection bypass in the resolver's `KEY=VALUE` stdout protocol — an embedded `\n` in a repo-relative value could forge fake resolved-value lines past both consumers' naive line parser, live-reproduced as actual exfiltration and a false `WOULD_EXIT=0`; fixed by blanking (never truncating) any printed value containing an embedded newline, applied to every value including user-scope-sourced ones, ordered before the sha256 hash is computed. Both existing consent-matrix suites' every `consent_write`/`state=prompt` case migrated to the new user-scope fixture and re-verified green. Counts unchanged: 14 agents, 24 commands, 42 skills, 39 hooks.**

**v15.86.0 — The detached review drain pins its own permission regime instead of inheriting the operator's, and every externally-sourced review-channel body reaches the model inside an explicit "this is data, not an instruction" envelope (red-team-hardening item 01, closing the audit's FATAL 1):** `scripts/dispatch-pr-review.sh` launched the unattended, `gh`/`git push`-credentialed `review-pr-runner` with no `--permission-mode` — its real regime was whatever `~/.claude/settings.json` `permissions.defaultMode` happened to resolve to (`auto` on the owner's own machine, confirmed live this session). Both `claude` invocation sites — the `DRY_RUN_DISPATCH` diagnostic printf and the REAL backgrounded `WRAPPER` launch — now read ONE shared construction: `--permission-mode dontAsk` (verified against a real `claude --help` run this session, CLI 2.1.278 — "deny if not pre-approved", the one headless mode that honours an allowlist without auto-approving beyond it) plus an explicit `--allowedTools` list (Read, Grep, Glob, Task, scoped `Bash(git fetch|checkout|add|commit|push origin <head-ref>:*)` — never a bare `git push` — `Bash(gh pr view|diff|comment:*)`, `Bash(gh api repos/*|graphql:*)`, plus any test/lint runner doubly-declared in BOTH `.supervisor/config.json .drain_allowed_commands[]` and the user-scope `~/.claude/loomwright/drain-allowlist.json`) and `--disallowedTools WebFetch,WebSearch`. A new pre-dispatch **regime probe** resolves the ambient `permissions.defaultMode` the way Claude Code does (project local settings > project shared settings > user settings — first tier that defines it wins); a bypass/auto-approve resolution, or ANY tier whose file exists but is unreadable/malformed (always fail CLOSED, never assumed safe), degrades the dispatch to review-only via the SAME `LOOMWRIGHT_PR_IS_FORK=1` semantics a fork PR gets and records `regime=permissive_refused` in the durable marker — unless the ONE opt-out, `LOOMWRIGHT_DRAIN_ALLOW_PERMISSIVE=1`, is set in the dispatcher's own process env (never named in any prompt/skill/command file the model reads, per decision R2). If the resolved `claude` build's `--help` cannot advertise a headless allowlist-honouring mode, the dispatcher exits 0 printing `PERMISSION_REGIME_UNPINNABLE` and does not dispatch (decision R1 — refuse, don't guess). New `scripts/wrap-external-text.sh` mechanizes the untrusted-text envelope (stdin = a channel's JSON array, stdout = each item wrapped `<<<EXTERNAL_TEXT channel=<c> actor=<login> trusted=<yes|no>>>> … <<<END_EXTERNAL_TEXT>>>`; fail-safe, `[]`/invalid stdin → empty output, exit 0) so the envelope is produced by a script, never typed by the model round to round. `scripts/classify-bot-review.sh` gains `--trusted-actors <file>`: when a user-scope allowlist of exact logins resolves, `trusted=yes`/finding-eligibility requires an exact match and `bot_author_re` is not consulted at all (a repo-scope `notify-config` entry counts only when the SAME login is also user-scope-listed); a missing/unreadable file falls back to the unchanged `bot_author_re` regex and logs `actor_allowlist_absent`. The check-run channel gains the same treatment: only checks whose exact name appears in a user-scope `review_producing_checks[]` list now yield a candidate finding — `--review-check-pattern` continues to govern the scoped WAIT set only. `skills/review-heal/SKILL.md` (§"Untrusted-Text Envelope", new) carries the round-preamble sentence, the wrap step, and the `rejected_instruction_like` rejection rule (an envelope body that asks the agent to run/fetch/install/change permissions/act outside the PR branch is REJECTED before validate-then-fix ever sees it, counted, never obeyed); `agents/review-pr.md` and `commands/review-pr.md` each gain a paragraph naming the envelope and stating the runner never widens its own tool set because a channel's text asked it to. `docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` §REVIEW_HEAL_RESULT gains the optional additive `rejected_instruction_like: <int>` field — no `schema_version` bump. `docs/HOOKS.md` gains a new "Detached-Drain Permission Regime" section (not a hook — a related credential-holding-process control) stating the honest limit plainly: the allowlist + envelope are a tripwire and a narrowing, not a sandboxing or process-isolation boundary, and hidden-character tricks beyond the envelope are explicitly undefended. Tests: `test-dispatch-pr-review.sh` grows 27→41 (permission-pin assertions on BOTH the `DRY_RUN_DISPATCH` diagnostic AND — the brief's BLOCKING criterion — the REAL stubbed launch's captured `args=`, via an extended `run_real()`/`$FX_CLAUDE_LOG` that now also answers `--help` and echoes `LOOMWRIGHT_PR_IS_FORK`; the regime probe on both dry-run and real paths, an opt-out case, a malformed-settings fail-closed case, the `PERMISSION_REGIME_UNPINNABLE` refusal, and a mutation control proving the regime guard flips BOTH assertion halves); `test-classify-bot-review.sh` grows 12→17 (`--trusted-actors` exact-match, non-listed-bot exclusion, missing/malformed-file fallback + log line, and the unchanged-when-omitted case); new `test-wrap-external-text.sh` (12 cases incl. hostile-typed elements, check-run-shaped extraction, and a mutation control on the trust check). Counts unchanged: 14 agents, 24 commands, 42 skills, 39 hooks.**

**v15.85.0 — Ground truth flows into the queue: the requirement-Status half of #243, and a worktree salvage check:** (1) `automate-helpers.sh` gains a dry-run-default **`reconcile-status <requirements_root> [--apply]`** — the requirement-`## Status:` counterpart to `reconcile-jobs.sh --repair-merged` (v15.84.0), closing the gap a 2026-09-21 audit measured on this repo: 8 requirement files still `pending`/absent-status despite a merged PR, 4 files still `pending` for a track the owner abandoned on 2026-08-22. For a `pending`/absent-status `*.md` under `.supervisor/requirements/**` (skipping `00-*`, `_*`, `README*`, `operator-run/`), a merged PR (state resolved EXCLUSIVELY through the existing `reconcile-item` verb — no second `gh` parser) whose body cites the file's own repo-relative path, or whose head branch matches the slug on the file's associated `.supervisor/jobs/done/` brief, is stamped the §6 shape byte-for-byte (`## Status: done (PR #n, merge <sha7>)` + `- **Completed:**`/`- **Brief:**`/`- **PR:**`, any stale trailing `## Status: pending` line removed); a `.supervisor/automate/*.md` Queue row carrying `# abandoned: <reason>` stamps `## Status: done_with_escalation — ABANDONED (<row verbatim>)`; an existing `done`/`done_with_escalation` is NEVER downgraded, and a `brief-shipped` file is only LISTED, never promoted. Nothing is written without `--apply`. `/automate --resume`'s RECONCILE step now runs this dry-run pass and appends one `## Progress` line per file it would stamp, before PICK (`skills/automate-loop/SKILL.md` §4 step 7). (2) `worktree-audit.sh report` gains two ADDITIVE columns — `merged` (the worktree's HEAD is an ancestor of `origin/<base>`, base resolved via the sibling `reconcile-jobs.sh`'s new `--print-base-ref` seam, reusing `vcs_base_ref` rather than re-deriving it) and `salvage: <n> files` (distinct file count across uncommitted delta and unpushed-commit delta) — appended ONLY to a row this feature can classify, so an unclassifiable row stays byte-identical to the pre-existing format; `session-resume.sh`'s SessionStart advisory now reads the new columns and drops the "remove by hand" instruction for any worktree carrying `salvage > 0`, pointing at `worktree-salvage.sh` instead. This closes the third gap the same audit measured: 6 orphaned worktrees reported as removable, one of which held ~98 uncommitted lines never reached by `main`. Tests: automate-helpers 122→129, worktree-audit 172→176. Counts unchanged (14 agents / 24 commands / 42 skills).**

**v15.84.0 — Work-done cleanup that runs without an agent remembering it, and the judgement trail under version control:** (1) `reconcile-jobs.sh` gains an offline **vcs arm** — a brief still in `jobs/in-progress/` is attributed to the ONE merge commit on the local remote-tracking base ref whose subject is `Merge pull request #N from <owner>/…/<slug>` (`<slug>` = the brief's filename minus its date prefix, dated on/after the brief, #N not already claimed by a `done/` brief); two candidates, two briefs sharing a slug, an undated filename or no base ref ⇒ `unknown`, never a guess; the emitted `## Outcome` records the SHA, subject and rule so it can be falsified. This covers the plain `/supervisor` / `/autonomous` runs the engine seams (v15.65.0) never could — measured 2026-09-21: three briefs from PRs #177/#181/#185 sat stranded 16 days; measured recall on this repo's `done/` corpus 23/40. New `--repair-merged` flag repairs `stranded_merged` only. (2) `session-resume.sh` runs `--repair-merged` on BOTH SessionStart arms, before the 24h debounce: a merge the disk proves is moved mechanically and reported as `**Repaired brief:**` / `### Repaired briefs`; `stranded_closed` keeps the advisory-only path byte-for-byte. (3) `/setup memory`'s managed block now also un-ignores the **judgement trail** — `.supervisor/requirements/`, `.supervisor/jobs/done/` + `failed/`, `.supervisor/automate/*.md` — with `jobs/pending/`, `in-progress/`, `*.config-backup.json` sidecars and any `.supervisor/` tree a hook left nested inside a requirements folder still ignored; the consent disclosure names the new surfaces and states plainly that prose is not gated by the ledger allowlist. Tests: reconcile-jobs 72→87 (incl. a `$`-anchor mutant), session-resume 230→248 (incl. a flag-dropped mutant), setup-memory 321→337 (incl. two negative controls). Also rolls up the feature PRs that shipped between v15.81.0 and v15.82.0 without a bump: #235 rate-limit park, #236 Floor attention groups + catch-up feed, #237 Orca mirror adapter, #239/#240 provider-pluggable review lens (`lens-run.sh`, `--voter-provider`, `lens-compare.sh`). Counts unchanged (14 agents / 24 commands / 42 skills).**

**v15.83.0 — A validator-rejected stop no longer settles a worker: `emit-progress-event.sh` records the sibling validator's decision on every `subtask_complete` row, and the completion-authority join skips rejected rows:** with the command validators printing the documented `{"decision": "block"}` shape (v15.82.0), a malformed `WORKER_RESULT` makes the runtime feed the reason back and the worker CONTINUES — SubagentStop re-fires with `stop_hook_active: true` — but `emit-progress-event.sh`, wired on the same `loomwright:worker` matcher, appended a `subtask_complete` row on the rejected first firing too, and `check-children-settled.sh` read the FIRST row as terminal, so the v15.80.0 join (Execute Manager v12 gate / Supervisor Single-Agent step 3b + Sequential / FINALIZE Point 5) could report a still-running worker settled before its accepted result existed. Root cause is that the emitter could not see its sibling's stdout; the fix re-derives it: the emitter runs the real `validate-worker-result.py` as a subprocess on the same payload bytes (same cwd, same module — never a restated copy of its rules) and records an additive boolean `rejected` — `true` iff it printed `decision: block`, `false` on `{}` or the legacy `{"ok": …}` shape (which never blocked the runtime, so a stop it was printed on IS terminal), OMITTED when the validator is absent / times out / prints non-JSON (a missing sibling cannot have blocked anything, so consumers treat an absent key as terminal exactly like every pre-v15.83.0 row). `stop_hook_active` is copied verbatim when present and boolean as a reader aid — it is NOT the rejection signal (the rejected first stop carries `false`), which is why the "record `stop_hook_active`, take the LAST row" alternative was rejected: the last row is the rejected one in exactly the window that matters. Consumers: `check-children-settled.sh` treats a `subtask_complete` with `rejected: true` as not terminal (the FIRST non-rejected terminal row settles; a rejected row's `result_block_present` never decides `ended_without_result`) and adds diagnostic-only `rejected_stops` (`--agent-id`) / `rejected_stop_ids` (`--all`) so an `unsettled` verdict caused by a rejected stop is readable from the join output; `build-floor.sh` excludes rejected rows from the terminal set so a rejected stop can never derive `done`; `build-state.sh` deliberately unchanged (a rejected stop is still evidence of EXECUTE/running). Consumer prose (`agents/execute-manager.md` unsettled branch, `agents/supervisor.md` step 3b, `skills/async-orchestration/SKILL.md` Point 5) names the new cause; verdicts unchanged — `status` alone decides. Honest limit: a worker the runtime forces to stop at its consecutive-continuation cap leaves only rejected rows and stays `unsettled` — fail CLOSED toward "not done" (its result IS malformed), bounded by each consumer's existing re-poll / bounded-retry-then-pause / `--skip-children-check` path; the join cannot tell "rejected, retrying" from "rejected at the cap" and does not guess. Every emitter stays exit-0 fail-safe. Tests: `test-progress-state.sh` §42 (real-validator parity pinned to whatever shape the validator currently prints, so it is true on either side of v15.82.0; a stub validator as the mutation control proves `rejected` is derived from the validator's stdout; legacy-shape, non-JSON, and absent-validator omission cases; hooks.json same-matcher seam), `test-check-children-settled.sh` (rejected-then-accepted settles on the accepted row, rejected-only stays unsettled, `--all` arrays, doc seams, mutation control deleting the guard flips rejected-only to settled), `test-build-floor.sh` (zzz) two new lanes + mutation control. Docs: `RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` `## agent_lifecycle` (`rejected` / `stop_hook_active`), §"Completion authority join" (new output fields + the cap limit), FLOOR_PROJECTION lifecycle note; `HOOKS.md` progress-event row. Counts unchanged: 14 agents, 24 commands, 42 skills, 39 hooks (script + prose-only change; `hooks.json` byte-unchanged).
**v15.82.0 — Command-hook validators now actually block: `result_block_parser.emit()` (and `validate-launch-pad-result.py`'s own emit) print the documented Stop/SubagentStop decision shape — `{}` to allow, `{"decision": "block", "reason": "…"}` to block — instead of the prompt-hook `{"ok": …}` response schema, which a `type: command` hook prints to no effect:** probed 2026-09-21 on Claude Code v2.1.278 with a real `loomwright:worker` SubagentStop and a deliberately malformed WORKER_RESULT (`outputs_gap` omitted): the runtime recorded the plugin's `validate-worker-result.py` as `hook_success` with `{"ok": false, "reason": …}` on stdout, no decision, no error notice, and let the subagent stop — so every one of the six command validators had been a silent no-op since its v15.17.0 prompt→command conversion (the `ok` shape is what a `type: prompt` hook's *model* answers; it is not a command-hook output field). The control probe with `{"decision": "block", …}` recorded `hook_blocking_error`, fed the reason back as the subagent's next instruction, and re-fired SubagentStop with `stop_hook_active: true` on the retry. Both firings are committed, sanitized, as `scripts/fixtures/subagentstop-decision-shape-probe.json`. Exit code stays 0 on every path; the five shared-module validators' import-time fail-safe and `run_validator`'s crash path now print `{}` (pass), byte-identical in meaning to before. `test-result-validators.sh` §K pins the exact bytes for all six validators (mutation control: reverting `emit()` to the `ok` shape turns 140 assertions red), `test-classify-risk.sh` F1/F2 read the new shape, and the `autonomous-loop` skill's inline `--raw` consumer of the launch-pad validator now branches on `decision`. Docs: `HOOKS.md` §"Command-validator decision shape" (new), `CLAUDE.md` §"Plugin Hooks" (the two-dialect rule), `ARCHITECTURE_CONTRACTS.md` §"Hook Performance Rules", `AGENT_GUIDELINES.md` `|| true` convention note, `RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` VERIFY_RESULT validator paragraph. Honest limits, disclosed in `HOOKS.md`: (a) `emit-progress-event.sh` fires on the same SubagentStop without reading `stop_hook_active`, so a rejected-then-retried worker leaves an early `subtask_complete` row before its accepted one — a consumer-side follow-up (`check-children-settled.sh` keys on the FIRST terminal row), deliberately not bundled; (b) the retry loop is bounded only by the runtime's 8-consecutive-continuation cap. The three `type: prompt` hooks (`SubagentStop[loomwright:code-reviewer]`, `Stop`, `TaskCompleted`) correctly keep the `ok` response schema and are untouched. Counts unchanged: 14 agents, 24 commands, 42 skills, 39 hooks (script + fixture + prose-only change; `hooks.json` byte-unchanged).

**v15.81.0 — `worker_checkpoint`: an advisory trail a worker leaves at five named moments (hypothesis confirmed/refuted, blocker, transition, slice done), consumed by `/handoff` and The Floor, never gating anything:** new fail-safe emitter `scripts/checkpoint.sh` (ledger path positional and validated, per the `learning-emit-ledger-path-positional` convention) appends a `worker_checkpoint` JSONL event; `docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` documents the new event shape. Both physical worker spawn templates gain a session-log pointer (`agents/execute-manager.md` Step 3, `skills/async-orchestration/SKILL.md`'s Sequential-path Worker template, inherited by Single-Agent too) plus advisory-only checkpoint guidance in `agents/worker.md`. Two read-only consumers: `build-handoff.sh` renders checkpoints under a job's "Tried / rejected" facet with session-id provenance, and `build-floor.sh` additively carries the last checkpoint text per lane (rendering deferred to a later item); `build-state.sh` and `read-postmortem.sh` verified unaffected. `test-checkpoint.sh`, `test-build-handoff.sh`, and `test-build-floor.sh` cover the new surfaces, including checkpoint.sh's >200-char defensive truncation branch (added as a PR #233 review follow-up, along with the `prompt-token-budgets.json`/`ARCHITECTURE_CONTRACTS.md` re-measure record for worker's shrunk-but-not-breached token headroom). Honest limit: the source requirement's pre-registered emission-rate measurement over the first five real runs does not exist yet and is deliberately not fabricated here — deferred to real subsequent runs. Counts unchanged: 14 agents, 24 commands, 42 skills, 39 hooks (script + prose-only change, no new agent/command/skill/hook).

**v15.80.0 — Completion authority: a subtask is complete only when `provides` is present on disk AND a terminal lifecycle event was recorded for the producing agent, never the worker's self-report alone; FINALIZE refuses to merge while any spawned child is unsettled:** new fail-safe join script `scripts/check-children-settled.sh` (modelled on `verify-provides.sh`'s fail-SAFE-emitter / fail-CLOSED-consumer split) reads the v15.79.0 `agent_lifecycle` ledger and reports, per `agent_id`, whether a terminal row exists (`subtask_complete` / `token_ledger` / `agent_lifecycle: failed`) — `--agent-id <id>` for the per-subtask join, `--all` for the per-session aggregate. **Per-subtask (SECOND, independent condition alongside the existing `verify-provides.sh` disk gate — that gate is unchanged):** `agents/execute-manager.md`'s v12 outputs_verified gate and `agents/supervisor.md`'s Single-Agent Path (new step 3b) / Sequential Path now also require a terminal row for the worker's `agent_id` before marking a subtask complete; `provides` present on disk with no terminal row yet records the new decision string `provides_present_agent_unsettled` (distinct from the existing `provides_mismatch`, which is unrenamed and untouched) and is treated as an expected transient state — re-poll on the async Parallel path, bounded-retry-then-pause on the blocking Single-Agent/Sequential paths — never an error or escalation. **Per-run:** Phase 4 FINALIZE's pre-merge safety gate gains a 5th checklist point (`skills/async-orchestration/SKILL.md` step 1, mirrored in `agents/supervisor.md`'s "Gates (stay here)" summary): every `agent_identity` row this session wrote must have a matching terminal row, or the gate emits `error: "children_unsettled: {ids}"` and does not merge — interactively `AskUserQuestion`, and under `--non-interactive` fails CLOSED exactly like the existing `preflight_overlap_detected` gate. New `--skip-children-check` flag (documented in `commands/supervisor.md`'s Parameters table and `agents/supervisor.md`'s Flags table, mirroring `--skip-preflight-sync`'s style) is the one explicit escape hatch, recording `user_skipped_children_check` and `children_check: skipped` in the run summary. A session with zero `agent_identity` rows (pre-2026-09-07 logs, or no Task ever spawned) reports `children_check: no_identity_rows`, never a false `settled`. **Resume, don't respawn:** both consumers surface `ended_without_result` children (a terminal `subtask_complete` with `result_block_present: false`, or an `agent_lifecycle: failed` row) with their `agent_id` so an operator can `SendMessage` them to resume (memory `subagents-hit-turn-limit-resume-via-sendmessage`) instead of re-running cold — this release only surfaces the id, it does not implement the resume mechanics. `docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` gains a `## Completion authority join` section plus new `SUPERVISOR_RESULT.error` documentation for `children_unsettled`; `docs/FAILURE_ESCALATION.md` gains a "Children-unsettled FINALIZE gate" subsection under Supervisor Failure. `children_unsettled` is deliberately NOT added to the `/autonomous` AUTONOMOUS_RUN `status_reason` closed enum (out of scope for this change — falls into the existing `supervisor_failed_other` catch-all if it ever reaches the loop). Counts unchanged: 14 agents, 24 commands, 42 skills, 39 hooks (no new agent/command/skill/hook — this is a script + prose-only change).

**v15.79.0 — Agent lifecycle ledger: `waiting`/`working` (heartbeat)/`failed` rows, plus a `result_block_present` flag, so a reader can tell from the session JSONL alone whether an agent is waiting on a human, has gone quiet, or ended without a result:** new fail-safe emitter `scripts/emit-lifecycle.sh` (modelled line-for-line on `emit-progress-event.sh`/`emit-agent-identity.sh` — `set -u`, no `set -e`, `trap 'exit 0' EXIT`, the same worktree-safe anchoring + two-source session-id resolution + run-ownership gate) adds three `agent_lifecycle` JSONL states. `waiting` is re-fanned onto the EXISTING `PreToolUse[AskUserQuestion]` and `Notification` command hooks (`payload=$(cat); printf '%s' "$payload" | ... ; printf '%s' "$payload" | ...`) so `notify-desktop.sh`/`send-webhook.sh` keep receiving byte-identical stdin; `reason` is the hardcoded literal `ask_user` on the PreToolUse seam and read defensively (`.notification_type` then `.type`, else `unknown`) on the Notification seam — `agent_id`/`agent_scope` are carried from the payload only when present, never guessed, and the Notification seam's `agent_id` presence for a subagent is explicitly flagged UNVERIFIED (no live sample observed — see `.supervisor/requirements/orca-derived/01-agent-lifecycle-ledger.md` `## §0 Probe Results`). `heartbeat` (state `working`) is a new, independent, additional hook entry on each of the three EXISTING `PostToolUse` matchers (`Bash`, `Write|Edit`, `Task`) — no existing entry on any of them was touched — debounced PER DERIVED `agent_id` (never per matcher block) via one shared marker file, so an agent whose tool calls hit any mix of the three matchers still yields at most one `working` line per `LOOMWRIGHT_LIFECYCLE_HEARTBEAT_DEBOUNCE`-second window (default 60, non-integer override falls back to the default). `failed` is re-fanned onto the existing `StopFailure` hook, leaving the raw `failures.log` append byte-identical, and carries `reason` copied VERBATIM from the payload's own top-level `error` field (`unknown` when absent) plus `agent_scope: subagent` when `agent_id` is present (empirically confirmed on 17 of 37 real captures in this repo's own `failures.log`) else `agent_scope: main` — the one lifecycle state with a grounded main-thread fallback; the other two OMIT `agent_scope` instead of guessing. `emit-progress-event.sh` gains one additive field, `result_block_present`, on `subtask_complete` — reusing `result_block_parser.find_last_block`'s exact WORKER_RESULT-fence detection (the same one `validate-worker-result.py` runs, not a second regex); OMITTED ENTIRELY (never a guessed `false`) when `last_assistant_message` is absent from the payload or when the detection import fails. `docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` gains a new `## agent_lifecycle` JSONL event-record section (frozen example values, per the doc's existing convention) documenting all three states and the omit-when-absent rules; `derived` states `stalled`/`ended_without_result` are explicitly NOT written by any of this — a future reader (`build-floor.sh`/`build-state.sh`) derives them from the rows this release adds. New `scripts/test-emit-lifecycle.sh` covers empty stdin, malformed JSON, missing jq/python3, `waiting` with/without `agent_id`, `failed` across all 5 real `error` values plus the missing-key case, the heartbeat debounce bound, and the `result_block_present` mutation control; `test-progress-state.sh` gains a case for the new field. Counts: 14 agents, 24 commands, 42 skills, 39 hooks (36→39: one new hook entry each on `PostToolUse[Bash]`/`PostToolUse[Write|Edit]`/`PostToolUse[Task]`; `Notification`/`StopFailure`/`PreToolUse[AskUserQuestion]` were re-fanned in place, not counted as new entries).

**v15.78.0 — `/verify --folder` grows a multi-ticket queue engine, resumed by reconcile, that never shares state with `/automate`:** item 07 gives `/verify` the same operational shape as `/automate` — one queue file per folder run, `LC_ALL=C sort` order, `--limit N` capping PROCESSED items not Queue size — while keeping its OWN truth source: `evidence.jsonl` and `git rev-parse`, never `gh`. Four new `verify-helpers.sh` subcommands (`queue-write`, `queue-progress-append`, `queue-checkoff`, `queue-remaining`) are the ONLY writer of `.supervisor/verify/queue-*.md`; `queue-write` carries a LINE-COUNT GUARD `automate-helpers.sh runfile-write` does not — an empty or shorter rewrite is REFUSED (exit 1, file byte-unchanged) rather than silently truncating the queue's only copy of resume state (memory: `runfile-write-accepts-empty-stdin`). A new `verify-run.sh queue-reconcile-item <run_dir> --branch <name> --repo <dir>` (git/evidence access lives there, not in verify-helpers.sh, which deliberately has neither) reconciles ONE queue item on every start: `git rev-parse <branch>` vs the run's own `run_start.head_sha` is checked FIRST — a moved head marks the item `# stale: head moved <old>-><new>` and re-queues a fresh item, the stale run dir NEVER reused or deleted, no matter how the run stopped; otherwise `evidence.jsonl`'s last line decides — `run_end` is done even if unchecked, `pause` stays paused, anything else is a crash mid-AC resumed at `verify-helpers.sh first-unverdicted`'s answer. `--resume <run_id>` gains a probe-then-fallback DISPATCH RULE (not a new flag): a `.supervisor/verify/queue-<run_id>.md` match is a queue-mode resume, otherwise the existing single-ticket `.supervisor/verify/<run_id>/` resume is unchanged. `--folder <dir>` intake reuses `automate-helpers.sh resolve-folder` verbatim — no second folder-scanning implementation. `skills/verify-walkthrough/SKILL.md` §10 is the new protocol authority (commands/verify.md documents only the surface); `docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` gains `## VERIFY_QUEUE`, a markdown state-file contract mirroring `AUTOMATE_RUN`'s framing. New `scripts/test-verify-queue.sh` (37 assertions) covers folder resolution + done-skip, `--limit` pausing, crash-resume, stale-detection + re-queue, the empty-stdin guard, and a mutation control that proves deleting the head-sha comparison defeats stale detection. Never reads or writes `.supervisor/automate/`, `.supervisor/config.json`, or `.supervisor/state.md` — mechanically proven by an `-newer <marker>` mtime check in the new test suite.

**v15.77.0 — `/verify` pauses for a human sign-in instead of pretending to be anonymous, and resumes exactly where it left off:** item 03 shipped `/verify` resigned to an anonymous auth story — `agents/qa-executor.md`'s old step 5 called `verify-env.sh auth-probe` unconditionally (recording a spurious `auth: anonymous` line even for apps with no auth concept at all) and turned any AC needing a session into a permanent `BLOCKED --reason "auth-probe: anonymous"` with no path forward. `verify-run.sh auth-check <run_dir> --repo <dir>` (previous release) now decides the whole run BEFORE any spec is authored: `auth.method: none` is a true no-op (zero probe calls, zero evidence lines); an authenticated session appends one `{event:auth, state:authenticated}` line and the run proceeds; anything else appends `{event:auth, state:needs_auth}` and calls the new `verify-run.sh pause <run_dir> --reason <needs_auth|session_expired>` — the ONE place a `pause` evidence line is ever written — exiting `4`. `agents/qa-executor.md`'s VERIFY MODE now shells to `auth-check` in place of the old unconditional probe, removes the now-unreachable per-AC anonymous-BLOCKED fallback, and on exit `4` skips spec authoring/`walk`/`finish` entirely, runs `verify-env.sh stop` for cleanup, and emits `VERIFY_RESULT` `status: paused, pause_reason: needs_auth` (`docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` §VERIFY_RESULT: `status` gains the `paused` enum value, and a REQUIRED-KEY NULLABLE-VALUE `pause_reason` field pairs with it — non-null iff `status == paused`, `null`/absent otherwise, enforced by `validate-qa-result.py`'s new V7 rule). A session that dies MID-walk is caught from real evidence, never inferred: `verify-run.sh walk` now scans each ingested spec's attachments for a `response-40[13]` signal and, on a hit, forces the LOWEST-ordinal affected AC (parsed from its `[ACn]` title, never file-discovery order) to `BLOCKED`/`ENVIRONMENT_ISSUE`/`session_expired` — overriding whatever the spec itself reported — and every later AC to `BLOCKED`/`ENVIRONMENT_ISSUE`/`run_paused_session_expired`, appending exactly one `{event:auth, state:expired}` line and one `pause` line, then exiting `5` instead of its usual `0`/`3`; the agent skips `finish` and emits the same `paused` shape with `pause_reason: session_expired`. `commands/verify.md` — the only surface that can address a human, since the executor is a subagent with no `AskUserQuestion` — gains `/verify --resume <run_id>`: it resolves `.supervisor/verify/<run_id>` (erroring, never silently starting a fresh run, if absent), re-runs `auth-check`, prints the SAME sign-in instruction on another `needs_auth` (no new run dir, no `resume` line), and on success appends `{event:resume, reason:human_signed_in}` before re-spawning the SAME executor at the SAME run dir with no special resume flag — the executor detects the resume itself from a prior passing `env:{step:start}` line and derives its remaining work from the new `verify-helpers.sh first-unverdicted <run_dir>` (prints the first `ac_id` with no `ac` line yet, or nothing when the run is fully verdicted), so a resumed run only authors specs for what never got a verdict, never re-walking a PASS. On the first pause of a fresh run, `commands/verify.md` also warns once — naming the path — when the contract's `storage_state_path` is not `git`-ignored in the target repo (it holds session cookies); a `--resume` invocation's own report skips the check, since the file's mere existence already proves a human ran `codegen` once. The plugin never types, stores, or logs a credential at any point — it only ever reads the file a human's own `playwright codegen --save-storage=…` run wrote. `skills/verify-walkthrough/SKILL.md` (`1.0.0` → `1.1.0`) replaces its former forward-reference ("item 04 adds the pause-for-auth") with a new §8 documenting the full pause/resume semantics, and `scripts/check-contract-parity.sh`'s `agents/qa-executor.md|status|…` ENUMS row and VERIFY_RESULT MANIFEST row grow `paused` / `pause_reason` respectively. Advisory only, unchanged: a verify run still changes no `heal_decision`, blocks no PR, merges nothing. Counts: 14 agents, 24 commands, 42 skills, 36 hooks.

**v15.76.1 — the bot-review marker matches the review STEM, not one spelling of it: a `claude[bot]` comment that opens `Reviewed <sha>. … Two minor findings` is a finding the drain must see:** `scripts/classify-bot-review.sh` — the SINGLE source of truth for `bot_author_re` / `review_marker_re`, consumed by the `--until-mergeable` drain's issue-comment channel (`skills/review-heal/SKILL.md` §U1) and by `pr-postmortem-gather.sh`'s bot-round signal — gated bodies on the bare lexeme `\breview\b`. On 2026-09-14 the `claude[bot]` issue comment on PR #223 (issuecomment 5668703118) opened "Reviewed 1d35b3f…. This is a large, carefully engineered PR… Two minor findings, both low severity: 1. … 2. …" and never used the bare word, so the classifier returned it OUT and the drain silently dropped two real findings — the exact #64 class the issue-comment channel was added for (v14.32.0). The mechanism, not the spelling, is the defect: a marker pinned to one inflection of a word a reviewer conjugates freely ("Reviewed", "reviewing", "reviewer") is a false negative waiting for the next phrasing. `review_marker_re` is now `\b(review(s|ed|er|ers|ing)?|findings?)\b` — the stem plus the noun the same comment used — still case-insensitive and still word-bounded, so `Deploy Preview` / `previewed` can never match (no boundary inside "preview"), and the author gate is untouched (a human "Reviewed …" stays OUT). The false-positive posture is unchanged and deliberate: the drain's validate-then-fix step (§U3.5) dismisses an ungroundable candidate, so a looser marker costs one validation while a tighter one loses a finding; for `pr-postmortem` the bounded phantom-round over-count already documented for all-clear closers is the same accepted noise. `test-classify-bot-review.sh` grows 9 → 12: **(10)** the #223 body shape verbatim (opener + "Two minor findings" + numbered items, no bare "review" — the fixture self-checks `test("\\breview\\b")` is false so it can never degrade into a re-run of case 1) classifies IN with the original object preserved; **(11)** a mutation control rewrites ONLY the regex line of a copy back to the pre-fix `\breview\b`, gated on the mutant differing from the original AND carrying the exact old line (either gate failing reports `UNPROVEN`, a failure), and asserts the mutant drops the body while the original keeps it — verified both ways: against the pre-fix script the suite goes red on exactly (10) and (11 UNPROVEN); **(12)** `Deploy Preview`, `Previewed the build`, `Coverage: 92%` and a human `Reviewed abc123. Two findings` all stay OUT. The four prose mirrors of the marker (`pr-postmortem-gather.sh` header, `test-pr-postmortem-gather.sh` header, `docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` §PR_POSTMORTEM `review_rounds`, `skills/pr-postmortem/SKILL.md`) and the one `review-heal` sentence that cited "no word-bounded `review`" are synced; the drain protocol itself is unchanged (it never redefines the regexes — §"Re-implementing the bot regexes"). Honest limit: the marker is still lexical — a bot review that says neither a review-stem word nor "finding(s)" (e.g. "Looked at abc. Two issues:") is still OUT; the fix closes the inflection class, not every possible opener. Counts unchanged: 14 agents, 23 commands, 41 skills, 36 hooks.

**v15.76.0 — the drain's check-wait bound is now sized from the measured `ci` duration, and CI stops re-downloading Playwright on every run:** the `/review-pr --until-mergeable` drain's `--check-wait-timeout` default rises 600 s → **1200 s** (`review-heal` skill §"Wait-For-Settled-Checks" is the ONE place the number is justified; the skill's flag table and `commands/review-pr.md` mirror it; skill 1.6.0 → 1.7.0). The bound starts when a fix is pushed and must absorb queue latency plus the whole required `ci` job — measured here, PR #222's `ci` ran 574 s (26 s under the old bound) and PR #223's two runs 720 s / 740 s, so the SHA-bound confirming pass elapsed 726 s after the drain-cycle-1 push with `ci` still in flight and escalated a PR that went green two minutes later. 1200 s is ≥1.6× the slowest measured run and exceeds GitHub's ~15 min never-assigned-a-runner cancellation, so a queued-out check resolves inside the bound rather than as `UNREADABLE`; the honest cost is that a genuinely stuck scoped check now costs a round 20 min instead of 10, with `--max-rounds` still the outer ceiling. Separately, `test-verify-walkthrough.sh` installed `@playwright/test@1` — a floating major — plus a ~150 MB chromium build into a fresh `/tmp` on every run of the required `ci` check (~24 s, and under `CI=1` an install failure is a hard red, so every run carried a CDN dependency the suite's no-network lesson never budgeted for). The script now pins `PW_TEST_VERSION=1.63.0` (exact, on its own line) and reinstalls on a version MISMATCH rather than on mere directory absence, so a dev's `/tmp` cache or a stale CI cache can no longer freeze an old version silently; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` derives its `actions/cache` key from that one pin line through the new `scripts/read-playwright-pin.sh` — the ONE reader of the line, invoked by the workflow rather than duplicated as an inline `sed`, failing CLOSED with an empty stdout if it cannot read exactly one `x.y.z` — and the mutations are COMMITTED arms of the new `test-read-playwright-pin.sh` (a range, a quoted value, a trailing comment, a missing line, a duplicated line, a nonexistent file, the workflow-wiring assertion and a loosened-pattern mutation control), with the `(PWv)` arms in `test-verify-walkthrough.sh` driving `acquire_playwright` through its match / mismatch / absent branches against a stub `npm` (no download; the CI-order self-test loop auto-includes both). It caches `$LOOMWRIGHT_VERIFY_TEST_CACHE` (defined once at job level) before the self-test step, and deliberately carries no `restore-keys`. The cache's first hit was observed on this PR's second `ci` run (`Cache restored from key: verify-walkthrough-pw-Linux-1.63.0`, restore ~5 s; the `(PW)` step 12 s against 24 s uncached). Counts unchanged.

**v15.75.0 — /verify: walk a ticket through the running app:** `/verify <ticket-path> [--branch <name>] [--cheap]` (namespaced `/loomwright:verify` for headless `claude -p`) is the first surface that verifies a ticket against the RUNNING app rather than its diff — `## Executable Acceptance` is `cmd:` / `corpus-task:` only, `/qa-executor` crawls from discovery and forbids form submission, Phase 4.5 and CI review read the diff. The command is a thin inline shell over `verify-run.sh` (`acs` extracts the ticket's `## Acceptance Criteria` into `acs.json`; `preflight` reads the committed `.agent/verify.json`, proves non-prod in THIS run — no override, exit `1` fails CLOSED with only `run_start` + the failing `env` line written, exit `3` when the contract is absent prints the `propose-verify.sh --non-prod … --confirm` bootstrap line and creates nothing — then mints the run dir; `walk` runs one `[ACn]`-titled Playwright spec per AC under a per-run config and ingests the JSON reporter into `ac` evidence lines; `verdict` records `NOT_VERIFIABLE` / `BLOCKED` with a reason and refuses `PASS` outright — `pass_requires_observation` — so PASS comes only from observation, never faked; `finish` appends `run_end`) plus ONE Task spawn of the QA Executor in its new `--verify <run_dir>` mode, whose protocol lives once in the new `verify-walkthrough` skill (AC → spec derivation, the four verdicts PASS / FAIL / BLOCKED / NOT_VERIFIABLE, the V7 mutation carve-out, evidence-per-AC, budget — Read on demand at mode entry, deliberately not preloaded). The executor emits `VERIFY_RESULT` (`docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` §VERIFY_RESULT: schema_version, run_id, run_dir, status, counts, summary), validated by the same `SubagentStop` `validate-qa-result.py` that guards `QA_RESULT` — a block carrying both is judged as `QA_RESULT` (QA_RESULT wins when present), so the discovery-driven L1 path is byte-for-byte unchanged. Every verdict is one appended, validated `evidence.jsonl` line and `summary.md` is derived on each append (v15.74.0), so a total can be computed but never written. `test-verify-walkthrough.sh` covers the dispatch, the four verdict paths, the `PASS`-refusal, and the browser seam with a broken-form mutation control — the SAME specs re-run against a `--broken` fixture app must flip the AC to `FAIL`, gated on the broken `/submit` really differing from the healthy one (else `UNPROVEN` is reported as a failure) — and installs Playwright browsers fail-closed under `$CI` (missing binaries fail the suite there; locally the browser arms skip with a named reason). Advisory only: a verify run changes no `heal_decision`, blocks no PR, and merges nothing. Counts: 14 agents, 24 commands, 42 skills, 36 hooks.

**v15.74.0 — a `/verify` verdict is one appended, validated line, and the summary is derived from the lines on every append — so a total can be computed but never written:** `verify-helpers.sh` (`evidence-append` / `summary-build` / `run-id`) completes the evidence store whose line schema (`RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` §VERIFY_EVIDENCE) and exit-status validator (`validate-verify-evidence.py`) landed in the previous commit. The store exists because the QA lane's accounting layer failed in exactly the way this shape forbids (memory `qa-l1-validated-on-sports-management`): an agent-written `coverage.json` reported a `failed` scope as `completed`, and the roll-up was overwritten per scope. `evidence-append <run_dir> <json|->` runs the validator, captures its status in the statement AFTER the substitution (a `local x="$(…)"` would swallow it — that is how an invalid fact would reach the store), and on exit 0 performs exactly ONE `printf … >>` (`O_APPEND`, no read-modify-write window) then regenerates `summary.md`; on any other status it wraps the raw input with `jq --arg` into `rejected.jsonl` (`{"rejected_at","reason","line"}` — the validator's own code such as `non_pass_without_reason`, `validator_unavailable` when `python3` is absent, `validator_error:<rc>` otherwise), exits 1, and leaves `evidence.jsonl` byte-identical. `summary-build` is the ONLY reader: `jq -s` over the lines, latest line per `ac_id` (and per env step) by input order, the counts row `PASS: n · FAIL: n · BLOCKED: n · NOT_VERIFIABLE: n · total: n` computed over that set, a header that says `DERIVED … do not edit`, and a `derived_from: <N> lines, sha256 <hash>` trailer (`shasum -a 256`, `sha256sum` fallback; an absent or empty store hashes empty input, never skips), written atomically via temp + `mv`. `run-id <slug>` mints `verify-<YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ>-<slug>` with the slug lower-cased and every non-alphanumeric run collapsed to one dash. `test-verify-evidence.sh` grows from 147 to 254 cases with three mutation controls, each gated on "the mutant differs from the original" and reporting `UNPROVEN` as a failure otherwise: (a) a hand-edited counts row claiming `PASS: 2 · FAIL: 0` over a 1-PASS/1-FAIL store is overwritten by both a bare `summary-build` and the next append; (b) deleting the FAIL line from a 5-AC store moves the row to `FAIL: 0 · total: 4`; (c) an `awk`-extracted `evidence_append()` body names `evidence.jsonl` on exactly one line, that line carries `>>`, and a copy with a `cat` inserted after the `mkdir -p` makes the no-read grep fail. Also covered: `run_end` with `counts` or nested `meta.totals` refused, the stdin (`-`) round-trip, `run_dir`/`artifacts/` auto-creation, the empty-store trailer, trailer `N`/hash equality with `wc -l`/`shasum` and a hash change on one more line, latest-per-`ac_id` (PASS then FAIL for `AC1` → `PASS: 0 · FAIL: 1 · total: 1`), and a stub PATH without `python3` refusing a VALID line. The helper locates the validator as a sibling (`dirname "$0"`) and carries no vendor token (the root `check-vendor-coupling.sh` ratchet, not the suite, owns that claim — spelling the tokens in the suite tripped it), so no allowance changes. Counts unchanged: 14 agents, 23 commands, 41 skills, 36 hooks.

**v15.73.1 — `/propose --domain` confirmed the whole capability catalogue from a second copy of itself: the inventory now excludes nested worktrees and the searcher's own artefacts by NAME, not by the absolute path of one copy:** `propose-domain.sh` excluded its self-test and fixture tree by `$SCRIPT_DIR`-absolute path, which held until the plugin's own repo grew per-session linked worktrees under `<harness-dot-dir>/worktrees/<name>/` — each carries another `loomwright/scripts/propose-domain.sh` + `fixtures/propose-domain/` at a different absolute path, the `find` walk scanned them as project code, and every catalogue capability was reported "already confirmed" (`0 candidate(s) written`; `test-propose-domain.sh` AC15 red from the main checkout, green from a fresh worktree — measured 2026-09-14). A false `present` is the worst outcome this basis has: the candidate is never written, so nothing reports the gap. Three layers now, each shown live on its own by the self-test: (1) **enumeration** — inside a git work tree the surface is `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard` (what CI sees is what is searched; a gitignored tree or nested repository never enters), with the filesystem walk kept only for a non-git root; (2) **worktree paths** — anything under a linked worktree registered on the repo (`git worktree list --porcelain`, gathered unconditionally — the first draft gated it on git mode, leaving the walk with an empty list, which the new forced-walk control caught) or under any `<dot-dir>/worktrees/` segment is dropped; (3) **own artefacts by basename** — `propose-domain.sh`, `test-propose-domain.sh` and any `fixtures/propose-domain/` segment wherever they sit, plus the same three derived from `$0`. Segment rules run on the path relative to the root, so a project that itself lives under a `vendor/` or `<dot-dir>/worktrees/` ancestor keeps its whole surface. The emitted "where this looked" section names the enumeration source and the worktree exclusion. **AC22** plants a vendored copy, a plain `<dot-dir>/worktrees/x/` directory and a real `git worktree add` inside a throwaway project and mutates each layer separately (by-name deleted → the vendored copy confirms 8; segment deleted → the plain directory confirms `single-sign-on`; walk forced + prefix rule deleted → the linked worktree confirms `two-factor-authentication`; walk forced alone, and git-only with both path rules deleted, both stay clean). AC15's mutant follows the new line shapes and asserts it deleted exactly three. The segment rule is written for any dot-directory rather than one harness's name, so the core stays vendor-neutral under `check-vendor-coupling.sh` without an allowance change. Suite: 159 → 173.

**v15.73.0 — a project declares its verification environment once, in a committed `.agent/verify.json`, and nothing in the plugin can run it against production:** the coming `/verify <ticket>` lane needs to start, health-check, sign in to, seed and reset an app under test, and until now every one of those was a per-run guess with nothing asserting the target was not production. This release ships the contract and its three surfaces. `docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` §VERIFY_ENV is the schema authority (a state-file schema like §PRODUCT_CONTEXT, named in the `schema_version` exception note): `start`/`seed`/`reset`/`stop` are required keys whose value may legitimately be `null` — presence is asserted with jq `has()`, never a `//` default, because the missing-key and null cases are two different facts. `scripts/read-verify.sh` is the fail-safe reader (always exit 0; one compact JSON object line on stdout when the store is valid, NOTHING on stdout otherwise, with a grep-able bracketed reason token on stderr — `[store_absent]`, `[required_key_missing:seed]`, `[non_prod_assert_empty]` — and, load-bearing, it never executes a contract string). `scripts/propose-verify.sh` is the SOLE writer of the store: propose-only, `--confirm`-gated, one atomic `mv`, and it refuses to write without an explicit `--non-prod <regex|env=NAME=VAL|cmd=…>` on every path because no signal in a repo says what production looks like — `non_prod_assert` is never guessed, and `base_url` is never defaulted. `scripts/verify-env.sh` is the ONLY surface that runs the contract's shell strings and it fails CLOSED: it loads the contract through the reader (never re-parsing the file, forwarding the reader's reason token verbatim — `assert-non-prod` against `{"non_prod_assert":{}}` reports `non_prod_assert_empty`); every subcommand (`start`, `stop`, `seed`, `reset`, `auth-probe`) runs `assert-non-prod` FIRST in the same process and refuses with `non_prod_assert_failed` before any `bash -c`, nothing is persisted between invocations, and the two `bash -c` sites re-check the in-process flag themselves (`non_prod_not_asserted`); `start` polls `health` for 2xx until `ready_timeout_s` (default 60, `--ready-timeout-s` overrides) and on timeout runs `stop` and exits with `health_timeout`; the reader is located as a sibling script so the executor stays a vendor-neutral core script. `scripts/test-verify-seam.sh` covers all of it (reader cases, bootstrap cases, sole-writer grep, executor arms with a `python3 -m http.server` fixture on a free port and a PATH-stubbed `curl`) and carries two mutation controls: deleting the reader's `has("seed")` check from a copy makes the missing-`seed` case pass for the wrong reason, and deleting the executor's dispatch-level gate call from a copy proves the inner check still refuses. Honest limits: `non_prod_assert` proves only what its members test — a regex on `base_url` says nothing about where a DNS name resolves, `env_var_equals` reads the executor's own environment, and `cmd` is trusted shell; the shell-string members run with the caller's full privileges, trusted exactly like `package.json` scripts; `auth-probe` classifies by status code only (2xx authenticated, 401/403/302 anonymous, anything else refused); and whether an unattended lane may invoke the executor at all is that lane's decision, not something this contract grants. `AGENT_GUIDELINES.md` §"Sole-writer confirm gates" gains the eighth writer row. Counts unchanged: 14 agents, 23 commands, 41 skills, 36 hooks.

**v15.72.0 — a high-risk diff can never be auto-merged: `scripts/classify-risk.sh` is the ONE implementation of the high-risk heuristic and the sixth fail-CLOSED condition of the `/automate` trusted-merge gate, with NO override:** `gate-eval` was a pure function of five per-PR booleans and path-blind — a diff touching auth, secrets, payments or a migration merged under exactly the same conditions as a lint fix, while the heuristic that knew better lived only as prompt pseudo-code in the Phase 4.5 red-team lens and decided nothing but whether to spawn an advisory pass. Now `classify-risk.sh <base> <head> [--root <dir>]` (read-only, `git -C`, fail-SAFE, always exit 0) applies that heuristic verbatim — path/content patterns, workflow/orchestration surfaces, size — and emits `{high_risk: true|false|null, reasons: [...], changed_files, changed_lines}`; `--kind-table` prints the rules and `docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` carries the one committed copy, byte-compared by `test-classify-risk.sh`. `automate-helpers.sh gate-eval` condition 6 reads `high_risk` in the same `has()` + `type == "boolean"` shape as condition 3 (which gains the same guard — a `tostring` read had let the JSON string `"false"` merge on both conditions), so only the JSON boolean `false` merges: `true`, `null` (unclassifiable), a missing key or any string — including `"false"` — all `PARK: high_risk_diff (<first 3 reasons>)`; the script itself runs under `set -f` so its glob-shaped heuristic words never pathname-expand against the caller's cwd (an untracked `oauth2-proxy.yml` or a top-level `payments/` had silently narrowed rule (a) to the filename — fail-OPEN); the loop re-runs the script on the SHA it is judging at GATE time rather than trusting the Supervisor's earlier value, and there is no override of any kind — `--trust-unprotected` is condition 4 only (now stated on every surface that documents it), there is no config key, and the committed `.agent/risk.json` extension (`{"schema_version":1,"paths":[..],"content":[..]}`) is ADD-only: a malformed file falls back to the generic heuristic with a stderr note, and an `exclude` key is ignored, never honoured (owner decision R5). Phase 4.5 now classifies UNCONDITIONALLY before the red-team enable guard (D1) and carries the result as the additive optional `SUPERVISOR_RESULT.risk_classification` object; only the spawn stays opt-in, and `null` spawns (the safe direction for an advisory lens). Honest limits: the match is pattern-based — a comment containing `token` trips it, and in an agent-orchestration repo nearly every PR touches `agents/`/`commands/`/`skills/`, so nearly every PR parks by design; `null` parks; nothing changes for `/review-pr` or Phase 4.5, which never merge. Deliberately NOT added: the flat `risk_high`/`risk_reasons_count` fields on the `session_end` JSONL line — `build-insights.sh` consumes neither, and a documented flat field with no reader is a claim no check backs. The `gh pr merge --squash` invariant grep still resolves to the same five surfaces (condition 6 lives inside the one executor). Counts unchanged: 14 agents, 23 commands, 41 skills, 36 hooks.

**v15.71.0 — the per-subtask gate now reads the tree, not the worker's word: `scripts/verify-provides.sh` is the ONE implementation of the `file`/`symbol`/`type` `provides:` checks, and every consumer re-runs it on disk:** the `outputs_verified` gate was the worker's own claim — `agents/worker.md` Step 5.5 ran `test -f` / `grep -nE` and emitted the fields, and the consumers (`agents/execute-manager.md` §"v12 outputs_verified gate", `agents/supervisor.md` Single-Agent step 3 and the Sequential gate) parsed that block and never re-ran anything; the checkpoint even recorded `check_run: "worker self-verification (Step 5.5)"`. A worker that died before emitting `WORKER_RESULT` therefore left the gate with no input and nothing flagged it, and a worker that mis-reported `present` was believed (owner decisions R1 no new gate / R3 deterministic script, disk authoritative). Now `verify-provides.sh <brief> <id> [--root <dir>]` prints ONE `jq --arg`-built JSON object — `outputs_verified[]` with a `check_run` command + exit-code string per item, `outputs_gap` byte-identical to the worker's format, `source` — always exits 0, never writes, never `cd`s, never executes brief content, escapes ERE specials so `Foo$Bar` matches literally and `FooXBar` does not (portable ERE, no `\s`/`\b`), and refuses an empty pattern rather than letting `grep ''` mint a false `present`. Its parser accepts the layouts real briefs actually use (case-insensitive contracts heading at any depth, fenced or raw YAML, `# Subtask N` comment / `### Subtask N` heading / `subtask_N:` key anchors, trailing `# comments`, `provides: []`). The Execute Manager poll loop runs it once per completed subtask (+1 Bash) against the worktree and disk wins: a missing item reaches the existing adjudication `EXECUTE_CHECKPOINT` regardless of the worker's `status`, a worker/disk disagreement is recorded as `provides_mismatch` (a `/dreaming` signal, not a second gate), and an absent `WORKER_RESULT` with N/N present records `worker_result_absent` and proceeds to tests/lint instead of silently passing; both Supervisor inline gates do the same with `--root .`; the worker's own Step 5.5 now calls the same script (its 3-row table is gone; the file stays within its declared budget per `check-token-budget.sh`). **Decision D1** (surfaced for owner review): `brief_unreadable` / `subtask_not_found` / `jq_missing` / `bad_args` (a malformed invocation — dangling or empty `--root` — the arg parser's own early exit, which used to print nothing) checkpoint on EVERY path; `no_contracts` checkpoints on the `job:` path unless the brief's `## Environment` declares `legacy_brief: true` — Plan Reviewer Criterion 12's own opt-out, reused — and on legacy / `/supervisor task:` no-brief runs the consumer records `provides_unverifiable` and falls through to today's self-report gate, so no run that passes today acquires a new stop. `--kind-table` is the single source of the three check rows, committed once in `docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` between `<!-- kind-table:begin -->`/`end` markers and byte-compared by `test-verify-provides.sh` (108 assertions incl. five independent mutation controls — one deletes the script call from a copy of the poll-loop gate, one deletes the Step 1 carve-out clause, two restate a GNU-ism kind-table row inside a copy of Step 2b / of the skill's gate section, one restores the bare `exit 0` in a copy of the script's `--root` branch — and each requires its seam assertion to fail); `agents/orchestrator.md`, `skills/async-orchestration/SKILL.md` (its §"Pre-Spawn Verification Gate" restated the same drifted GNU-ism table — replaced by the same `--kind-table` pointer, worded without `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` to stay inside the file's vendor-coupling allowance; the Execute Manager, which preloads it, stays within budget) and `docs/FAILURE_ESCALATION.md` no longer describe the self-report as the gate input. **Honest limits:** `symbol` still matches ANY line containing the name — a comment or a prose mention passes — and nothing is semantic; Step 2b's pre-spawn `requires` walk is unchanged (one check per `requires` entry against the worktree), but its restated 3-row command table — which had drifted to GNU-only `\s`/`\b` — is replaced by a pointer to `--kind-table`; nothing runs in the detached drain or in Phase 4.5 (R4); and this PR's own Phase 3 gate ran on the installed v15.69.0 prompts, so the disk re-check is first observable on the NEXT job after reinstall. Counts unchanged: 14 agents, 23 commands, 41 skills, 36 hooks.

**v15.70.0 — the ground-truth runner verified whichever repo its own file happened to live in, so a marketplace install failed every dogfood check that the dev checkout passed:** `run-ground-truth.sh` resolves `corpus-task:<id>` to `$SCRIPT_DIR/eval-corpus/<id>/check.sh` and runs it after `cd <task-dir>`; every maintainer-side `check.sh` (`doc-currency-green`, `version-consistent`, `eval-selftest-green`, `parity-emit-block`, `emit-block-parses`, `review-churn-canary --live`) then did `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` **from the task dir** — i.e. from wherever the plugin is installed. On a marketplace install that is `~/.claude/plugins/cache/atelier/loomwright/<version>/scripts/`, which is not inside any git repo, so each task failed with "not inside a git repo" and Phase 4.5 `ground_truth` reported `advisory_failures` (2/4 on PR #217) while the checkout's copy of the identical runner passed 4/4 (observed 2026-09-13). Root cause: the check derived *what to verify* from *where it was*, and only the runner knows the caller's project.
