Fix (`loomwright/scripts/run-ground-truth.sh`, `run-eval.sh`, the six `eval-corpus/*/check.sh`): both runners now resolve ONE **project root** — `--project <dir>` (new flag on both) → the git toplevel of the caller's CWD → the caller's CWD — and export it to every check as **`EVAL_PROJECT_ROOT`** (the corpus contract, `eval-corpus/README.md` §"Project root"); a check resolves its repo as `--root` (where supported) → `$EVAL_PROJECT_ROOT` → the task-dir git root (direct-invocation fallback only), and a maintainer-side task whose resolved root lacks the gate script now fails with an explicit "maintainer-side task; the project root must be the loomwright repo" instead of a misleading "not inside a git repo". The same root drives the `.supervisor/twin/ground-truth.json` fallback, `cmd:` checks (previously the caller's bare CWD — identical in every real call path since Phase 4.5 pins repo-root CWD; now also identical under `--project`), `run-eval.sh`'s default `results.jsonl` location (still skipped when there is neither a git root nor an explicit `--project`, so a non-git CWD is never littered), and the contextual `commit` field (now the PROJECT's HEAD, not the runner's — on a marketplace install those differ). A `--project` naming a non-directory is the fail-safe `unverified` 0/0 shape on both runners, never a run against some other directory. Tests: `test-run-ground-truth.sh` 14→19 — new case (m) copies runner + corpus into a git-less `mktemp -d` (the plugin-cache shape) and asserts (m1) a corpus task run from the repo-root CWD passes with `commit` == the project's HEAD, (m2) `--project <repo>` from a non-git CWD passes and a repo-root-relative `cmd:` runs from the project root, (m3) the in-repo runner from a non-git CWD with no `--project` FAILS the maintainer-side task (the check must verify the caller's project, never fall back to the repo enclosing its own file — the mutation control for the check.sh precedence), (m4) invalid `--project` ⇒ `unverified`, (m5) check.sh precedence `--root` > `EVAL_PROJECT_ROOT` > task-dir git root; cases (f)/(l) now run from the repo root (the project they verify). `test-run-eval.sh` 9→14 — case 5d proves an explicit `--project <non-git dir>` records to `<project>/.supervisor/eval/results.jsonl` by default while a non-git CWD with no `--project` records nothing (control); case 6 proves `EVAL_PROJECT_ROOT` reaches `check.sh` as the CWD's git toplevel (a hermetic fixture repo, entered from a subdir), as `--project <dir>`, and that an invalid `--project` is `unverified` with the check never run. All ten new assertions were run against the pre-fix scripts and fail there. No schema change (`GROUND_TRUTH_JSON` / `EVAL_RESULT` fields unchanged); the Phase 4.5 invocation in `skills/self-heal-advisory/SKILL.md` is unchanged (its pinned repo-root CWD is exactly what the runner now derives the project from — a comment there says so); `RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` §"`## Executable Acceptance`" and the five affected `spec.md` files restate the contract. Counts unchanged.

**v15.69.0 — the only PR-gating review lens now sees what was asked for: the brief's acceptance criteria reach the Phase 4.5 Code Reviewer as a bounded BRIEF-CONFORMANCE line, and an unaddressed criterion becomes an ordinary `new` HIGH finding the existing fix loop already consumes:** the Supervisor Phase 4.5 reviewer spawn prompt (`skills/self-heal-advisory/SKILL.md` Part 2 §"Review-and-fix loop") carried BASE_BRANCH, the diff target, PRIOR-CHURN and HOUSE-RULES — no brief path, no criteria — so a diff that cleanly implemented the wrong or partial thing landed as `heal_decision: PASS` with a low `rubric_score` nobody acts on (surfaced by an external review 2026-09-13; owner decisions R1–R4). The fix is a THIRD pre-review enrichment shaped exactly like the two before it: Part 1 gains `## Brief-conformance advisory (acceptance-criteria enrichment)` (same HARD ADVISORY CONTRACT blockquote; source = the in-progress brief at `brief_path`, the path `run-ground-truth.sh --brief` and `parse_rubric` already read; parse rule = every leading-`-` bullet under `## Acceptance Criteria` with a `[ ]`/`[x]` prefix stripped, plus `## Outcomes Rubric` bullets when present, each section read up to the next `## ` heading; the cap, criteria first then rubric, and the `(+N bullets omitted — see brief)` marker where N counts every omitted bullet — criteria and rubric alike, worded "bullets" because rubric bullets are dropped first, so the common truncation omits no criterion at all — are stated ONCE there per the one-authoritative-place house rule — step 1f, the prompt line and the seam test say "the cap in Part 1"; three explicit ordered skip conditions — no `brief_path` on a direct `/supervisor task:`, no `## Acceptance Criteria` section, zero bullets — each yielding an empty `brief_conformance` silently, the line omitted, no error, the same back-compat case the completion tail handles for a job file absent from `in-progress/`); Part 2 gains on-entry step `1f.` after `1e.` with its `record_decision(... "brief_conformance: {non-empty | empty}" ...)`, and the **BRIEF-CONFORMANCE ADVISORY** prompt line directly after HOUSE-RULES ADVISORY, included only when non-empty: for each listed criterion the reviewer decides `addressed | not_addressed | cannot_determine` from the diff text, emits exactly one `category: new` / HIGH finding per `not_addressed` criterion quoting it verbatim in `description` with what is missing in `suggestion`, puts `cannot_determine` (runtime-only criteria) in one summary line rather than a finding, and treats rubric bullets as orientation only — they never produce findings and stay the Rubric Grader's lane (R1). The line says explicitly what makes it different from the two advisories above it (R2): it DOES yield ordinary gating findings, because a `new` HIGH finding is already the existing gate's input — `heal_decision` still derives ONLY from `CODE_REVIEW_RESULT`, no new gate, no new schema field, rubric / ground_truth / contract_conformance stay advisory. The fix prompt was verified, not restructured: its `category=new + severity>=HIGH` selection and step 5 already admit a conformance finding; step 1a gains one sentence — the class of a conformance finding is the criterion itself. `brief_conformance` is a named miss-class in the DIFFERENT-LENS DIRECTIVE parenthetical, the Self-Heal Miss-Class Checklist (`skills/quality-checklist/SKILL.md`, class signal: a stated acceptance criterion with no corresponding change in the integrated diff), and `agents/code-reviewer.md` (new Review Process step 5a describing the three-way verdict and the HIGH/`new` mapping without copying the skill's parse rules, plus the restated Self-heal-lens enumeration — the plan named that inline list as the exact restated-list drift AC7 would otherwise land as). New `loomwright/scripts/test-brief-conformance-seam.sh` (24 assertions, `ok`/`no` defined, `RESULT:` tail, `$BASH_SOURCE` paths, bash-3.2/BSD-safe; auto-included by ci.yml's `test-*.sh` glob): the prompt line sits directly after the `**HOUSE-RULES ADVISORY (non-gating` prompt-line literal (anchored on that literal, not the bare token Part 1 prose also carries), is gated on non-empty, carries the verdict and the mapping; the cap phrase appears exactly once, inside the Part 1 section, and step 1f refers to it without restating it and names all three skip conditions; the two mirrors name `not_addressed` / `brief_conformance`; and a mutation control deletes the prompt line from a copy, gates the mutant on non-empty + differs-from-original, and asserts the adjacency assertion goes red against it (a first draft's `awk -v` regex was escape-mangled and failed on BOTH the original and the mutant — the real-run red is what exposed it; the matcher is now literal `index()`; the external control — the line deleted from the real skill — turns 9 assertions red, restored and re-verified green). The Phase 4.5 heal iteration on this PR itself added two prompt-contract pins the first cut lacked: the over-cap clause now carries the brief pointer (`brief: {brief_path}`, pointer-not-payload, so criteria past the cap are reachable), and a conformance finding's `file:` is the criterion's evident target path or the sentinel `brief` (parallel to `file: environment`; the SubagentStop validator requires a non-empty `file`, so an unaddressed criterion with no evident target never emits an empty one). Budgets: `check-token-budget.sh` green with no raise (code-reviewer 2133 headroom, orchestrator 665 after the checklist bullet). **Limits:** the reviewer decides `addressed` from the diff text only — it executes nothing, so a runtime-only criterion is reported under `cannot_determine`, never verified; rubric bullets are orientation only; the reviewer of THIS PR runs on the old prompt text — the line first takes effect on the next job's Phase 4.5 run, so AC1's behaviour is not observable here; the standalone `/review-pr` / `review-heal` drain is untouched (R4 — a bare PR URL has no brief). Counts unchanged.

**v15.68.0 — retention without amnesia: every `.supervisor/` directory gets a recorded verdict, and only what is classified exhaust is ever swept — report-only by default:** nothing pruned `.supervisor/` (measured on the primary checkout 2026-09-12: 21 directories + 4 top-level files, `logs/` 145 files / 7.8 M, `drain-rounds/` 737 / 2.9 M), and the naive fix is worse than the growth, because three things there look like garbage and are not — the per-PR `review-dispatch/` and `postmortem-dispatch/` markers are idempotency guards durable by design; the session logs are the input `curation-status.sh` counts, so an age sweep would lower the "your knowledge is stale" number in the direction of "everything is fine" (61 of 106 logs were >30 days old and 30 of them still pending for `/dreaming`); and the five tracked files under `memory/` and `postmortem/` are the Twin's judgment. So the deliverable is a CLASSIFICATION — the new `ARCHITECTURE_CONTRACTS.md` §"`.supervisor/` retention policy": one row per directory (25 rows: the 21 directories on disk + the three plugin-defined queues absent there today — `agent-memory-proposals/`, `orientation-proposals/`, `capability/` — + the top-level entries; the population is every `.supervisor/<name>` the plugin writes or reads, not what one checkout happens to hold), each *tracked* / *guard* / *consumed* / *exhaust* with its consumers and its rule, of which exactly two carry an exhaust rule (`drain-rounds/*.json`; `logs/` partially — `*.jsonl` whose id is absent from the pending set, plus the unread `pr-postmortem-dispatch-*.log` transcripts; `review-pr-dispatch-*.log` is CONSUMED, `build-insights.sh` reads it as the durable opt-out evidence of its missing-drain reconciliation) — and a tool downstream of it, `loomwright/scripts/retention-sweep.sh` (`bash scripts/retention-sweep.sh [--project-root D] [--older-than N] [--delete] | policy`; fail-SAFE, always exit 0): REPORT by default, `--delete` to act, `--older-than` default 90 via `find -mtime +N` (no stat flavor; `0`/`08`/`abc`/missing/above 36500 — where `find` would silently yield no candidates — ⇒ a one-line reason and REPORT), the IRREVERSIBLE line printed FIRST in both modes, a `policy` machine surface held equal to the doc table in both directions by the suite. Guards are layered and all decided before the first `rm`: the row verdict (memory/ and postmortem/ are never even listed), `git ls-files --error-unmatch` returning EXACTLY rc 1 (rc 0 ⇒ tracked, kept; any other rc — 128 for a non-git `--project-root` — ⇒ "git could not answer" and `--delete` degrades to REPORT, never read as "nothing is tracked"), `jq` present, the `ls-files` answer and `-f`/not-symlink re-checked at deletion time (the glob is decided once, at listing time; tab/newline names are refused at listing time with `keep (unsafe name, not swept)`) (a file that becomes tracked between the listing and the removal is kept — `keep (tracked at deletion time)` — asserted with a `git` shim that stages the candidate on its first probe), an unreadable dir or failed rm reported per item and never widened, and an on-disk directory with no row reported `unclassified — not swept` (the two promotion queues `/dreaming` drains — `agent-memory-proposals/`, `orientation-proposals/` — are classified consumed, so the fail-closed line is reserved for a directory the plugin does not know). **Consumer honesty is preserving the input, not re-basing the counter:** new `curation-status.sh pending-ids` prints the union of the two pending sets from the SAME predicates the counters use — `/dreaming`'s set complement ∪ `/insights`' strict-newer-than-dashboard watermark (`never` ⇒ every session_end log; an `is_uint`-failed mtime is INCLUDED) — failing CLOSED toward keep (every id + one stderr line) whenever either counter would be `unknown`; the sweep resolves it as a sibling and runs it FROM THE TARGET ROOT, so `--project-root` from another checkout's cwd cannot compute the keep-set from the wrong corpus (asserted: a second, differently-populated fixture as cwd), and `status --json`'s `dreaming.pending` / `insights.pending` are asserted identical before and after against that script directly. **The 737-file `drain-rounds/` was test pollution, and the leak is closed at its source:** 732 ledgers were `{"rounds":0,…}` with `max_rounds` split evenly across `0/5/10/100` — the parameter set of `test-drain-rounds.sh` §H, which called `init` without the suite's cwd-isolated `run` helper while `drain-rounds.sh` writes relative to cwd; §H now runs through `run "$ROOT/h"`, and an end-of-suite LEAK PIN (`ORIG_PWD` + `PRE_EXISTS` + a `.started` marker, two arms because the script `mkdir -p`s its dir: absent-at-start must stay absent; present-at-start must gain no file `-newer` than the marker) goes red on recurrence — both arms verified red against the reverted §H. `test-retention-sweep.sh` (133 assertions, every fixture under `mktemp -d`, every invocation with an explicit `--project-root`, refuses to start when its own `mktemp -d` root is inside a git repository) covers AC-1…AC-11 with the five tracked files committed THEN aged (a just-committed file would be skipped by `-mtime +1` even with every guard removed), transcript prefixes DERIVED from the producers' `RUN_LOG=` lines, AC-7's five fail-safe arms (unreadable dir ×2, garbage `curation-state.json`, `PATH` without jq, without git, non-git root rc 128), AC-8's positive hooks.json pin, and five gated single-edit mutants asserted RED — (a1) memory/+postmortem/ reclassified exhaust deletes their aged UNTRACKED files while the tracked five still survive (guard ii is independent of the row), (a2) BOTH `ls-files` guard lines removed — listing-time and deletion-time — deletes a tracked file inside `drain-rounds/`, while removing only the listing-time line still keeps it and names `keep (tracked at deletion time)`, and removing only the deletion-time line lets the becomes-tracked file fall; the same shim answering rc 128 at deletion time keeps that one file with the rc named while its siblings are still removed, a mutant deleting that arm goes red, and a candidate renamed away or replaced by a symlink between the passes is `skipped (not a regular file now)` with the moved file and the link target untouched, and a candidate whose basename holds a TAB is refused at listing time (`keep (unsafe name, not swept)`) because the candidate row is tab-separated — the gated mutant removing that guard deletes an UNLISTED same-prefix sibling, the exact damage the guard exists to prevent, (a1+a2) the rows reclassified and both lines removed is the only way the five tracked hashes fall (the layering claim itself — a deviation from the brief's a1 ⇒ AC-1, which the independent guard makes impossible for one edit), (b) the guard rows reclassified lose their markers, (c) the pending-ids exclusion line removed deletes an aged unconsumed log AND moves the pending pair; `test-curation-status.sh` gains group (s) for `pending-ids`. `PITFALLS.md` gains "Two `.supervisor/` artifacts that look deletable and are not". `OBSERVABILITY.md`, `TELEMETRY.md`, `commands/insights.md`, `commands/dreaming.md` were read for a corpus-completeness claim and carry none (TELEMETRY's `subtask_complete` count is a bounded since-reinstall recipe; insights "reads every `*.jsonl`" reads what exists) — untouched. Not wired into any hook; not a command. **Limits:** the sweep is irreversible and says so; `consumed.logs` keeps deleted ids as inert members and whole-corpus totals (`/insights`, `build-loop-evidence.sh`) shrink to the retained window; a `review-dispatch/` PR-closed rule and `salvage/` retention are separate decisions. Counts unchanged.

**v15.67.0 — the removal that discards without asking now salvages first, mechanically:** `loomwright/scripts/worktree-salvage.sh` (new, fail-SAFE, always exit 0) captures a worktree's uncommitted content — `BASE_SHA`, `tracked.patch` (`git diff --binary HEAD`, so staged additions and binary edits round-trip), `modified/<rel>` verbatim, `untracked/<rel>` verbatim, and a tool-written `README.md` that carries the base-SHA caveat (the patch is against that worktree's own HEAD and will NOT apply to current `main`) — into `<dest>/.supervisor/salvage/<basename>-<ts>[-N]/` — where `<dest>` is exact per caller: the true primary checkout (the parent of `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`) for FINALIZE and any hand-run without `--dest`; the DISPATCHING checkout's `.supervisor/` (beside the per-PR marker, passed explicitly as `--dest`) for the drain's three sites — the exact shape of the two rescues that had already been done by hand. Detection is `git status --porcelain=v1 -z --untracked-files=all` WITHOUT `--ignored`, so gitignored runtime state under `.supervisor/` is excluded structurally, not by a denylist; a clean worktree produces nothing and prints nothing. Two directory-shaped entries are handled explicitly and differently: an UNTRACKED directory reaches that stream only as a nested repository (git never descends into one — a stray clone or `git init` a worker left behind, listed as `?? nested/`), and it IS uncommitted content, so it is copied verbatim into `untracked/<rel>/` with its own `.git` and the README names it under "Nested repositories" (a `cp` failure there is `status: partial (untracked/<rel> failed)`, never a silent drop — the drain-cycle-1 form `continue`d past it and still reported `complete`); a modified or newly added SUBMODULE is the one directory entry NOT copied — nothing exists to copy verbatim — and the patch carries it as a `Subproject commit` line, which the README states in its own item. It is wired BEFORE every plugin-owned `git worktree remove`: all three `--force` sites in `dispatch-pr-review.sh` (the pre-add cleanup of a crashed prior dispatch's dir, the header-write teardown, and the detached wrapper's EXIT trap — the site that fires on a runner's crash/SIGTERM mid-fix; `SALVAGE_BIN` is resolved above site 1 because the dispatcher runs under `set -u`, and the trap receives it as an 8th positional) and the Supervisor FINALIZE step 4 (protocol form in `skills/async-orchestration/SKILL.md` + `skills/workflow-management/SKILL.md`, runtime form as a Phase 4 gate in `agents/supervisor.md`, always its own statement, never `&&`-joined to the remove). Every call is `|| true`; every removal still runs. `test-worktree-salvage.sh` covers AC-1…AC-11 — including the real dispatcher with a stub `claude` that dirties the sibling, the pre-add site with a pre-existing dirty sibling, the trap with `.supervisor/salvage` pre-created as a FILE, the dispatcher copied without the tool, the pinned-timestamp collision arm, the 999-cap fall-through, and a gated mutant (capture short-circuited, copied with both `dirname "$0"` siblings), plus AC-7c (RUN_LOG's directory deleted before the trap fires — the trap captures the salvage output first and appends it labelled to RUN_LOG second, so a failed log redirect can never skip the salvage; a mutant re-applying the pre-fix `>>"$_log"` form goes red) and an AC-11 mutant that comments out the trap's salvage line against which AC-1/2/7/7b go red and AC-4 stays green; AC-12…AC-14 cover the directory-shaped and multi-field records — AC-12 an untracked nested repository copied verbatim with its `.git` (a gated mutant re-applying the silent `continue` goes red while AC-1 stays green), AC-13 a real `git submodule add` with a commit inside it (` M sub_mod`: absent from `modified/`, `+Subproject commit` in the patch, README item present, `status: complete`), and AC-14 two staged renames followed by a later modified file (`R  new\0old\0` — the origin field is consumed exactly once and later entries stay in register; the second origin is shaped so that, mis-read as a record, it names a real unmodified file, because a stray `a.txt` record parses to a non-existent path and the later-file assertion alone cannot see the desync; a gated mutant dropping the origin read goes red on that phantom copy). **Limits, not claims:** (a) SIGKILL / power-loss skips the salvage exactly as it skips the trap — the window narrows, it does not close; (b) a worktree whose `git status` cannot be read (broken `.git` file, git absent) is not captured — one stderr line, exit 0; (c) retention/pruning of `.supervisor/salvage/` is reconciler-repair item 06, not this release; (d) the salvage directory is created under its destination (the primary checkout by default; the dispatching checkout for the drain) even where `.supervisor/` did not exist — the deliberate inverse of `worktree-audit.sh`'s population gate, because a salvage is someone's work and a log line is not; (e) a `git worktree remove --force` typed by a human or an agent through the Bash tool is NOT a plugin-owned path — nothing intercepts it. Counts unchanged.

**v15.66.0 — the worktree log watched the wrong worktrees, and the hook that wrote it was a replacement VCS provider that provided nothing:** a real `WorktreeCreate` firing was measured on 2026-09-11 (an agent spawned with worktree isolation). Its payload carries exactly seven keys — `session_id`, `transcript_path`, `cwd`, `scratchpad_dir`, `prompt_id`, `hook_event_name`, `name` — and no `worktree_path`; the shipped hook echoed the missing field, printed an empty line, and the harness aborted with `hook succeeded but returned no worktree path`, because per the hooks reference a `WorktreeCreate` hook *replaces* the default git behaviour and must return the path of a worktree it created itself. Every one of the 5 `WORKTREE_CREATED` lines ever written recorded a creation that never happened; 0 `WORKTREE_REMOVED` lines ever fired; `docs/CAPABILITY_BASELINE.json` had recorded the abort as a NO-GO on 2026-06-06 and roadmap item #6 was closed on top of it. **Both hooks are removed** (leaf hooks 37 → 36; `WorktreeRemove` goes too — a native create can never be observed without the plugin becoming a VCS provider, so a native `REMOVED` line would be an unpairable half-record, and the harness creates and removes that population itself). **The population the plugin IS responsible for is now observed — precisely, worktrees added through the Bash tool in a repo the plugin has run in:** a third leaf on the existing `PostToolUse` Bash matcher runs NEW `scripts/worktree-audit.sh record`, which is gated on the log root ALREADY holding a `.supervisor/` directory (a scratch worktree in a repo the plugin never touched writes nothing and never creates `.supervisor/`), parses every `git [-C dir]… worktree add|remove|prune` in the tool command (multi-command strings, `-b` ordering, repeated `-C` chained per git, the path expression re-joined across whitespace while a `(`/quote/backtick is open, one layer of matching quotes — so `"../repo sp"` is one literal path), resolves the absolute path, sets `confirmed` from `git worktree list --porcelain` at record time, and appends one `jq`-built JSON line to the same `.supervisor/logs/worktrees.log`. The command string is parsed unexpanded, so when the path expression still carries shell syntax (`$(basename $(pwd))`, `$VAR`, `~`) and git does not list it, the `add` arm falls back to a branch-keyed lookup — the `-b`/positional branch is literal — over the porcelain block whose `branch refs/heads/<branch>` line matches exactly, and accepts the hit ONLY when that block's path ends with the expression's non-empty literal tail (`-mine` for `../$(basename $(pwd))-mine`): accepted ⇒ `confirmed:true`, `resolved_by:"branch"`; rejected ⇒ the expression as written, `confirmed:false`, `resolved_by:"path"`, and `branch` null unless `-b`/`-B` gave one (a positional after an unresolved expression may be a SHA or a tag). That tail rule is what keeps a FAILED add — `feature/held` already checked out at `../repo-foreign` — from being paired with `repo-foreign` and reported as a phantom orphan; a literal path git does not list stays `confirmed:false` with no fallback at all. `note` refuses a relative path out loud (one stderr line, nothing on stdout, still exit 0). `skills/async-orchestration/SKILL.md`'s Phase 3 example, which had documented the `$(basename $(pwd))` form, now uses the literal `../{project}-…` form `agents/execute-manager.md` uses; `note created|removed <abs>` is the direct entry for in-script creations the observer cannot see (wired into `dispatch-pr-review.sh` after its `git worktree add --detach`) and, hand-run from the repo, `note removed <abs>` is the dismissal for a listed worktree you are keeping (a direct `removed` clears the candidate regardless of `confirmed`; an OBSERVED `removed` with `confirmed:false` — a failed dirty-tree remove still fires the hook — does not); `report` is read-only, folds the log by path, intersects the survivors with `git worktree list`, and prints one `orphan` row per worktree recorded, never seen removed, and still live — gone is gone, the log is an input, git is the authority, and nothing is ever removed. `session-resume.sh` surfaces the rows as `### Orphaned worktrees (advisory)` on startup and resume, byte-identical when there are none. Honest limits stay in prose: a hard kill skips recording; only literal `git worktree …` through the Bash tool is seen; unexpanded shell syntax beyond what the branch fallback recovers (a `cd` inside a subshell, an unexpanded path on `remove`, a hit the tail rule rejects) fails toward under-report, with one stated exception: the tail rule is a suffix test, so a same-branch worktree whose path merely ends with the same literal suffix as a failed add's expression is still paired and can surface as a phantom advisory row (nothing is ever removed) — otherwise the only path the fallback can ever write is one whose porcelain entry ends with a suffix the command literally named; native harness worktrees are deliberately not logged; the fixed hook set reaches a session only after reinstall — **post-release re-probe (not claimed done):** after reinstall, one agent spawn with worktree isolation must succeed and `worktrees.log` must gain no line for it. Tests: NEW `test-worktree-audit.sh` (real `git worktree add` in temp repos, payloads derived from the checked-in real PostToolUse fixture, a sanitized real-firing fixture `fixtures/worktreecreate-real-firing.json` pinning the seven keys, the 1-byte defect pin against the OLD hook string, the `$(basename $(pwd))` shape recovered via the branch fallback with a prefix-sharing-branch exactness case, the failed-add over-report probe (branch held at `../repo-foreign` ⇒ no `confirmed:true` line for it, 0 rows) beside its succeeding-add control, the `--detach` null-branch case, the quoted-whitespace path re-joined and resolved literally on both `add` and `remove`, chained `-C` from the grandparent, the exact stderr line for a relative `note` path, the population gate in both directions with a `git -C` arm, the direct dismissal, and eight gated mutants — re-added bucket, `.worktree_path` read, dropped live-list intersection, ignored `confirmed` on `removed`, dropped branch fallback, dropped population gate, dropped direct-dismissal clause, dropped tail guard (the failed add pairs with `repo-foreign` while the succeeding control stays green)); `test-session-resume.sh` 193 → 230 with groups (w)–(y). Roadmap #6's RESOLVED verdict is corrected to RE-OPENED AND CLOSED DIFFERENTLY rather than quietly re-closed.

**v15.65.0 — the brief is repaired where the evidence is strongest: the merge the engine itself witnessed.** `reconcile-jobs.sh` is offline by construction, so the strongest evidence a stranded brief can have — its PR merged — was exactly what it could not fetch; measured 2026-09-11, 3 of 4 in-progress briefs classified `unknown`, including one whose merge the `/automate` engine had reconciled against the forge and then could not act on. The engine now hands that evidence in: a new opt-in, repeatable `--evidence <requirement_path>=<pr_url>` argument on `reconcile-jobs.sh` (validated lexically, never on the filesystem; compared by EXACT string equality against the brief's extracted pointer token, so it works in a worktree where the requirement file is absent) makes the matching brief `stranded_merged` with an `automate engine supplied merge evidence …` verdict the `## Outcome` records verbatim, and **scopes `--repair` to evidence-matched briefs the moment ANY `--evidence` flag is parsed — accepted or rejected** — so a rejected list repairs nothing rather than falling back to the unscoped sweep, and a key matched by two in-progress briefs (a stranded earlier attempt plus the one just merged) classifies both `unknown`/`ambiguous` and moves nothing. The engine-side seam is ONE new fail-SAFE `automate-helpers.sh brief-repair <item> <pr_url>` subcommand in the `learning-emit` mould (`set +e`, always exit 0): evidence-POSITIVE (`gh pr view` must say `MERGED`; gh absent / failing / unparseable / OPEN / CLOSED are each their own `skipped — <reason>` line and the reconciler is not invoked), it finds the reconciler as a `dirname "$0"` sibling and prints exactly one line for `## Progress`, called at `automate-loop` §6 step 1 RECONCILE (an item parked `awaiting_merge` found merged) and step 5 SYNC (after `gate-eval` printed `MERGE`). It never merges — `gate-eval` remains the sole executor of `gh pr merge --squash` and is untouched. **What the suites prove:** at the reconciler level, scoped repair against a live `stranded_closed` decoy that a bare `--repair` demonstrably moves, six malformed values (including a value with no `=` and a trailing valueless flag, and a repeated key — first wins) each ignored with the decoy staying, a no-`--evidence` porcelain pin on a three-brief fixture — a frozen golden literal that runs everywhere, plus a `cmp` against `origin/main`'s reconciler that runs only where that ref exists (a `pull_request` CI checkout has no `origin/main`, so there it prints `skipped` and is counted neither green nor red), the offline invariant under a `gh` stub that would prove itself called (direct, and through `session-resume.sh` on `resume` and `startup`), idempotency, ambiguity, every `## Outcome` field, and two mutants — the classify arm removed, and `EVIDENCE_MODE=1` moved back onto acceptance (the exact regression the scoping rule forbids) — each beside an unmodified real `brief-pointer.sh` behind a positive gate; at the helper level, ten separate fail-safe groups (the eight skip reasons plus the reconciler-refused and missing-argument arms) each asserting the run file, `## Status`, a captured `gate-eval` PARK decision and `merge.log` unchanged, OPEN/CLOSED with a marker-writing reconciler stub proving it was not invoked, and an invocation-removed helper mutant beside the real siblings whose red is discriminated by its reason string. `--help` now prints the whole header (the old fixed line range stopped above USAGE). **Honest limit:** only items the engine processed carry engine-held evidence; a `/supervisor` or `/autonomous` run outside the engine still has no online repair and remains a manual `reconcile-jobs.sh --repair`, and `session-resume.sh` is unchanged — still no forge call on any source.

**v15.64.0 — the reconciler fired on every session except the one it was for:** a stranded brief — merged work still sitting in `.supervisor/jobs/in-progress/` because the prompt-instructed lifecycle move never ran — was reported by `session-resume.sh` only on `resume`/`clear`/`compact`, the sessions where the operator is already returning to known context. A fresh `startup`, the session with no prior context at all, was silent, which is how `2026-09-05-floor-ui-redesign.md` (PR #185) sat unnoticed for five days. The dedicated `startup)` arm now composes a second line: `stranded_briefs_startup_line` runs the same offline `reconcile-jobs.sh --porcelain`, keeps ONLY `stranded_*` rows, and emits one bounded `**Stranded brief:** <path> — evidence: …` line per brief plus a `--repair` trailer — worded distinctly from every Section-1 header so the startup-path tests can assert those headers absent by exact literal. **Composition, not a second emitter:** the arm already emitted its own envelope for the curation nudge, and a second `jq -Rs` would put two JSON objects on stdout — not a valid hook response — so `curation_nudge_startup_only` is replaced by `startup_arm_emit`, which joins the two lines with a separator inserted only when both are non-empty; when nothing is stranded the body IS the curation line, and byte-identity with the previous arm falls out of the code rather than a fixture (pinned by a `cmp` against the independent pre-change formula — the arm that runs everywhere, including CI — with a silent pair counted as a failure; a second `cmp` against the captured `origin/main` hook, each run on its own fresh repo, is an additional local-only arm that self-reports `skipped` on a shallow checkout where `origin/main` cannot be shown, so it adds evidence where it runs and is counted neither passed nor failed where it does not). The helper mirrors the curation nudge's gate order exactly — `LOOMWRIGHT_STRANDED_NUDGE=0|off|false|no` opt-out, its OWN cwd-relative `.supervisor/` check (the arm exits above the shared bail), a zero-cost glob, a 24h marker (`.supervisor/.stranded-nudge-shown`, stamped only when a line is emitted), then the sibling via `dirname "$0"` — and is fail-safe on an absent, unreadable, erroring or silent reconciler, each degradation its own test group. The shared gate is NOT widened: every other section is asserted absent per item, with a control proving the fixture would have produced them; helper placement above the case is pinned statically, dynamically (empty stderr — a below-the-case definition prints `command not found` before the arm's `exit 0` swallows rc 127) and by a moved-definition mutant; and a call-removed mutant — gated non-empty, byte-different, `bash -n`-clean and POSITIVE on the curation line — turns the fires-on-startup group red while every other group stays green. No hook was added and no `gh` call was introduced. **Honest limit:** the path is offline, so a brief whose only evidence of shipping lives on the forge classifies `unknown` and is not surfaced on startup at all — it still appears as UNVERIFIED on resume — and the 24h debounce means a NEW strand that appears within a day of the previous nudge waits for the next window (the resume path is undebounced and still lists it).

**v15.63.0 — one field, one producer, two consumers, and each of them wrong in its own way:** the `- **Source requirement:**` pointer Launch Pad stamps on every brief was parsed twice, by reconcilers written a month apart. `reconcile-jobs.sh` stripped the label and nothing else, so a backticked pointer failed its very first containment guard; `stamp-requirement-status.sh` stripped surrounding delimiters but only at end-of-LINE, so a pointer ending in a parenthetical annotation survived the strip, passed containment, and then failed the existence check. Measured 2026-09-10 over the three briefs then in `.supervisor/jobs/in-progress/`: the backtick-plus-prose one resolved in NEITHER consumer, the backticks-only one in only the stamper, and only the bare-path one in both. That is not cosmetic — `2026-09-05-floor-ui-redesign.md` corresponds to PR #185, merged 2026-09-05, and sat stranded for five days because of a backtick, which four consumers read as "Supervisor is mid-run" and which structurally blocked requirement close-out too. Both parsers are now deleted in favour of ONE shared extractor, `brief-pointer.sh`, sourced by both call sites as a plain sibling. **Counts are unchanged — 14 agents / 23 commands / 41 skills / 37 hooks.** No new agent, command, skill or hook: one uncounted script and its co-located test.

**The root cause was mirror drift, so the fix is subtraction rather than a third parser.** The field has one producer and had two independent consumers, and the producer contract says "repo-root-relative path" while saying nothing about styling — yet 2 of 3 real briefs carried backticks and one carried a parenthetical. Briefs are agent-written prose, so tightening the producer cannot retire the corpus that already exists and would be the same unreliable-bookkeeping bet this repo has measured and rejected before. The extractor tolerates the shapes the corpus actually carries — bare path, backtick/quote wrapping, a whitespace-separated trailing annotation, and label case / leading-whitespace / `- `-prefix variants — by taking the pointer's first whitespace-delimited token after delimiter stripping. **The limit is stated rather than guessed around:** an annotation NOT whitespace-separated from the path (`…/05-archive-views.md(amended)`) is unreadable, no brief in the corpus has that shape, and no heuristic was added to guess at prose structure. Measured before and after on the live corpus: `did not resolve` appeared on 2 of 4 briefs and now appears on none; all four still classify `unknown` for want of evidence, which is the correct answer and deliberately not asserted as a pass condition.

**Unification moved the weaker consumer UP, and the suite is built so it could not have done otherwise.** `reconcile-jobs.sh` had no symlink guard at all; every containment check — absolute path, `..` segment, outside the requirements prefix, symlinked final component, symlinked directory component caught by physical `pwd -P` resolution of the parent — now lives in the shared extractor and both call sites run all of them. Those five guards are asserted **through each script as a subprocess**, never through the helper in isolation, because "the helper exists" and "the consumers go through it" are different claims and only the second one is the change. The refusal discriminator is pinned to the evidence STRING rather than the verdict, because `reconcile-jobs.sh` emits `unknown` both for a refused pointer and for a resolved-but-unevidenced one — a case asserting `state == unknown` would have passed with every guard deleted. Three controls keep the rest honest: the single-parser detector is run against the three pre-change parse lines as committed fixtures and must find all three (without that arm, "zero matches" was already true on the unmodified tree), and two mutants — one cutting the delimiter strip, one cutting the symlink guards — are each gated as non-empty, byte-different and parseable before their reds are trusted, with the first also proving it still resolves a bare pointer so a merely-broken mutant cannot pass for a targeted revert.

**Two deliberate divergences from nearby precedent, recorded in the helper so neither is "corrected" later.** The helper is located by a plain `dirname "$0"` sibling lookup, not by a harness-specific plugin-root variable: the files in this seam carry no entry in the vendor-coupling manifest at all, so `unclassified_default: core` holds them at allowance 0 under a hard CI gate with no `|| true`, and the resolution ladders elsewhere in `scripts/` are grandfathered debt (`validate-entry.sh` 8, `add-rule.sh` 3) rather than the sanctioned idiom. And a caller that cannot find the helper SKIPS SILENTLY rather than refusing loudly: the fail-closed load guard used by the rules writer is right for a writer whose purpose is a validated append, but these two are reconcilers for which a missing helper means no stamp and no reclassification — no write at all — and `reconcile-jobs.sh` runs on every SessionStart, where an error exit would wedge every new session. The extractor also deliberately does NOT export the name the weak function used, because bash's last-definition-wins would let a surviving local definition restore the weak guard set while every parsing test stayed green. 58 -> 91 assertions across the three suites.

**v15.62.0 — every item this system has ever executed was typed by a human, and the ledger can only ever propose our own mistakes back to us:** `/propose` read one basis, `.supervisor/floor/floor.json`, which is derived entirely from churn we recorded ourselves. A project's most valuable missing work is rarely in its own ledger — it is the thing the domain expects and the app lacks (SSO, rate limits, multi-currency), and nothing in this system looked outward at all: `/capability-check --strategy` is maintainer-only and plugin-scoped, and `/product-owner --brainstorm` only researches a problem a human has already named. This release adds `--domain`, the **second basis of one command**: `propose-domain.sh` reads the committed product store (`.agent/product.json`, through `read-product.sh` — never re-parsed, so `stance_default_action` keeps its single home), inventories this project's own code and docs by direct grep, fetches competitor sources, and writes the same kind of evidence-carrying candidate into the same `.supervisor/requirements/proposed/` folder under the same propose-only contract. **Counts are unchanged — 14 agents / 23 commands / 41 skills / 37 hooks.** No new command, agent, skill or hook: one flag, one uncounted script.

**The two bases never run in one invocation, and that is a cost boundary rather than a style choice.** The default basis is local `jq` over a local file: it reaches nothing. The domain basis spends fetch budget and emits judgment-class output, so bare `/propose` must never trigger a network call — which is why `--domain` is explicit and never implicit, the precedent being `/capability-check`'s two never-mixed modes. Every external call in the domain basis goes through exactly one seam, `fetch_source`, resolved from `PROPOSE_DOMAIN_FETCH_CMD` and invoked as `<cmd> <url>`. **Unset means nothing is fetched at all** — there is deliberately no implicit `curl`, no `wget` and no shell default — which is what makes "bare `/propose` makes zero external calls" and "a cap of 1 makes exactly one call" mechanically assertable with a counting stub rather than read off the source. The cap (`PROPOSE_DOMAIN_MAX_FETCHES`, default 5) covers the whole run; local reads never draw on it. Filenames are namespaced `domain--<slug>.md`, so the two bases cannot collide in `proposed/`, and only the ledger basis authors that directory's `README.md` contract.

**The honest limits, stated here because the emitted files state them too.** (1) **The expectation catalogue is fixed** — ten domain expectations the run knows how to look for, seven marked baseline and three edge. It is not discovered, it is not learned, and it is emphatically **not evidence**: nothing is written unless a source this run actually fetched corroborates the expectation, so with no fetcher there is no corroboration and **no file is written at all** — the run says so and reports coverage as partial. (2) **The fetched half is the weakest of the three inputs** and every emitted file says so in the section heading: it is competitor and vendor text, which overstates, and no user has been observed asking for any of it. The `TABLE-STAKES` / `DIFFERENTIATOR` / `NOT-FOR-US` classification is likewise a judgment about the domain, not a measurement — there is no score, rank, priority or ordering field anywhere in the output. (3) **`--domain` is a no-op in most repos today**, because the 02a product store is per-project and usually absent: it then prints `**No product context: .agent/product.json is absent.**` with the path it looked at and the `propose-product.sh` bootstrap offer, writes nothing, fetches nothing and exits 0. Named, never a silent skip.

**Unverified is not absent — the rule this lane was most likely to break.** "We don't have X" is the claim a domain basis gets wrong, so it is never asserted from silence. A capability named in the docs but unconfirmed in code, one carrying no inventory terms, or one searched against an empty surface is emitted as `unverified` **with the reason**; only a search that actually ran against a non-empty code surface and found nothing may say `missing`. Every gap reports where it looked — both surfaces with their file counts, the per-surface cap, and the exact terms used, matched case-insensitively **on word boundaries**, because a bare `grep -F` for the acronym `sso` matches the word "processor" and invented a corroborating source on the first fixture run. Each cited source carries its url and the store's recorded `last_fetched`; a source past `PROPOSE_DOMAIN_MAX_SOURCE_AGE_SECONDS` (default 7776000 = 90d) is named **STALE** rather than cited silently, an unparseable date degrades to *staleness unknown*, and never-fetched is never rendered as a date — unknown is not old.

**Two self-confirmations found by running it, not by reading it.** The output directory sits under the project root by default, so a candidate written by an **earlier run** was read back as project documentation and flipped an inventory status from `missing` to `unverified` — the output and requirements directories and every `domain--*.md` file are now pruned from both surfaces wherever they sit. And the script's own catalogue contains every term it searches for, so scanning the repo that **ships** it confirmed every capability and made every real gap disappear as "already present": the script excludes itself, and the product store too, since the store's own competitor prose would otherwise corroborate the question it is being asked. A tool must not measure itself.

**A third round, and the pattern held: a fix in one direction is not a fixed class.** (1) The word-boundary correction was applied to `classify_gap` without auditing the catalogue it reads — a scope column authored for SUBSTRING matching, carrying the deliberate stem `invoic`. No word-boundary pattern can match a stem, so the fix for over-matching silently created **under-matching**: measured, "an invoicing platform for freelancers" and "an ecommerce storefront builder" both flipped from `TABLE-STAKES` to `NOT-FOR-US`, so an invoicing product's PCI-vaulting and multi-currency gaps were never written and nothing reported them — the same silently-disappears class as a false `present`. Every stem in both scope rows is now spelled out, and a case asserts both directions plus a catalogue-wide sweep for surviving stems. (2) The staged-write rewrite regressed the **planted-directory** path: `mv -f` into a directory succeeds, moving the temp inside it and returning 0, so the run counted a candidate it never wrote and abandoned a PID-named temp in a tree this basis promises to leave nothing in — 6 claimed against 5 real files, zero refusals. The parent commit had refused it correctly for free, because `cat >` fails on a directory; the replacement lost that and the refusal is now explicit. (3) **AC16's mutation control was the seventh vacuous one on this branch, and it guarded the mechanism that shipped defect (1).** Its mutant referenced a variable the same commit had unbound, so under `set -u` it died inside a command substitution and produced an EMPTY classification — which satisfied an absence-of-`NOT-FOR-US` assertion. Any mutation that merely broke the function would have passed it identically. The mutant is the real pre-fix code now, the assertion is on the classification VALUE, and every mutant run additionally refuses an unbound-variable death. The standing lesson, stated plainly because it recurred seven times here: **a mutation control is not evidence until it has been seen to fail for the reason it names.** 145 -> 159 assertions.

**A second review round, and every finding was in the FIX rather than the feature.** (1) The symlink guard closed the wrong half of its own threat model: `[ -L ]` is false for a **hardlink**, and `cat >` truncates the shared inode — measured, a hardlink planted at a legal name took 5,856 bytes into a file outside the output directory with no refusal at all. The write now stages to a temp entry and `mv -f`s into place, which REPLACES the directory entry instead of writing through it, closing the hardlink case, the check-then-write race on the symlink path, and any future link type in one change. The named symlink refusal is kept, because naming a planted link is more useful than silently replacing it. (2) `classify_gap`'s scope match was a **bare substring** while the inventory matcher next to it built word boundaries — the second instance of the defect that made `sso` match "processor", and this one decides `TABLE-STAKES` versus `NOT-FOR-US`. Measured: a store whose domain reads "a flashcard-based spaced-repetition app" matched the scope term `card` and was told PCI card-data vaulting is table stakes. (3) The new cap knob's documentation promised an out-of-range value is *named and ignored*, while the clamp was a silent `||` fallback — `0`, a negative and an overflow value were all replaced with the default with no message, and no test covered those branches at all. (4) The cap line attributed a **doc-only** truncation to the code surface: every number in it was true and the sentence was not, which for a basis whose whole contract is evidence honesty is the worst kind of wrong. It now names the surface actually cut short. One more restated-list drift — `agent-help.md`'s env enumeration missed the new knob, a class no gate here can catch. Every fix carries a mutation control that turns its case RED with the mechanism deleted; several of those controls were themselves vacuous first, one because a `sed` cut a block and left a dangling `|| {`, so the mutant could not parse and the control read as broken rather than as passing. 121 -> 145 assertions.

**Three defects in this release found by reviewing and running it, not by reading it.** (1) The per-surface file cap was applied BEFORE the surface was counted, so on any tree above the cap the file count equalled the cap, the empty-surface arm could never fire, and a capability whose evidence sat past the cap was reported **absent** — in a file that simultaneously stated truncation is why it would not be. Unreachable in this repo at 268 code files, which is exactly why 104 green assertions were no evidence against it: the cap is now env-overridable so the case is reachable, the count is taken before the cut, and **either** surface being truncated (not just the code one) forces `unverified`. (2) `guarded_write` proved a name was a plain file inside the output directory but not that the entry AT that name was a regular file — `cat >` follows a symlink, so a symlink planted at a legal name walked the write back out of the directory the guard had just cleared. Refused now, not unlinked: this basis never removes a file it did not write. (3) The self-confirmation exclusion was under-scoped. Excluding the script itself was not enough — its **self-test** must name every catalogue term in order to assert on them, and its committed **fixture tree** sits inside the repo being scanned, so `single-sign-on`, `two-factor-authentication` and `usage-analytics` were all confirmed away by the searcher's own files, silently dropping 5 real candidates to 3. A false `present` is worse than a false `missing`: the candidate is never written, so nothing reports it. All three carry mutation controls that turn the case RED with the mechanism deleted. 104 -> 121 assertions.

**v15.61.0 — the page could not say which build was drawing it, and a lane that never spoke never left:** two faults found by using the thing. Three separate incidents in one day were the same shape — a page rendering bytes older than the code just merged (an un-updated plugin, a bundle applied from a stale checkout, a stale ui directory) — and each cost a multi-step trace because **nothing on the page said which build it was**. `serve` now publishes `module.bundle_version` into the index it already writes (no new fetch, no stamped file), and the header reads `· bundle v15.61.0`. An engine that cannot read a version omits the field and the page says **`bundle version unknown`** rather than claiming one.

**The second is a rule that was right alone and wrong in company.** `last_ts` is a lane's FRESHNESS and may only come from a real recorded event — which is why an `agent_identity` line deliberately carries no `ts`. But a lane that had never emitted then had no time of any kind, so a recency filter could never drop it, while the lanes that *did* carry events aged out normally. A long session therefore converged to a list of only the rows nothing was known about: measured on a real run, **ten rows all reading zero while the four rows that had events had been filtered away**. The projector now carries `identified_at` from the identity line's own `recorded_at`, the page ages a never-emitting lane by it, and a 0-event lane states **how long ago it was spawned**. The separation is the point and is asserted directly: `identified_at` is used for the age filter only, is never assigned into `last_ts`, and is never rendered as an event age.

**Two defects in the fix itself, both found by running it rather than reading it.** The version probe first ran at **load time**, spending a `jq` on every invocation of the engine — `add`, `list`, `forget`, `check` — for a value only the served index uses; it also reddened `(o6)`, the lock-contention case, which deliberately slows `jq` to widen its critical section, because a load-time probe runs under that same shim and shifts the timing the case measures. It is now a function called at the index write, and `(j58)` pins it as a call rather than an assignment. The probe also originally used this file's own `have()` helper, which is defined further down — so at load time it was not a function yet and the feature was silently disabled behind a stderr line.

**One test defect too, of exactly the kind this suite exists to catch.** `(j59)`'s control counted with `occ()`, which hands its pattern to awk as a DYNAMIC regex where the string escape collapses and the escaped paren reopens as a group — so it matched nothing, both counts read 0, and comparing equal reported the control as broken rather than the code. It counts with `grep -cF` now. The control also had to count rather than check for absence: `tsToEpoch(r.identified_at)` appears three times, so a mutant removing only the age arm leaves the literal behind.

**Review round.** `RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` — the file CLAUDE.md names as the schema single source of truth — enumerated the per-agent row fields without `identified_at` and carried no version-history bullet for it, while every previous addition to that same array got one. Its required-key parse could not catch it, because the field is optional; the enumerated prose is what a consumer actually reads. Both fixed. The review also found a dead `sed` in `(zz2)` whose output the following block overwrote and whose `\n` could never match in a line-at-a-time `s///` — and validating that turned up something the review did not: **`(zz2)` was a vacuous control.** It built a mutant and asserted only that the file differed, never running it and never requiring `(zz)` to redden. It now runs both variants against a fixture with `recorded_at` on a NON-identity line — the only shape that discriminates them, since with `recorded_at` only ever on identity lines both agree and the control is green for the wrong reason — and requires the projector to ignore it (false) where the any-line mutant picks it up (true). build-floor stays at **373** — the control was replaced rather than added, and the count is stated here only because a first draft of this sentence claimed 374 from arithmetic instead of from a run.

**Second review round, and the finding is the SAME awk trap one paragraph up.** `(j58)`'s leak check passed its pattern to `occ()` with SINGLE backslashes — `identified_at.*fmtAge\(age\)` — so awk's `-v` escape processing dropped them and the parens recompiled as a group, making the pattern mean "fmtAge followed immediately by age", which never occurs. Measured on a control file that DOES contain the leak: the single-backslash form counts **0**, `grep -cE` counts **1**. The assertion was green regardless of the invariant it named — in the very release whose changelog documents finding this in `(j59)`. It now counts with `grep`, and **(j60)** plants an `identified_at → fmtAge(age)` leak and requires the count to see it, because a fixed pattern is still only a claim until something makes it fail. A sweep of every remaining `occ()` call carrying a backslash found all three already using the doubled form, so this was the only instance. setup-ui 477 → 478.

**Not shipped, for lack of evidence: cache-busting the script URL.** The page references `floor.js` with no version and the server sends no `Cache-Control`, which looked like the cause of a stale render — until the same text appeared in a private window, which has no cache. Diagnosing on that theory cost a round trip, and the real cause was elsewhere. A fix with no measurement behind it is what this release's own header line exists to make unnecessary.

**v15.60.0 — an agent with no emitter can never record an event, and three of five had none:** v15.57.0 made The Floor name its lanes, and the first real run under it showed most of them reading `spawned, no events recorded yet` forever. Measured against `hooks.json` rather than guessed: of the five agent types on screen — `worker`, `code-reviewer`, `plan-reviewer`, `rubric-grader` and Claude Code's own `general-purpose` — only the first two had a `SubagentStop` emitter registered. **The other three could not emit at all.** The identity hook gave them a name; nothing gave them an event.

**A lane emitter is now registered for every agent this plugin ships** (hooks 27 → 37): `context-keeper`, `execute-manager`, `launch-pad-runner`, `orchestrator`, `plan-reviewer`, `product-owner`, `qa-strategist`, `red-team-reviewer`, `review-pr-runner` and `rubric-grader` join the **three** matchers that already ran this script (`code-reviewer`, `qa-executor`, `supervisor-runner`). `worker` also produces a lane, but via `emit-progress-event.sh` — a different script, so it is not part of this one's fan-out. `general-purpose` stays uncovered on purpose — it is Claude Code's own agent type, not one this plugin has any business claiming a matcher for, so a lane for it still reads zero and that is honest rather than fixable here.

**The fan-out this creates is the part worth knowing, and it is asserted rather than reasoned about.** A `SubagentStop` matcher only discriminates when the payload carries an `agent_type`; an UNTYPED completion matches every matcher at once, so one such stop now fans out to **thirteen** concurrent invocations of this emitter against one file (3 + 10 — the fourteenth SubagentStop emitter is `worker`'s `emit-progress-event.sh`, a different script writing a different line). v15.55.0's mkdir-locked adjacent-duplicate guard is what collapses them to a single line — but that guard was written and tested against **two**, and at fourteen the bounded lock wait (20 × 50 ms) becomes a real budget whose exhaustion appends unguarded by design. `test-token-ledger.sh` case 23 fires **fourteen** concurrently — one more than production can produce, deliberately, because headroom above the real figure is the safe direction for a control like this — and requires exactly one line, because the failure mode here is a PARTIAL collapse — some duplicates, not all — which no smaller case can see. 174 → 175.

**And the wording that started this is corrected: `spawned, no events recorded`, without the "yet".** "Yet" promises events that are coming, and for a type with no emitter they never were.

**v15.59.1 — the rule/churn correlation reprinted a line's evidence once per matching path:** `correlations[].matched` is one entry per `(line, path, pattern)` triple, but the ledger's evidence is a property of the **line**. The projector already knew this — it hoists evidence into `evidence_by_line` keyed by line, with a comment measuring the ~4x duplication that motivated the hoist — but `floor-ui/floor.js` looped `matched` and re-joined that evidence onto every match, so the bytes were de-duplicated on the wire and the pixels never were. On this repo's own store: **347 rendered rows for 153 (rule, line) facts over 79 distinct ledger lines**, one line rendered 19 times, because a rule scoped to `["CLAUDE.md", "loomwright/scripts/*"]` claims every script path a commit touched. The Floor now draws **one row per ledger line**; a line with a single match keeps its original wording verbatim, and the paths are not dropped — they move to one subordinate line **per glob**, so which pattern claimed which path stays checkable. Rendered evidence text 87,657 → 48,477 characters.

**No fixture could tell the two renders apart, and none could tell the projector's two serialisations apart either.** Every line in every committed fixture matched exactly **once**, so a per-line row and a per-match row produced identical output, and `evidence_by_line`'s whole reason for existing was equally unfalsifiable — the hoist could have been reverted with the suite staying green. `floor-postmortem.jsonl`'s first line now carries three in-scope paths across both of the fixture rule's globs (plus one out-of-scope path, so the globs are still shown excluding). `test-build-floor.sh` asserts `matched` = 5, that **one** line carries three of them, that those span **two** distinct patterns, and that `evidence_by_line` still holds **3** keys rather than 5. `test-setup-ui.sh` gains (j55) — the single row-creating `createElement('li')` sits in a loop bounded by the line grouping, found by its **bound** rather than by a literal so renaming the index cannot disarm it — plus (j55b) on the grouping key, (j56) a mutation control reverting that bound to `matched.length`, and (j57) asserting the browser fixture has something to collapse. The honest limit, and it is the same one group (b) carries: CI installs Node only **after** the bash suite runs, so these gates read floor.js's structure rather than executing it. Suite 379 / 474; all 74 `test-*.sh` files and the `scripts/check-*.sh` gates pass.

**v15.59.0 — every product-shaped surface needed to know what the project is, and none of them could find out:** `/product-owner` took its audience from whatever the human typed into the prompt and forgot it the moment the run ended. Measured 2026-09-06, `grep -rl "competitors"` hit three plugin files and **zero** project-level stores — there was nowhere to record that a repo is a B2B invoicing API whose users are finance teams and whose missing SSO is table stakes rather than a differentiator. This release adds one committed place to say it: **`.agent/product.json`**, a sibling of `.agent/rules/` and `.agent/orientation/` holding `domain`, `stance`, `audience`, `competitors[]` and a `written_at`/`head_sha` provenance pair, plus `read-product.sh` to emit it and `propose-product.sh` to create it. **Counts are unchanged — 14 agents / 23 commands / 41 skills / 27 hooks.** The requirement considered a `/setup` module and rejected it on the evidence of the closest precedent: `rules` earns a module because it seeds *portable* content, and product context is project-specific by definition, so the one property that justifies a module has no analogue here. Five uncounted scripts, no new command, agent, hook or skill.

**`stance` is data, not a policy the lane carries.** It decides the default action on a discovered gap, and hard-coding either answer would make a downstream lane tell a payments app to skip its missing fraud checks because parity is a NO for a tool. So the reader emits a **derived `stance_default_action`** from a two-entry lookup keyed on the stored value (`product` ⇒ `build-highest-priority`, `tool` ⇒ `do-not-build-by-default`) — a function of project data rather than a constant — and when `stance` does not resolve it emits `unset` and substitutes **neither** default. The mapping lives in exactly one named place in code and one table in `RESULT_SCHEMAS.md`; a consumer reads it and must not re-derive it. The bootstrap will not guess it either: `--confirm` without `--stance` refuses and writes nothing, while a bare run still prints a full proposal saying that is what stands between it and a write. Competitors are **never auto-detected and never fetched** — every entry is written `last_fetched: null`, meaning *never fetched*, because nothing here fetches anything, and a date there would be an invention.

**`last_fetched` is nullable-required, which is the shape this repo has already been bitten by.** The key is required and its value may legitimately be `null`; a present-but-wrong-type guard silently accepts a MISSING key when null is also legal, which is exactly how PR #84's `read-rules.sh` `check` field shipped past both a review and a full-marks rubric. Presence is therefore asserted with jq `has()` on both sides — reader and writer — and the missing-key and explicit-null cases are tested **separately**, never collapsed. The reader renders them apart (`unset (key missing)` versus `never fetched`) and neither is ever rendered as a date or as "stale": unknown is not old.

**Absence is announced by the consumer seam, not by the reader — and that division of labour is the whole lesson.** The reader is advisory and fail-safe: it always exits 0 and emits nothing on stdout when the store is absent, malformed, or `jq` is missing, naming the reason on stderr in all three cases. Silence is right for a reader that must not break its callers, but *silent no-op-when-absent* is a documented feature-killer here: `brain-context` was read-path-only with no bootstrap path, and ended up absent, its bridge **255 commits** stale, and `brain_context` appearing **zero times across every session log**. So `/product-owner`'s `Context Setup` gains a `Load Product Context` step, in both the agent and the command mirror, which names `.agent/product.json` and offers the propose-only bootstrap when the store is missing — and never skips quietly. That is prose a reviewer reads past, so `test-product-seam.sh` holds it with a **mutation control**: deleting the message from *either* seam file must fail the suite, and so must rewriting the runtime plugin-root prefix to a developer-side `loomwright/scripts/...` path, which would be silently dead for every marketplace install while the message stayed intact. Each mutant is gated on being non-empty and differing from the original before its run is trusted, because a silently-invalid mutant passes every fail-open assertion.

**The bootstrap is the seventh sole writer, and the first that deliberately does not share `validate-entry.sh`.** That validator's three blocking checks — contradiction, duplicate, provenance — are all defined against an append-only store of curated prose *entries*; `.agent/product.json` is a single structured object, so there is no second entry for a new one to duplicate or contradict and the checks have no referent. It validates **shape** instead, and the confirm gate is untouched. `AGENT_GUIDELINES.md` records both the exception and a second thing worth more than the row itself: the section's mechanical `git ls-files <store-path>` test gives the **wrong** answer here, returning empty in this repo now and forever because the plugin deliberately ships no store of its own — the bootstrap creates one per project at runtime. Read literally it would answer "not tracked ⇒ no gate", the opposite of the truth. The correct derivation is by destination — the store lands in the tracked `.agent/` tree and is committed by design.

**Write containment is why the tests look the way they do.** This repo's root IS a primary checkout, so the writer's non-primary-checkout guard (the `.git`-is-a-FILE discriminator carried from `add-rule.sh`) does **not** protect it, and a `--confirm` run here would create a real store in a tracked directory. Every case therefore runs inside a `mktemp -d` + `git init` throwaway tree, the before/after state is compared by hashing the tree rather than by reading the code, and the suite asserts the developer's real `.agent/` is byte-identical at the end. Those containment assertions carry their own mutation control — a planted stray file and an in-place content edit must both change the hash — because a hash that noticed nothing would make every "wrote nothing" assertion vacuous while printing `ok`. **Measured ratchet delta** (`bash scripts/check-vendor-coupling.sh`, run after `git add -N` because the scanner enumerates via `git ls-files` and an untracked file reads as a false zero): **610 → 613 references, 78 → 79 files, breaches unchanged at 0**. All three occurrences are in `test-product-seam.sh`, which must name the harness variable literally in order to assert that the seams use it; one allowance row was raised in the same change. The other four new scripts contribute **zero** — measured, not assumed — and the two seam files are adapter-classified and uncounted.

**v15.58.1 — three numbers in one block, on two different bases:** `/propose`'s emitted candidate prints its pair count, its per-class count, and a ledger total. The first two filter to entries carrying a real `class`/`flow_stage` string; the third was a raw `length` over `.surfaces.postmortem.detail.entries` printed under the label **"classified entries in the ledger"**. `build-floor.sh` includes `class`/`flow_stage` only CONDITIONALLY on type, so an entry can legitimately appear with no class key at all — meaning the label overstated its basis, in a document whose own `## Evidence` section asserts that nothing in it is inferred. Now counted on the same basis as its siblings.

**The reason it survived review-by-reading is the reason it needed a new fixture.** Every committed fixture was **100% classified**, so the filtered and unfiltered counts were identical and no assertion could tell them apart — the mismatch was structurally invisible to a suite that already carried 14 mutation controls. AC15 ships `floor-unclassified.json` (17 entries, 12 classified, 5 built the way `build-floor.sh` emits a malformed `categories[]` object: no `class` key at all), asserts the printed number matches an independently recomputed classified count, and gates that on a discriminating-fixture precondition so it cannot pass on an all-classified basis. Its mutation control reverts the filter to the raw `length` and requires the printed number to become **17**. Suite 112 → 117; all 74 `test-*.sh` files and eight `scripts/check-*.sh` gates pass.

**v15.58.0 — the proposer shipped with nothing to call it:** this release's predecessor added `propose-work.sh`, a reader that turns the churn ledger into evidence-carrying candidate requirements. It was tested to 101 assertions and **wired to nothing** — no command, no hook, no skill, no schedule referenced it, so the only way to make the loop propose was to know the script's path and type it. The commit that shipped it was titled *"the loop recorded 109 mismatches and proposed nothing"*, which was an overclaim: the loop still proposed nothing, because nothing ran the proposer. The gap was in the requirement, not the implementation — its non-goals close every automatic path (**no new hook**, "no auto-queue, no auto-dispatch, not now, not behind a flag") and never name the manual one, so the brief inherited a proposer with no invocation seam and three Plan Review spawns plus two code reviews all passed it. `claude-review` did note the script was unwired and reasonably waved it through as "intake-only scope, dispatch left for later" — which is how a headline outruns its diff.

**`/propose` is that seam, and deliberately the smallest one that closes it** (commands 22 → 23). A command, not a hook: a hook is forbidden by the requirement's own non-goals and would also decide *when* to propose, which is a cadence question nobody has answered. `/propose` answers only "when you ask." It shells out to the tested implementation rather than restating its behaviour, so the doc surface cannot drift from the code, and it documents the one thing the emitted files alone cannot teach: that **deleting a proposal is not a durable dismissal** — the cited coordinates are the earliest entries for a pair, so the `evidence-set:` token is stable and the candidate returns on the next run. The durable path is to paste that token into a `## Status: done` requirement file. Counts: 14 agents / 23 commands / 41 skills / 27 hooks. **Two further defects, both found by review after the seam landed, both the same class the seam was built for.** `/propose` was absent from `agent-help.md`'s own Command Reference — the count said 23 and the reference described 22, because bumping a number and adding the thing it counts are separate acts and only the first was mechanised. **No gate could catch it:** `check-doc-currency.sh` and the `test-setup-ui.sh` ratchet both assert that NUMBERS match; neither asks whether the counted thing exists. So the fix is the gate — `check-doc-currency.sh` gains a **`[command-documented]`** check requiring every file in `commands/` to have a `### /<cmd>` section (`/agent-help` exempt: it IS the reference). Building it surfaced a pre-existing gap, `/telemetry`, which was **added rather than exempted**, because a carve-out for the one thing a gate should catch is how gates go vacuous. And `guarded_write` joined filenames as `<cls>--<fs>.md`, so `(a, b--c)` and `(a--b, c)` both resolved to `a--b--c.md` and one silently overwrote the other — unreachable today only because the six-class enum lives in `skills/pr-postmortem`, another file, not the boundary this script reads. Now refused by name, with AC14's mutation control asserting the two distinct pairs really do collapse to ONE file without the guard. Suite 101 → 112.

**A count bump moves two gates in opposite directions.** `test-setup-ui.sh` pairs **(z13)**, which sweeps for the PREVIOUS count as stale residue, with **(z14)**, its anti-vacuity twin asserting the CURRENT count is present. Bumping 22 → 23 required editing both — (z13) 21 → 22 **and** (z14) 22 → 23 — and grepping the OLD value, the standing advice after a count bump, surfaces (z13) and **structurally cannot** surface (z14), which holds the value that was current until the bump and so goes red precisely BECAUSE the docs became correct. Both were invisible locally because `check-doc-currency.sh` never scans `test-*.sh`. A third miss followed from the same edit: `README`'s live `(22 commands)` pointer, a current-state claim **no `check-*.sh` gate verifies** — the only thing that catches that form is the stale sweep, after the fact. **Measured ratchet delta** (`bash scripts/check-vendor-coupling.sh`, run after `git add -N` because the scanner enumerates via `git ls-files` and an untracked file reads as a false zero): **unchanged at 610 references / 78 files / 0 breaches**; scanned 413 → 414, adapter-exempt 41 → 42, since `loomwright/commands/` is adapter-classified and the new file contributes nothing.

**Two more, found by review after the above and fixed in the same release.** `check`'s leftover note ends *"'serve' overwrites it; 'stop' deletes it"* — and `stop` did not, in that state. It returned at its pidfile check before ever reaching the cleanup, so the one case the note sends a reader to `stop` for (a leftover URL whose pidfile is gone, which is also how `check` reaches that arm) printed `no-op` and left the file. A claim no code backs. `stop` now removes it there and **reports the removal**, since a stale credential quietly deleted is indistinguishable from one that was never there.

**And the page discarded its token on any 403.** The guard answers 403 for three distinct reasons and names which in the body: the token, the `Origin`, or the `Host`. Dropping on all three threw away a token that was very likely fine — an extension or a proxy rewriting a header is not a stale credential — and then told the reader a specific and wrong story about a previous run, sending them to re-paste a URL that reproduces the identical refusal. The page now reads the reason the server already provides: it discards only on `token-missing-or-wrong`, and keeps the token for the other two while saying so. A cross-file case asserts the page's literal is byte-identical to the engine's spelling, because a rename on either side would silently stop the comparison matching and restore the replay-for-ever bug under the fix's own name.

**v15.57.0 — the view was showing the operator's own chat, not their run:** five faults in one region, all the same mistake — **the page organised by the wrong unit**. It picked the session with the newest `ts`, and a person working directly in Claude Code emits the same hook events as a Supervisor run while being the thing they are actively typing into. So the newest session was almost always the operator's own chat, and an `/automate` run sat unseen one row down in a list of 183 sessions with nothing on the page revealing it.

**The shown session is now chosen by recorded plugin work, not by recency.** A session qualifies when it recorded an agent whose `agent_type` starts `loomwright:`, or an `autonomous_session_start` — both positive identifications from lines the emitters already write, never an inference from absence. Measured when the rule was written: of six recent sessions, exactly one qualified. **It fails OPEN** — with nothing qualifying, the newest recorded session is still shown and labelled `newest_recorded`, because showing nothing is worse than showing the operator's own session with a label saying so. **This only became possible with v15.56.0's identity hook**: before it, `agent_type` was absent on 976 of 1,040 agent ids and there was nothing to filter on. Two consequences are reported rather than hidden — a session recorded before that hook cannot qualify, and a plugin run that has not spawned anything yet has nothing to qualify with.

**A `cc_session_id` is not a unit of work, and lanes stopped pretending it is.** It survives `/clear` and survives resume, so one id can persist for days and accumulate every agent that ever ran under it: measured on a real project, one session's lanes spanned **four days**, three last recorded on Sep 2 sitting beside three from the last minute — all presented as current. Lanes now list only those with an event inside a window (`?lane=`, default 30 min) and the heading **counts what was dropped**, because a lane that silently vanishes is indistinguishable from one that never existed. A lane with **no** recorded `ts` is not old — it is unknown — and is never filtered out.

**Three smaller honesty faults in the same region.** `EXECUTE` in a cell headed `Execute` rendered as "Execute EXECUTE" — the value slot repeating the label; a coinciding phase is now a marker while a differing one (`FINALIZE` under Review) keeps the word, which is information.
