It uses `jq empty`, bulk-first for speed and per-file only on failure, because jq's bulk parse error does not name the file and "the reason is named" requires naming the offender. **The schema block is annotated YAML, not a JSON example** — the self-test **parses the required-key set back OUT of `## FLOOR_PROJECTION` in `RESULT_SCHEMAS.md`** (the `emit-block-parses` precedent of parsing a MANIFEST out of source rather than restating it), so a doc/validator divergence fails CI; a fenced JSON example would carry no required/optional marking, leave nothing to parse, and silently imply every field is mandatory — contradicting the omit-absent-evidence rule. Each required key removed in turn is rejected **by name**, with a control proving the intact payload passes and an optional-key removal still passing, so the rejection is discriminating rather than blanket. **The tests are hermetic by construction, and that is the load-bearing property.** `.gitignore`'s `.supervisor/*` means eight of the nine inputs are absent from every fresh clone and every worktree — measured: a fresh `git worktree` has **0** `logs/*.jsonl` — while CI runs every `loomwright/scripts/test-*.sh` as a hard gate in exactly that environment, so a real-tree assertion would compare 0 to 0 and go **silently green**. Every assertion runs against `mktemp -d` + `git init` trees with known counts; the real-`.supervisor/` run is a local-only corroboration that prints `SKIPPED — <reason>` and is reported separately, never counted as a pass, and it reads the live surfaces through a symlink mirror so the developer's checkout is provably byte-unchanged. **Containment is hashed with `find`, never git:** `.supervisor/` is *ignored*, not untracked, and a probe written into it is reported by **neither** `git status --porcelain` **nor** `--ignored` — so a git-based tree check sees neither an illegal write nor the legitimate output and could never fail. The test measures that blindness itself rather than asserting it. **Determinism diffs two RAW, unfiltered runs under two DIFFERENT injected timestamps** (a `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`-style seam) and demands exactly one changed line pair, then demands byte-identity under the same injected value — filtering the timestamp line first would never exercise the filter, since two consecutive runs share a wall-clock second and the test would pass *with* the defect. **Mutation controls prove both invariants can turn red:** a variant writing one byte to `.supervisor/state.md` (gated on still being `bash -n` clean and still reaching its own success line) must break the containment assertion, and a variant embedding a second clock read must break the single-clock-read pin. **Two defects were found INSIDE this release and are recorded here because the entry above would otherwise assert them away.** First, `exit 0 always` was simply **false on every fresh clone, every worktree, and for every user before their first run**: macOS bash 3.2 aborts expanding an EMPTY array under `set -u`, so `"${logfiles[@]}"` raised `unbound variable` and exited 1 — while bash 4+ on Linux expands it happily, making this **macOS-red / Linux-green**, the exact inverse polarity of the `stat`-flavour trap and therefore invisible to CI. Fixed with `${arr[@]+"${arr[@]}"}`. Second, **the projector twice proved a zero it never measured** — the precise lie it exists to refuse. `count_json_docs` lacked the readability guard its sibling `count_glob` already had, and under nullglob an unreadable directory is indistinguishable from an empty one, so `drain_rounds`/`rules` reported `counted: 0` *with an `mtime_epoch` implying a real reading*; separately the session reader's bulk `cat … 2>/dev/null` silently dropped an unreadable log, reporting `counted` with an undercount and `lines_malformed: 0` actively asserting cleanliness. Both now report `unverified` with the offending path named, matching the siblings that were already correct. **The root cause was a hole in the tests, not the script:** the 105-assertion suite made no input unreadable anywhere — a single `chmod` appeared in the whole file, on the `stat` stub — so all three *existing* `! -r` branches had never once executed. The coverage was written FIRST and run against the unfixed script, where 15 assertions failed on exactly the two missing guards; suite 105 → 155. A later pass closed two more branches the suite structurally could not have failed on — blank/whitespace-only log lines (the live tree reports 0 across 13,085 lines, so even the real-tree corroboration could never reach that branch) and `generated_at_epoch: null` on a failed clock read, documented as always-present-but-null and asserted in prose only. Both behaviours were verified correct BEFORE the tests were written, so the assertions pin correct behaviour rather than freeze a bug; each was observed failing against a deliberately broken variant first. Final suite: **187**. The artefact is gitignored and regenerable; nothing commits it. Counts unchanged.

**v15.42.0 — the plugin had no visual surface, and the legend nobody generated had drifted:** `grep -rn "statusLine"` over the tracked tree returned **0 hits** — the status-line surface the host offers was entirely unused, so the only way to know a run's phase was to open `.supervisor/state.md` by hand, which is exactly what a user will not do while a long run is in flight. Three things ship. **(1) `status-line.sh`** renders ONE line — phase · branch · finished-over-total subtasks · age of the newest logged event — read-only, dependency-free, and it ALWAYS exits 0. Every field is **omitted rather than guessed** when its source is absent; a missing state file reads `Loomwright · no run state`. **The subtask counter reads BOTH table shapes that exist on disk** — the live `state.md`'s `| # | Title | Status | Review |` with bare-integer ids, and the 7-column `| ID | … |` schema with ids like `ST1`/`subtask_1`/`BD-1.2` and lowercase `completed` that 7 of the 17 history files carry. Which of the two is authoritative is an unresolved owner decision, and this read-only consumer deliberately does not vote on it: it matches any non-empty id that is not a separator or header cell, and matches the status as a **column** rather than as a substring of the line, so a subtask merely *titled* “Delete the COMPLETED marker handling” is not counted as finished. `FAILED`/`SKIPPED`/`ABANDONED` are terminal for `reconcile-resume-state.sh`'s question and deliberately **not done** for this one — `N/M` reads as progress, and rendering a failed subtask as done would present failure as success. Two mutation controls revert each half of the widening and prove the Shape B fixture goes red without it. It carries **no active-agent field**, and that absence is the honest answer rather than a gap: the spawn emitter that would make such a field truthful was investigated and closed NO-GO, so no spawn event exists in any log, and inferring liveness from another record's recency would be a guess wearing the costume of a measurement. `test-status-line.sh` asserts the absence mechanically — in the rendered output AND as a whole-word source grep for `token_ledger`/`agent_spawn`/`agent_type`/`agent_id` — with a control proving a log line that DOES carry those fields changes nothing, so the claim cannot rot into prose the code does not back. **Time is read from the record, never the filesystem:** the age comes from the log line's own `ts`, parsed try-BSD-then-GNU and validated numeric before any arithmetic, and the field is omitted when neither flavour parses it. `stat` is invoked nowhere — `stat -f %m` is BSD and *succeeds with garbage* on GNU/Linux, so a flavour bug there would put a plausible wrong number on screen rather than failing visibly; a fixture whose `ts` is three days old renders `3d ago` although the file's mtime is seconds old, which is the assertion an mtime implementation cannot pass. **(2) A 9th `/setup` module, `statusline`**, script-backed by `setup-statusline.sh` (`check`/`apply`/`remove`) — the only module that writes the user-scope settings document. Backup-first (collision-proof name; an apply that cannot obtain one ABORTS rather than write unbacked-up), parse-gated (an unparseable document ABORTS with **no write and no backup**), atomic, and byte-compare idempotent. **The pre-existing status line is the point:** the owner's live settings already carried one, and the requirement's original AC protected only keys *unrelated* to `statusLine` — which is precisely the key this module writes, so a naive deep-merge would have silently destroyed it. A status line this plugin did not write is now PRESERVED and REPORTED; replacing it takes an explicit `--replace`, the replaced value is recorded, and `remove` restores it verbatim (and REFUSES to touch a foreign one). **A real defect was caught by its own self-test and is now pinned as a regression case:** writing the restore record inside the jq pipeline — `.statusLine = $sl | .[$k] = (.statusLine // null)` — reads correct and is WRONG, because jq evaluates left to right and the first assignment has already landed, so the "record" became OUR OWN line and `remove` handed the user back a copy of the very thing it was undoing. The prior value is now captured in the shell and passed in with `--argjson`, and a companion case asserts the record survives re-application unchanged. The suite writes only `mktemp -d` fixtures and CHECKSUMS the developer's real settings document before and after the whole run. **(3) The colour legend is now generated output.** `ARCHITECTURE_CONTRACTS.md` §"Color Legend (Status Line)" carried **12** rows while **14** agents declared a `color:` — `review-pr-runner` and `rubric-grader` were missing, and nothing mechanical noticed. `gen-color-legend.sh` derives the table from frontmatter (Agent = `name:` minus the `loomwright:` prefix, Hex = `color:` verbatim, Colour word = the **full published CSS named-colour table** keyed by hex, camelCase-split) between a `<!-- loomwright:color-legend BEGIN … -->` sentinel pair, so a re-stamp keys off the marker and is idempotent; `--write` REFUSES a marker-less document rather than guessing where the table goes. The honest limit is stated rather than papered over: that CSS table IS a map embedded in the generator — the claim is not "no map anywhere" but that the only map is a published standard keyed by hex, so it cannot drift *relative to frontmatter*. Because all 14 live hexes are CSS names the fallback branch is unreachable in production, so **two fixtures pin both halves**: a non-CSS hex must render `—`, and a CSS name from OUTSIDE the 14 in use (`#FA8072` → `Salmon`) must still resolve — without the second, a 14-entry subset passes the first trivially by returning the em-dash for everything it does not know. The check folds into `scripts/check-doc-currency.sh` rather than adding a CI step. **Its mutation control reaches across trees and mutates the CALLER**, because the three suites live under `loomwright/scripts/` (the only path `ci.yml`'s glob auto-runs) while the wiring lives in root `scripts/`: a verbatim copy of the gate with the single `run_legend_check || fail=1` line deleted must WRONGLY PASS a genuinely-drifted fixture, while the unmutated copy FAILS with the specific `LEGEND DRIFT` diagnostic. The control is gated so it cannot be vacuous — the mutant must be non-empty, differ from the original, pass `bash -n`, and still reach its own success line (a syntax error also exits non-zero, so rc alone is no evidence), and a final assertion pins that the real gate still carries the line the control mutates. The 12 surviving rows regenerate **byte-identically**; the Agent column deliberately becomes frontmatter slugs. Vendor-coupling allowances for the two new coupled files were **measured** via `--print-allowances`, never hand-typed, and the manifest's explanatory note is written *without* the literal token so declaring them could not trip the manifest's own allowance — the second-order trap its prior 4→5 self-raise recorded. Counts unchanged: 14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 24 hooks.

**v15.41.0 — the sole writer ate the blank line between sections:** `write-lessons.sh` rebuilds the target `## <category>` section with awk, and on the `sec_start > 0` arm — a SECOND entry into a category that already exists — it reconstructed the section from its `^- \[` lines **alone**. `sec_end` is the line immediately before the next `## ` heading, which in this format is the **blank separator**; it matched neither the entry loop nor the remainder loop (which resumes at `sec_end + 1`, the heading itself), so it was silently dropped on every write into a category that is not last in the file. The asymmetry was real rather than stylistic: the sibling `sec_start == 0` arm a few lines above prints `""` explicitly before a brand-new heading, so the two arms disagreed on the format the file is in. **Deterministic and compounding** — once a separator is gone it never comes back, so the committed `.supervisor/memory/LESSONS.md` lost one per affected category, a little more every `/dreaming` session. The rebuild now **replays the section in place**: `idx[]` remembers each entry's original line number, surviving entries print where they were, the new entry prints immediately after the last pre-existing one, and every non-entry line — the separator, or any prose a future format adds — is carried through. Eviction is untouched (still front-first, still one `evictfile` id per evicted entry), as are the supersede path and a section that is genuinely last. **The gap in the suite was precise, which is why the bug survived:** case 4 ("two categories independent") creates two FRESH categories, so BOTH of its writes take the `sec_start == 0` arm and the buggy arm was never executed — no test in the file mentioned "blank" or "separator". New case **4b** adds a second entry into a non-last category and asserts the separator survives, with the control that one existed beforehand (without it the assertion could pass against a file that never had one) and the counter-case that a genuinely-last section gains **no spurious trailing blank** — the fix has to restore symmetry without over-correcting. An in-file **MUTATION CONTROL** removes the carry-through arm, which reverts the rebuild to exactly the old pure-`entries[]` output, and asserts `(sep2)` goes red; it is gated on non-empty + differs-from-original + `bash -n` + the mutant having actually written its three entries, per the repo's own `[fa32a308]` lesson that a mutant is evidence only if it is valid. Reverting the whole hunk externally flips `(sep2)` and **only** `(sep2)`. 168 → 182 assertions, six of them added across review rounds. Four cover an empty-section arm the fix also introduced (a heading whose sole entry was retracted, then added into again — reachable in production via `retract`, and never reached by the section-9 retract fixtures, which always retract one of TWO entries); reverting the fix flips that arm's separator assertion too, so it was a SECOND instance of the same defect rather than a new edge case. Two more pin eviction and the separator **in combination** — the `<=3` cap was covered only in a single-category store, where the evicting section is always the last one and has no following heading to lose. Reverting the fix flips the separator half of that pair and leaves the eviction half green, which is what makes it a control rather than a restatement. **The already-damaged store is repaired in this release, whitespace-only:** LESSONS.md is a sole-writer store whose provenance index keys on a content hash, so a hand-edited entry line would leave `read-lessons.sh` silently dropping it — blank lines are not entries, so restoring only the missing separators is safe. Three sections had lost theirs (before `## review-process`, `## verification` and `## jq-safety`); `diff -B` against the pre-repair file is empty, the `^- \[` lines are byte-identical, and all 13 entries verifiably still read back. The repair is a re-runnable whitespace pass rather than a hand-edit, which is what let it be re-applied unchanged when the store moved under it mid-change. Counts unchanged: 14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 24 hooks.

**v15.40.0 — the harvester proposed a rule the store already had:** `harvest-conventions.sh` prints `rules store (read for context, NEVER written)` in its own report, and it did read it — for context. It never compared its **proposals** against it. On a real `/dreaming` run over this repo, proposal 1 of the batch (theme `restated-count-version`, 36 motivating findings) restated a rule `.agent/rules/process.json` has carried since 2026-08-03. `/dreaming` is the batch's **only** consumer and would have offered the duplicate for per-item Accept; accepting it puts two differently-worded copies of ONE convention in ONE file — precisely what the `process` cross-surface-sync rule prohibits. It was caught **by hand**. Nothing mechanical caught it. **Exact-string matching could not have caught it either**, and that is the design constraint: the live rule says "A count or version claim lives in exactly ONE authoritative machine-readable place …" and the proposal said "A count or version number is claimed in exactly one authoritative machine-readable place …" — one claim, two wordings, no shared sentence. So the new **store dedupe pass** reuses the signal the harvester already trusts for its project-wide `pw=` bucket test: the share of the PROPOSED statement's distinctive terms (≥5 chars, de-stopworded) that already appear in a live rule's statement, at a **60%** floor. Both call sites now go through **one** `term_overlap` implementation, because the claim "same approach as pw=" is only true if there is nothing to diverge — and the pw refactor is verified byte-identical on the real corpus (every bucket, every percentage unchanged). The direction is asymmetric on purpose — *is this proposal's claim already made?*, not *are these texts similar* — since a stored rule may be far longer. **The threshold is measured, and the gap is the argument, not the number:** on the live store the true duplicate scored 63% while the four other emitted statements scored 17/16/8/0% against the same rule; `--dup-pct 101` re-derives every figure without deferring anything. A match is reported as an **already-covered DEFERRAL naming the covering rule's id, its file and the overlap**, exactly as a corroborating corpus entry is reported rather than silently dropped, and it **spends no cap slot** — the cap bounds what a human reads, and a proposal never offered cost the human nothing. Every EMITTED proposal prints the nearest live rule it did *not* match, so the floor is falsifiable from the report in both directions without opening the store. **The deferred theme's findings stay in every denominator**, which is the half that is easy to get self-flatteringly wrong: `coverage` still measures against all `convention_mismatch` findings, so a deferral **lowers** proposed-rule coverage instead of shrinking the number it is measured against; the theme is itemised in the unmapped remainder with `already covered` as its stated reason (not the cap, not the support floor — the itemisation still sums to its own headline); and new `already covered by the live store` and **COMBINED convention coverage** figures show what the store already answers. Two downstream branches had to move with it: the batch listing is now printed whenever it holds anything at all (an all-covered run emits 0 rules, and the deferral blocks it had just written were being skipped by a `cat` sitting behind an `$EMITTED` test, under a diagnostic saying "named above"), and the empty-batch diagnostic gained a third **measured** cause so an all-covered batch is not blamed on thin evidence. `/dreaming` is told to relay deferrals as context and offer only the numbered proposals. **Honest limits, in the script's own header:** the check is LEXICAL, not semantic — it can miss a duplicate written in wholly different vocabulary, exactly as the harvester did before this pass — and it is advisory, never a gate; an unparseable rule file is fail-safe but **NAMED**, with the dedupe reported UNVERIFIED against it rather than silently clean; and `docs/HARVEST_DRYRUN_SAMPLE.md` is a pinned transcript that PREDATES this pass, so it now carries a note saying what a re-run prints instead of being regenerated against a ledger that has since grown. **97 → 128 assertions, all three mechanisms mutation-verified:** removing the dedupe decision re-proposes the duplicate; crediting a deferred theme's findings to the proposed batch flatters coverage to 10/10 (100%) for a batch that proposed one rule over four findings; and making the already-covered CONTINUATION line unreachable loses the second cause from a combined empty batch. **That third one is a review finding, kept as one:** the continuation line was reachable and correct and had **never been executed** — every rule-seeding fixture ran at `--cap 5` (never exhausted) and the only `--cap 0` fixture seeded no rule, so the two counters were never both non-zero in the same run. Reasoned about, not run, which is this file's most-repeated defect class. The fixture's live rule is a **paraphrase of the harvester's own committed statement, extracted from the script rather than hand-typed**, with a control proving the extractor found it and a second control proving neither statement contains the other — an exact-string dedupe cannot pass the section. Counts unchanged: 14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 24 hooks.

**v15.40.0 also contains the System Twin dark-read-path fix (PR #163), which merged into this release's tree before the version bump and is narrated here rather than under a version of its own — it shipped in v15.40.0, and claiming a separate number for work already released would be the drift this changelog exists to prevent.** For two weeks `read-system-contract.sh` returned nothing while `.supervisor/twin/contracts/` held 21 substantive contracts, so Phase 4.5 twin enrichment, the Phase 4.5 delta line and `/dreaming`'s contract-drift input all recorded `skipped` — and every gate stayed green. **Root cause, proven rather than inferred:** on 2026-08-12 a tree-wide rebrand `sed` (`ai-agent-manager-plugin` → `loomwright`) swept the working tree and hit the twin store. `.supervisor/twin/` is gitignored, so the edit was invisible to CI and to review; it never went through the sole writer, so no provenance was appended. Two independent measurements pin it: reversing the substitution and re-hashing reproduces a ledger `content_hash` for **21 of 21** files (control — the raw bodies match **0 of 21**, which is what makes the reversal non-trivial), and all 21 share **one mtime to the second**, a single sweep rather than 21 writer runs. Three candidate causes are **ruled out**: the chain is intact, the hashes are computed identically on both sides, and the writer's provenance path never broke — its commit is `mv "$prov_tmp" "$PROV" && mv "$c_tmp" "$CONTRACT"`, provenance FIRST and `&&`-joined, so a contract file cannot land without its ledger entry. **The read gate is UNCHANGED and deliberately so** (red-team W1: *provenance alone is theater*).

**The half that mattered more was recoverability, not darkness.** The dedup guard short-circuited on hash equality alone, so re-running the builder re-derived the same on-disk body, hit `unchanged — skipping`, and exited 0 having appended nothing — **forever**. The sole writer had become structurally incapable of restoring provenance for its own store, leaving hand-editing the ledger — forging exactly the evidence the gate demands — as the only way out. The guard now requires the stored body to be **ledger-TRUSTED**, and *consults* the read gate rather than re-implementing the chain walk (trust must mean "chain-valid `add`", not "hash present somewhere"); the coupling fails safe — a format drift makes the writer append a redundant-but-valid entry, never weaken the gate. **A 100% drop was indistinguishable from an empty store, which is why it stayed silent:** the drops *were* recorded, in `.supervisor/logs/twin.log`, a file no consumer reads and no human opens unprompted. The reader now emits `twin_store_status: empty|healthy|degraded|dark` plus a loud `!!` block **on stdout**; `session-resume.sh` surfaces dark/degraded every resume; Phase 4.5 records `unverified`, not `skipped`. The legacy `(no verified System Twin contracts)` sentinel is kept **verbatim** because `self-heal-advisory` pins that exact string. New **`loomwright/scripts/reprovenance-twin-contracts.sh`** is the sanctioned recovery path: **report-only by default**, `--confirm` routes every repair **through the sole writer** and **re-verifies through the read gate** rather than trusting the writer's exit code. It is human-gated because re-provenancing blesses bytes; on a genuinely poisoned store, confirming blind would launder the poison. It writes **in place under the filename stem** — 19 of the 21 live contracts have pre-rebrand filenames and post-rebrand body ids, and writing under the logical id would create a second file and orphan the old one; the report names every divergence rather than deciding it silently.

**The instructive part is the review, and it is not flattering.** Five rounds; the executable scripts drew **zero** functional findings after round 1, while the agent-followed pseudocode in `self-heal-advisory/SKILL.md` drew one **every round** — and all four were *the same defect*, each scope narrower: the status sampled inside the per-subsystem loop (order-dependent); then only the whole-store `dark` case tested, missing the commoner *degraded* store whose one touched subsystem is the dropped one; then the withheld test nested under `if not checked`, so a PR touching a verified subsystem **and** a withheld one reported **`pass`** over an invisible dropped contract. Each time the wrong reasoning was written into a confident explanatory comment, and each time mutation controls were structurally incapable of catching it — they prove the mechanisms you thought of are load-bearing and say nothing about the ones you did not write. **The asymmetry is the finding:** prompt logic here carries the weight of code and had nothing holding it to account. A new structural invariant over the decision rule is the first, and it states its own limit. A round-4 item disputing the test counts was **measured, not argued**: a clean `origin/main` checkout runs 74 and 68, exactly as claimed; the higher figure counted `ok`/`no` **call sites**, and the dominant `<test> && ok … || no …` idiom is two sites per execution (measured: 175 sites, 118 executed). **74 → 123 assertions.** **Honest limit, in the scripts' own headers:** this repairs and reports, it does not **prevent** — a future sweep darkens the store again; the difference is that it now shows on the next read and the next resume instead of after two weeks. The live store was repaired under `--confirm` and verified through the *unmodified* reader: 21/21 emitted, bodies byte-identical to the pre-repair backup, `twin-graph.sh` back to 105 dependency edges from zero.

**v15.39.0 — a lifecycle step that only a living agent could take, and the probe that lied about the wreckage:** the brief lifecycle move `in-progress/` → `done/` is completion-tail **step 2**, and the originating-requirement stamp is **step 2.5** — both PROMPT-INSTRUCTED steps in `skills/self-heal-advisory/SKILL.md`, executed by the agent, not by code. On 2026-08-30 the Phase 4.5 reviewer failed **4×** with a server error; the tail was never reached; PR #160 merged and shipped while its brief sat in `in-progress/` forever. **No prompt change can fix that** — you cannot make an instruction atomic against the agent dying before it is read — so this release does not pretend to harden the tail. It adds the reconciliation that was missing — and this is the **second** such reconciler, not the first. `stamp-requirement-status.sh` has closed out step 2.5 since 2026-08-04 by keying on a byproduct (a brief landing in `jobs/done/`), on the same argument. But it reads `jobs/done/` **exclusively**, so a stranded brief is invisible to it: the strand does not merely skip step 2, it **structurally blocks the existing step-2.5 reconciler from firing at all**. Repairing the move unblocks it; the two compose without overlap (this one never stamps a requirement, that one never moves a brief) and both run from the same SessionStart. New **`loomwright/scripts/reconcile-jobs.sh`** classifies every in-progress brief `stranded_merged` / `stranded_closed` / `unknown` and, with `--repair`, finishes the move. It is **OFFLINE BY CONSTRUCTION** — it never calls the forge CLI, because the SessionStart hook consumes it on every resume — so evidence comes from an automate run file that already recorded a reconciled merge, or from a requirement already stamped done. **`unknown` is never repaired and never guessed at**: a stranded brief carries no PR URL (the `## Outcome` block that would carry one is precisely what did not get written), and for a plain `/supervisor` run there may be no evidence at all. The `## Outcome` it writes says which fields it could not recover rather than inventing them. **The probe stops asserting.** `session-resume.sh` Section 1 used to print every in-progress file under "Supervisor was mid-run" and point at `/supervisor --continue` — for merged work that is a false signal recommending a wrong action, re-emitted every session until a human intervened. It now reports **Stranded** (with the repair command) separately from **UNVERIFIED**, and the resume hint is suppressed unless something is genuinely unverified. Fail-safe: an absent reconciler falls back to a neutral listing, never to the old claim. **A third defect, found while scoping — real but narrower than it first looked:** completion-tail step 2.5 stamped `## Status` with the value in a `- **Status:**` bullet, while `automate-helpers.sh`'s `is_done()` reads **only** `^## Status:[[:space:]]*done` — so a *successfully* closed-out requirement stayed re-enqueueable by `resolve-folder` / `resume-glob` **forever**. It had never fired: zero requirements carried the close-out sentinel, meaning step 2.5 had never once completed here — which is also why it was invisible. **Scope, stated precisely:** this affects the PROMPT path only. The mechanized `stamp-requirement-status.sh` writes `## Status: brief-shipped` — deliberately never `done`, because a landed brief proves the work ran, not that every acceptance criterion was met — and `is_done()` correctly does not treat that as terminal. A new control (`11f`) pins exactly that, so widening the matcher cannot swallow the intermediate state. The value moves onto the heading (with the reason recorded inline so it cannot be demoted back), and `is_done()` gains the `done_with_escalation` arm — `\b` does not match after `done` when `_` follows, so escalated-but-shipped work was silently re-pickable too. **Two of this release's own tests were vacuous and are recorded rather than quietly fixed:** the reconciler-absent case tried to hide an absolutely-pathed script with `PATH`, so it never reached the fallback arm it claimed to test; and the contract-stamp fixtures were **hand-typed in the new format**, so they survived reverting the code — the same hand-typed-value-inside-the-drift-gate defect PR #160 found, reproduced one release later. The fixtures are now **extracted from the skill file**, with a control asserting the extractor found ≥2 (a gate that finds nothing must not pass). **38 assertions; all nine mechanisms mutation-verified** — reverting `is_done`, the contract stamp, Section 1, the repair evidence gate, and the conditional resume hint each fails exactly its own cases and nothing else. **Review round (PR #161), all three findings real and verified before acting:** `repair()` stamped `completed` for BOTH terminal values, flattening a `done_with_escalation` close-out into a clean one — while the requirement file it had *just read to classify the brief* still held the answer. That contradicted this script's own stated principle, so it now mirrors the contract's `completed_with_escalation` vocabulary plus a note saying the heal reason and remaining-issue count are not recoverable; `stranded_merged` keeps `completed`, because a merged PR proves the work shipped and says nothing about the heal decision. **The first fix for it was itself broken and caught by executing it:** BSD `sed` (macOS) does not support `\\|` alternation in a basic regex, so the helper returned EMPTY and silently flattened every escalated close-out anyway — same family as the repo's recorded `stat -f`/`-c` flavour trap. Replaced with two portable greps and pinned by cases `3b`/`3c`/`3c2` plus control `3d` (a plain `done` must stay `completed`), both arms mutation-verified. Also: the `ARCHITECTURE_CONTRACTS.md` State Ownership table now names `reconcile-jobs.sh --repair` as the second, evidence-gated mover of `in-progress/` → `done/` (and records that `stamp-requirement-status.sh` reads `done/` exclusively, which is why a strand blocks it), the `HOOKS.md` SessionStart row notes the new `--porcelain` call, and a superseded `is_done` comment asserting the old single-value contract is removed. **Review round 2** found the first escalation fix covered only ONE of the two repairable arms: `classify()` consults the automate run file BEFORE `is_done()`, so a brief can be `stranded_merged` while its requirement is *also* stamped `done_with_escalation` (step 2.5 can stamp without step 2 having moved — the skill says so explicitly), and gating the check on `state` re-created the exact failure it was added to remove. The requirement is now consulted for both arms, with only the message differing. Round 2 also found the repair ORDER could wedge a brief permanently — it rewrote the source in place and then moved it, so a failed move left the brief carrying an `## Outcome` that the guard then refused **forever**. Fixed at the root rather than documented: the staged copy lands in `done/` first and the original is dropped only after, so the worst case is a reported duplicate instead of a wedge. That reordering is only observable when the move fails, so case `8c` forces it (unwritable `done/`) and `8c2` proves the brief still repairs once the obstruction clears — without them the change would have been a claim no check backs. 38 assertions. **Honest limits:** the completion tail is UNCHANGED, so strands still happen — this repairs them, it does not prevent them; and `unknown` means UNVERIFIED, not clean. Counts unchanged: 14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 24 hooks.

**v15.38.0 — vendor-neutrality becomes a ratchet, and the ratchet keeps failing its own standard:** portability was asserted in a memory and a requirement doc and **no check enforced it**, so the core kept absorbing harness references — prompt-layer `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` refs up **31%** between v15.13.0 and 2026-08-22, and `loomwright/scripts` portability down **82% → 70%**. New gate **`scripts/check-vendor-coupling.sh`** reads a committed **`loomwright/docs/vendor-coupling-manifest.json`** — every vendor token, class glob and per-path allowance lives there, the gate hard-codes none — classifies each tracked path CORE / ADAPTER / COUPLED, and fails **CLOSED** when a count exceeds its allowance. Breach is `actual > declared`; `actual < declared` **passes**, because debt paid down must never be a CI failure. Raising an allowance is legal only in the PR that spends it. **`loomwright/scripts/resolve-loomwright-root.sh`** is the vendor-neutral `LOOMWRIGHT_ROOT` seam (harness var → explicit neutral override → location-derived dev checkout), deliberately ADAPTER-exempt because its whole job is to name the harness ONCE so nothing else has to; **migrating the existing call sites is explicitly not in scope** — this stops the growth, the inventory pays the debt down. **A stated assumption, refuted:** the requirement assumed the refs were "dominated by legitimately-adapter surfaces" and flagged it unverified — measured, adapter-ish is **47%**, core-ish **50%**. Not dominant; recorded in the manifest rather than planned around. **The instructive part is the review, not the feature.** Six rounds found six defects, and *five were the gate failing its own standard*: an adapter glob whose note claimed "the two manifests" while it also swept in a prose README; a **hand-typed "16 files"** inside the count-drift gate (a single-token sweep quoted against a four-token gate — the real figure is 35); `--print-allowances`, the mechanism every allowance is regenerated by, proved only by reading; non-integer validation that ran only on files which *already* had references, so garbage on a clean path passed silently; and a `count_mode` field nothing read, where `"lines-or-bananas"` passed. Each was reproduced before being fixed and is now backed by an executed assertion — **51 → 71** — every new case mutation-verified, including the controls (`19b`, `20c`) that stop a case being satisfiable by a gate that simply rejects everything. **Honest limits, stated in the gate's own header:** it counts LITERAL occurrences, so a reference assembled at runtime is invisible — a green run means "no new *literal* coupling"; root prose, the sibling plugins and `.github/` are **unscanned, not certified clean**; and the resolver's exhausted-resolution branch is **untested by design**, with the note carrying its own deletion condition so it cannot become permanent cover. Counts unchanged: 14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 24 hooks.

**v15.37.0 — a template nothing ever parsed, and the gate that checked its field names instead:** `EXECUTE_CHECKPOINT` has two shapes, and only one was ever written as a template an agent can copy. The five adjudication fields (`adjudication_required`, `adjudication_kind`, `missing_outputs`, `colliding_lanes`, `adjudication_options`) lived in prose and in `RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` but in neither emit block, so an adjudication pause emitted from the base template would be **rejected by its own SubagentStop hook**. The fix is deliberately not to paste the five fields into the budget/error template — `validate-execute-result.py` rule 6b rejects any of them appearing without `adjudication_required: true` — so a third labelled template is added in both kinds, matching rule 6a's evidence-array requirement: `missing_outputs` for `requires_gap`, `colliding_lanes` for `lane_collision`. **Then the re-landed fix turned out to be unparseable itself.** Both new templates wrapped an `adjudication_options` flow sequence onto a second line and spanned a quoted `reason` scalar across three, and `result_block_parser.py` cannot read either shape — an agent copying one verbatim was still rejected, at the PARSE step, before rule 6 was ever consulted. Measured in both directions: the wrapped forms return `unexpected end of flow collection` / `unterminated quoted scalar`, the same content unwrapped returns `ok`. `adjudication_options` becomes a block sequence in both templates and the lane-collision `reason` is emitted on one line — line structure only, option strings and reason text unchanged — with a note above them stating the constraint so the next editor does not re-wrap them for readability. **The root cause, not just the instance:** `parity-emit-block` checks that required field NAMES appear in a template, which is exactly why this passed every gate. Nothing checked that a template PARSES. New eval-corpus task **`emit-block-parses`** does, reusing `check-contract-parity.sh`'s MANIFEST as the same single source of field truth, and its oracle is deliberately parse-level only — a template's `{placeholder}` values cannot satisfy semantic rules, and asserting them would make the task untestable rather than stronger. Its two placeholder substitutions never add or remove a line break, so the check cannot normalize away the thing it is checking. `test-emit-block-parses.sh` **executes** the mutation table rather than leaving it as prose in `spec.md` — a documented mutation table nothing runs is a claim no check backs — including the control that a re-wrapped flow sequence goes RED and the one that proves a no-locatable-template case fails loudly instead of passing vacuously. Corpus is now 7 tasks; 17 emit templates across 7 blocks parse. **Also in this release:** the last two un-annotated copies of `knowledge_sources_used` (the compact `SUPERVISOR_RESULT` yaml comment and the `supervisor.md` template — the fourth and fifth restatements of a field whose three detailed bullets already carried the retirement note) are annotated in the same one-line style, and the Brain Context index row's token estimate is re-measured after its 311→120-line shrink; the `self-heal-advisory` row was **deliberately left alone** because calibration shows the whole column runs 3–10x below bytes/4, so correcting one row to a different scale would mislead — a column-wide re-measure is its own follow-up. **A provenance note worth keeping:** the adjudication fix merged once already, as PR #154 on 2026-08-20, and was destroyed when `main` was force-pushed backwards later that day. The re-land is byte-identical to the merged version and was re-verified against current `main` rather than carried over on trust. Counts unchanged: 14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 24 hooks.

**v15.36.0 — the graphify/bridge tier is retired, and the `/setup` twin module goes with it:** measured 2026-08-06 and re-verified at execution: `brain_context` appears **0 times** across all recorded session logs — not one run ever consumed the tier — `graphify-out/graph.json` was built once (2026-06-22, gitignored) and never rebuilt, `.supervisor/bridge/bridge.json` survived as a **255-commit-stale orphan** that `read-bridge.sh` emitted nothing against, and the 71% Step-4 gate score that justified wiring it has **no control arm** (every strict hit is anchored by a shared file a plain path join would also have found). The tier fell into exactly the staleness trap its own north-star doc warned about — an expensive deliberate rebuild, gitignored, degrading silently — so this release removes it at every seam (`brain-context`'s ladder now reads **memos → repo-map → nothing**; the self-heal-advisory area-knowledge read, Launch Pad's consults, Code Reviewer's brain-consult bullet + reachable-tag passage, and context-setup's graph detection are all gone — each reader was already a self-gating no-op with the graph absent, so every caller's success path is byte-identical) and deletes the bridge/setup scripts (`build-bridge.sh`/`.py`, `read-bridge.sh`, `setup-twin.sh` + their tests). **User-facing removal, stated prominently: the `/setup` twin module (cold-start code graph + bridge + starter-CLAUDE.md bootstrap) is REMOVED** — its sole purpose was bootstrapping the retired tier — **and the external-brain path (`LOOMWRIGHT_BRAIN_ROOT` detection + wiki query order) retires with the rung**: the 0-uses measurement covers the whole rung, and the wiki path had no separate consumer and no separate measurement. A measured correction against the source requirement: **`twin-graph.sh` + `test-twin-graph.sh` are RETAINED, byte-untouched** — they serve the live System Twin **contract store** (`.supervisor/twin/`), not the graphify tier the requirement's disposition list lumped them into. D9's blanket "flag-only, never auto-delete" rule is **split by regenerability** — Derived artifacts (rebuildable from the repo at a known cost) → auto-delete freely; Distilled stores (human-gated judgment) → never — amended in `docs/SPIKES/FINAL_STATE_GOAL.md` with the original sentence quoted and the amendment dated; the distilled-store `flag-only` surfaces in `build-insights.sh`/`test-insights.sh` were traced and correctly left standing (under the split they are the "never" row and remain true). The reversal condition is citable from a fresh clone: the validation harness is committed **verbatim as a 3-file closure** (`validate.py` + `validate_gen.py` + `reposcan_multi.py`, plus a README carrying provenance and the external deps) under `docs/SPIKES/code-graph-harness/` — any revival, graph or external brain, must be a **cheap incremental refresh** and must pass this harness — and the spike evidence docs are banner-marked RETIRED, never deleted. Counts unchanged: 14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 24 hooks.

**v15.35.0 — the plugin had been recording convention violations for months and never converting them into conventions:** the ledger holds 225 recorded findings (measured at the sha `docs/RULES_BASELINE.md` pins); **107 of them (48%) are `convention_mismatch`**, the single largest class, and **60 of the 95 self-heal misses (63%)** are that same class — the thing review is worst at catching. Meanwhile `.agent/rules/` held **one** rule. This release closes the loop: `/dreaming` becomes the distiller that turns accumulated findings into proposed conventions, `/setup` seeds a cold-start repo that has accumulated nothing, and a committed baseline makes the claim falsifiable. Counts are unchanged: no new agent, command, skill or hook — the `/setup` module is a module, not a command file.

**A new `scripts/harvest-conventions.sh` triages, and it is a dry run by default.** It reads BOTH intake sources — `convention_mismatch` entries in the postmortem ledger AND the existing `.claude/agent-memory/**/*.md` corpus (plus any pending `.supervisor/agent-memory-proposals/`) — and assigns every candidate to **exactly one** of three destinations (`agent-memory` / `rules` / `project-memory`) with the assigning reason recorded per candidate. Dry-run is the DEFAULT and the harvester has **no write mode at all**; every proposed rule is authored through the store's confirm-gated sole writer `add-rule.sh` with stdin **detached** (`< /dev/null`), because a no-`--confirm` call is *not* a dry run on a TTY — that writer's `[ -t 0 ] && [ -t 1 ]` branch prompts and writes on `y`, which is exactly the case `/dreaming` runs in. Each batch reports three computed numbers — **coverage**, **dedupe rate**, **scope fidelity** — every rule names the finding ids that motivated it, and a batch approaching one rule per finding is reported as a **failure of distillation** rather than emitted as if it had distilled anything. `applies_to` is derived from the motivating findings' `changed_paths`; a repo-wide proposal requires a stated justification. `check` stays **`null`** — the harvester never synthesises shell into a rule, rules stay DATA, and **no new gate is added**. The real dry run over the real corpus is committed as `docs/HARVEST_DRYRUN_SAMPLE.md` so the batch can be judged by reading rather than by counting.

**`/dreaming` gains its first remote-affecting action — and says so at the contract line.** Accepted rules are delivered as a **pull request**, not an in-place write, and the "read-only until you Accept" callout is amended where the contract lives instead of being qualified three sections down. Reaching the remote takes **two distinct per-item gates**: the **Accept** that authorises a rule's *content*, and a separate **Pre-push confirmation** that authorises the *push*. Neither implies the other; if nothing clears both, **no branch is created at all**. The commit carries `.agent/rules/*.json` and nothing else — staged by path, never `git add -A` — and delivery is refused outright if that store already has uncommitted changes. **A rules-only commit is not a rules-only PR**, so a fourth pre-flight resolves the base branch *before* branching and aborts unless HEAD is already contained in `origin/<base>` — a PR is the branch-versus-base diff, not the commit, and neither human gate reads that diff (the repo's own stale-branch class, the one Supervisor Phase 1.5 exists for). A batch the harvester marked `DISTILLATION FAILURE` is **not offered at all** rather than offered with the verdict printed above it. `/dreaming` opens the PR and stops; it **never merges**, and the repo-wide single-executor grep still resolves to the same five sanctioned surfaces. The same change wires the pending `.supervisor/agent-memory-proposals/` queue into per-item promotion (**PROMOTE PENDING PROPOSAL**), and retires the standing "NOT wired here yet" disclaimer at the three surfaces it was found on: `dreaming.md`, `write-agent-memory.sh`'s own header, and `AGENT_GUIDELINES.md` §"Agent memory write permission" — the shared agent contract the six `memory: project` prompts point at, and the most authoritative of the three. Each was found by a separate pass, the second and third only after a check scoped to the previous one reported clean. **The sweep is not claimed to be exhaustive** — no gate covers workflow prose (`check-doc-currency.sh` scans these files for version/count claims only), and asserting completeness is precisely what made the last survivor findable.

**A repo with no history gets seeded, and seeded is not learned.** A ninth `/setup` module (`rules`, engine `scripts/seed-rules.sh`) gives a cold-start repo a non-empty store by seeding conventions that are **portable** — true of any repo, in any language, with any layout. A fresh repo has accumulated nothing, so the distillation path yields an empty batch and the store would stay cold forever. The seeded/learned distinction is **data, not prose**: it rides in `provenance.source` (`setup:rules-seed` vs `dreaming:<session_id>`), because the rule object's schema is frozen at seven always-present members plus the optional `supersedes`, and `add-rule.sh` rejects unknown arguments outright — a labelling member would have been a create-shaped path with no writer behind it. Every seed is repo-wide with a stated justification (a path glob is a claim about a directory layout, and no layout is universal) and `check: null`. The module is registered in **both** places the setup contract requires, in the same change. **Because the store is meant to be curated, presence is keyed on the seeded stamp rather than the statement text**: a seed whose wording a user rewrites is recognised as curated, not missing, so a re-run reports it already seeded and writes nothing. (Keying on the exact statement made the skip strictly narrower than the near-identical duplicate refusal it exists to avoid — one edited word made a seed look absent, the writer then refused it as a near-duplicate, and the module reported a hard failure on **every** subsequent run of a correctly-configured repo.) The matching honest limit is stated rather than papered over: **retract is not a permanent opt-out** — it deletes the object, so nothing carries the stamp and a later run re-offers that seed. Recording a refusal would need a store the frozen schema has no room for, so editing is the durable curation action and retracting is not.

**And the measurement that can falsify all of it.** `measure-heal-signal.sh` gains a **`--distribution`** mode: per-class findings and misses with their shares, counted over **raw** ledger records rather than deduped PRs — a deliberately different view from the same script's confusion matrix, which dedups per PR because it answers a per-PR question. It prints and stops, writing nothing at all: no artifact, no trend line, not even its `--out` directory. The wrapper's exit contract is **unchanged and asserted as such** — exit 0 on missing `python3`, missing engine and unknown arg; the engine's own code propagated otherwise — with every case tested twice, once with the new flag and once without, because a contract that holds on only the new path is not the contract. The baseline itself lands in **`docs/RULES_BASELINE.md`**: a committed plain doc, deliberately **not** routed through a sole writer, since `write-project-memory.sh` refuses to run from a worktree, needs a human for `--confirm`, and FIFO-evicts at 200 lines — three independent reasons the writer route was unexecutable, recorded rather than quietly worked around. The record **states its own limits in itself**: 83 of 84 records carry `agent_generated_guess: true`, N is small and its unit is not independent, and there is **no control arm**. So a rising `convention_mismatch` share after rules land is a signal to investigate whether the rules are being injected and read at all — **never proof they failed**, and a falling share is not proof they worked. Re-measurement is append-only, after N further PRs, and is an operator step: a PR cannot measure its own effect from inside itself.

**v15.34.0 — a writer may not vouch for itself, and a committed store may not be written without a human:** v15.33.0 wired five shared checks into six sole writers. Two of them were not actually load-bearing, and this release closes both — plus the gating asymmetry that made "validated" read as "gated" when for three writers it was not. Counts are unchanged: no new agent, command, skill or hook.

**`add-orientation.sh` was validating the memo it stamps, not the memo it was given.** The entry it handed the validator was the COMPOSED file — header + summary + body — and the header is one the writer writes itself, carrying `head_sha: <short HEAD sha>`. A 7–40 char hex token behind a `sha`/`head_sha`/`commit` label IS a citation to `validate_provenance`, so **every memo vouched for itself** and the provenance check could not refuse anything through this writer. Measured, not inferred: a memo whose summary and body cited nothing at all was written with rc 0. The validated entry is now **body + summary only**; the self-stamped header is never part of it. The fix is entirely writer-side — `validate-entry.sh`'s five checks are untouched, because it is shared by six writers and must not learn one writer's file format. A new `--source` flag supplies provenance the legitimate way, and `/dreaming`'s promotion path passes `dreaming:<session_id>`, the same value it already passes to `write-lessons.sh`.

**This knowingly reverses the reasoning of the commit three before it, and the reversal is the point.** That commit made a *labelled* `head_sha:` count as a citation after an earlier fix — which demanded a hex letter so a bare magnitude could not masquerade as a sha — refused real memos whenever an abbreviated sha happened to be all digits (2.6% of this repo's commits, roughly one in thirty). Its own stated objection to `--source "head_sha:$sha"` was that it would let the writer vouch for itself *outside the artifact a reader can see*; keeping the header inside the validated entry is the same self-vouching one layer in. The labelled-sha acceptance in the validator is **unchanged** and still serves every other writer, and any memo that genuinely cites a commit in its prose still writes with no `--source` at all — a fact now pinned by a test in both directions, because a refusal that fires for everything is not a working check.

**Two of the six writers could append to a committed store with no human in the loop, and one review invocation proved it by accident.** `.supervisor/memory/` looks gitignored and is not: `.gitignore` carries a `!.supervisor/memory/` negation, so `LESSONS.md` and `PROJECT_MEMORY.md` are tracked files. `write-lessons.sh` and `write-project-memory.sh` had no `--confirm` gate, so a single non-interactive invocation from the repo root landed a *validated* entry in the repo's history. Both are now gated on every mutating action, in `add-rule.sh`'s existing shape. The rule is stated rather than left to be inferred: **a sole writer whose store is COMMITTED requires `--confirm`; one whose store is gitignored does not**, decided by `git ls-files <store>`. `write-system-contract.sh` keeps no gate — `.supervisor/twin/` is genuinely untracked — and that is now recorded in its own header so nobody "fixes" the asymmetry for symmetry's sake. The load-bearing lesson, written into `AGENT_GUIDELINES.md`: **validation is not a substitute for a confirm gate** — validation decides whether an entry is well-formed, the gate decides whether a human asked for it to land.

**Three things the gate work found that the plan did not contain.** `write-lessons.sh` has a **third** mutating action, `--attest-existing`, which appends to the committed provenance chain — gated too, positioned after the already-chain-trusted no-op check so an idempotent attest reports itself rather than printing a plan for work that would not happen. **A gate placed correctly was still not sufficient:** both writers bootstrap their store *before* the validator, i.e. before any legal gate position, so a "dry-run" still created two committed files on a fresh checkout; the bootstrap is now conditional on the verdict, pinned by an assertion. And **`supersede` cannot be gated per-half** — its retract half commits before its add half, so one gate covers both; a naive per-site gate would let a "no" exit 0 having already tombstoned the target.

**Half of the source requirement was already fixed before it ran, and that is recorded rather than quietly absorbed.** The item's duplicate-check scope, its AC2, and its "not deferrable past 05" argument all rest on a claim that `add-orientation.sh`'s duplicate check only bit above ~90 body tokens. A prior commit had already replaced the comparison store with a derived whole-store corpus; probed on the live writer, an identical body reposted under a second slug at a **12-token** body is refused. The threshold never existed — the check had not been biting late, it had not been biting at all. So AC2 was already satisfied, its demand to delete a `VE_N=120` fixture inflation is void (the inflation remains, with a different and correct rationale already stated in the suite), and **item 05 was never blocked by this work**. The stale premises are written into the source requirement, which lives under the **gitignored** `.supervisor/requirements/` and is therefore not visible to a reader of this repo — which is why the finding is stated in full here, in committed prose, rather than by reference to it.

**v15.33.0 — validate before you write: five shared checks across six sole writers, a real write path for agent memory, and a stated capability contract:** the curated stores only ever grew. The five sole writers appended without looking around first, so a store quietly accumulated entries that had stopped being true — and on the agent-memory side there was no sanctioned write path at all, so every write was hand-pasted, which is how one index rotted to a single pointer over eighteen files and made seventeen of them invisible to the agent that owns them. This release adds one uncounted plain script, **`scripts/validate-entry.sh`**, holding five checks (duplicate, contradiction, provenance, dead reference, cross-repo reference) behind one aggregate entry point, and wires it into the write path of all six sole writers. It adds the sixth writer, **`scripts/write-agent-memory.sh`**, which does not *maintain* a store's `MEMORY.md` but **rebuilds** it from the files on disk on every write — a file can now only be missing from the index if it is also missing from the directory — plus a `.supervisor/agent-memory-proposals/` queue mirroring the shipped orientation-proposal pattern. Counts are unchanged: no new agent, command, skill or hook.

**One helper, sourced defensively, because a broken helper must degrade into the failure mode the design already sanctions.** Five independent copies were rejected (they make "one allowlist, two consumers" drift-prone by construction) and so was a hard dependency (it inverts the writers' fail-safe convention). Each writer's load guard is three clauses, all required: the `source` must exit 0, all five validator functions must be present, and a sentinel version string must match the literal the writer hardcodes. Two clauses would not be enough — bash defines every function **above** a syntax error before aborting the parse, so a truncated helper leaves *some* validators defined and a one-function probe would report "examined and clean" over half a validator. `|| true` is forbidden on that source line: this repo's otherwise-pervasive convention applied there yields exactly the silent unvalidated append the design forbids, and a mutation control proves that replacing the guard with `|| true` turns the test red. Every check refuses on an input it **cannot examine** (unreadable file, absent `jq`, unparseable JSON, an allowlist that resolved to nothing) with a named reason, rather than reporting clean — the examined-vs-could-not-examine split v15.28.0 was built to close.

**The cross-repo check's direction was INVERTED in the source requirement, and a literal implementation would have gone fully green while behaving backwards.** The requirement said to derive the check "from the allowlist's known foreign slugs", which presumes the allowlist is a deny list of other people's repos. It is not: it is *this* repo's own permitted slugs, defaulting to the current git remote, and the shipped ledger filter **keeps** records whose repo is in it. Implemented as written, the check would have refused citations of this repo and passed genuinely foreign ones — worse than a no-op, because every acceptance criterion would still have been satisfiable. Caught at Plan Review and corrected before any code was written; the direction is now stated in the helper's own header next to the membership test, and a mutation control inverting the test is required to turn the own-repo case red. A second consequence is pinned in the tests: no fixture may add a foreign slug to the **live** allowlist, because that would make `setup-memory.sh apply` judge foreign ledger records clean and emit the negation that un-ignores the churn ledger — publishing another repo's analysis from this public repo. Fixture allowlists therefore move through the process-global env var or a `--root` fixture repo, and a negative control asserts the publication gate still withholds the negation on a foreign record.

**The first wired implementation falsely refused 12 of 21 live curated entries — 57%, every one a false positive.** Replaying the real `LESSONS.md` + `PROJECT_MEMORY.md` corpus through the validator was the only thing that surfaced it; the hand-written fixtures were all green, because fixtures are written by the same person who wrote the checks and inherit their blind spots. The refusals were: a dead-reference check that could not resolve a script living under `loomwright/scripts/` rather than the repo root; glob-shaped and tilde-shaped paths (`test-*.sh`, `~/.claude/settings.json`) treated as dead references when they are *unresolvable by shape* and must skip rather than refuse; and six ordinary English prose pairs (`yaml/json`, `dev/ci`, `budget/zone`, `user/pr-text`, `shell/wget`, `worker_result/code_review_result`) read as `owner/repo` slugs. The corpus replay now ships as a permanent regression test, with a seeded-synthetic control proving the replay harness itself discriminates and an assertion that an empty corpus is a failure rather than a pass. For the two over-recognising checks the bias is stated in the code rather than left implicit: **a false refusal is worse than a miss**, because it blocks a legitimate write and the check's whole value is that a human trusts its verdict.

**A residual bound, stated rather than papered over: a bare all-lowercase `word/word` whose owner is not a known owner is a deliberate miss.** `otherco/othersvc` standing alone in prose is shape-identical to `budget/zone`, and there is nothing in the text to separate them — refusing that shape is precisely what refused six live entries above. The check therefore recognises a slug only when it is *marked*: by a `github.com` URL, a `#123`/`@sha`/`.git` citation, a neighbouring cue word, a known owner, or slug-only structure such as a hyphen, digit or CamelCase — plus the identifier-shaped `NAME #123` form. A paired test pins both halves for the same reason: `otherco/othersvc` unmarked **passes**, `budget/zone` **passes**, and the marked forms of the former are refused. The refusal message itself carries the bound, so a reader of a real refusal is told what the check does not see. **The cross-repo check is not complete coverage and is not described as such anywhere** — a clean verdict is not proof that no cross-repo reference is present.

**TWO OF THE FIVE CHECKS NO LONGER REFUSE, AND THIS CONTRADICTS SCOPE 2 OF THE SOURCE REQUIREMENT** ("a validation failure is a refusal to write"). It is an owner decision taken on measurement, and it is recorded here in the form that lets a later reader disagree with it rather than in the form that makes it sound inevitable. **`dead-reference` and `cross-repo` are now ADVISORY: they report on stderr and always return 0. `duplicate`, `contradiction` and `provenance` are unchanged and still refuse.** The reason is that six consecutive review rounds found false refusals and **every single one was in those same two checks** — the other three have never produced one. That is structural, not luck: the three that block key on input whose shape the writer controls, while the two that scan free English prose for file paths and repository names are looking at a medium where a citation and an ordinary sentence are frequently the same characters. The rounds, in order: 12 of 21 live curated entries refused (57%, every one a false positive); then prose pairs (`YAML/JSON`, `ground-truth/conformance`, `Count/version`), numeric ratios (`117/117`), a git branch name (`fix/v14.23.1-combined`), in-repo directory paths (`docs/Spikes`) and prose shorthand (`CHANGELOG/CLAUDE.md`), each of which refused a legitimate write and each of which was fixed by a narrowing that did not end the class. **The last round is the one that settled it: the fix for "gitignored but real files are unresolvable" made the verdict MACHINE-DEPENDENT.** Unioning an on-disk `find` into the path index was green on the author's working tree, where `.supervisor/state.md` exists, and red on every clean checkout, where it does not — measured with a `git archive HEAD` extraction at **231 passed / 0 failed locally versus 229 / 1 clean, 3 of 28 live curated entries falsely refused** — and the flagship corpus-replay guard could not see it because it replayed `$REPO_ROOT`, the one environment where the artifact exists. A write-time gate whose answer depends on which files happen to be lying around is not a gate. **What is bought:** a false positive can no longer block a legitimate write, and the verdict of a write no longer depends on the machine it runs on. **What is sold, stated plainly:** a genuinely dead reference and a genuinely foreign repository citation are now written, with a warning, and only a human reading that warning will act on it. Their could-not-examine paths (unusable `--root`, absent `jq`, unparseable record, unresolvable allowlist) are advisories too — an advisory check has nothing to fail closed *for* — while decision (b) governs the three blocking checks exactly as before, which is why they keep the 0/1/2 verdict codes and an unreadable store is still a refusal.

**An advisory nobody reads is worse than no check, so the reporting is the load-bearing half of this change.** Every finding prints a stable `ADVISORY:` marker carrying the *same* detail the refusal used to carry, in a shape no grep can confuse with a refusal (no advisory token begins with `REFUSE_`); the count and tokens are left in `$VALIDATE_ENTRY_ADVISORY_COUNT` / `$VALIDATE_ENTRY_ADVISORY_TOKENS`; and all six sole writers call a new `validate_entry_advisory_notice` on the write path so the finding is repeated at the call site, in the writer's own name, next to the fact that the write went ahead. `validate_entry_all` runs the three blocking checks first and returns the first non-zero among them, then runs the two advisory ones and **absorbs** their status — not because they are trusted to return 0 (they are, and a static test asserts neither function contains `_ve_refuse`, `_ve_unexaminable`, `return 1` or `return 2`), but because "this long function contains no non-zero return" is a property a future edit can break silently, and if it broke, an advisory finding would block a write again. A mutation control makes an advisory check return 1 and requires the aggregate to still exit 0 *and* name the anomaly. The contract sentinel moves to **`validate-entry/2`**: a writer built against `/1` was entitled to read "rc 0" as "all five checks passed", and that is no longer what it means, so a stale writer paired with this helper must refuse rather than write under a contract it does not implement. **The corpus replay was re-aimed rather than dropped** — it now resolves cited paths against a `git archive HEAD` extraction instead of the working tree, which is the method that exposed the defect, and it still gates the three checks that *can* refuse a live entry. Its probe is constructed, not derived from whatever gitignored files happen to exist: the first version picked the first ignored file it found and measured 0-vs-0 on another machine, because that file's owner directory existed in one tree and not the other — deriving a probe from the environment, inside the guard whose purpose is to stop the environment deciding the verdict, is the same defect one level up. A paired assertion now pins that the *verdict* is identical whether or not a cited runtime artifact exists while the *advisory* still moves with it, and a re-blocking mutant reproduces the original 1-vs-0 divergence on demand.

**Two writers gained a worktree guard they never had, and provenance stopped being a per-writer opinion.** The "existing sole-writer worktree invariant" was only 3 of 5: `add-rule.sh` and `add-orientation.sh` carried none and resolved their store with a bare toplevel lookup, which in a linked worktree returns the worktree's own toplevel, so both wrote happily from a worktree. They now refuse — guarding the **resolved** store root, so an explicit `--root` pointing at a worktree is refused too, which a cwd-only guard would miss — while their deliberate non-git-repo fallback is left unchanged rather than inheriting `write-lessons.sh`'s hard exit. Provenance moved into the shared helper: a source qualifies only if it carries a real reference (a digit, or a `:`/`/`/`#`/`@` id separator), so the shipped `/dreaming` accept path passing a bare command name is refused and now passes the session id it already held. And `write-project-memory.sh`'s benign "already stored" no-op had been turned into a hard failure by the validator firing first; the dedup now runs **before** validation, pinned by a test.

**Agent memory finally says what an agent may do with it.** `memory: project` agents may **not** write their own store: they write proposals, and every store write goes through the sole writer under a human `--confirm`. The trigger is **surprise-only** — an entry is proposed when something genuinely contradicted what the agent expected, not once per run — and queue volume is recorded rather than acted on, so widening the trigger stays a later decision made on data. Both rules are now stated under an identical `### Agent memory write permission` heading in the root `AGENT_GUIDELINES.md` and in all six `memory: project` prompts; the silence was the actual defect, because the tool surface gives the **wrong** answer. `disallowedTools` blocks `Write`/`Edit` for some of these agents, but Bash is not restricted by the harness, so a shell-redirection store write is reachable for all six whatever the allowlist says — which is exactly why this had to be prose and not an inference. `scripts/test-agent-memory-permission.sh` asserts **content, not a heading**: a heading grep is satisfiable by adding seven empty headings, so each file is reduced to the text under its heading and four literals are required there, with a control proving an empty section is rejected, a control proving a deleted refusal sentence goes red naming **only** that file, and a check that no forked contract has appeared under `loomwright/` — a copy there would satisfy the self-reported outputs gate while writing the rule into a document nothing reads.

**Then three of those writers turned out to accept flags they had never implemented — and write anyway.** `write-lessons.sh`, `write-project-memory.sh` and `write-system-contract.sh` ended their argument loop in a catch-all that silently dropped every unmatched argument and returned a clean success. All three derive their store from the **current directory**, never from anything the caller passes, so an argument that looks like it redirects the store parses, is discarded, and the write lands in whatever repo the caller happened to be standing in. This was not theoretical when it was found: during review of the work above, a reproduction run passed `--repo <temp repo>` believing it isolated the store, the flag was ignored, and a junk entry landed in this repo's real `LESSONS.md` and its provenance chain — caught only because someone ran `git status` afterwards. **The reach is ordinary, not exotic: `--repo` and `--store` are REAL flags on `write-agent-memory.sh` and `add-orientation.sh`**, so a caller reaching for one on a sibling writer is following house vocabulary, not making a typo. All three now refuse with **exit 2** — each writer's own could-not-examine convention, verified against its other argument checks rather than assumed — naming both the offending argument and itself, and writing nothing. **The guard is scoped to FLAGS only, deliberately.** A first revision also refused stray *positionals* — the same defect class, and tempting for exactly that reason — and it was narrowed back on review: a bare word is the shape most likely to appear in a caller's own script *outside* this repo, where no call-site audit can see it, so refusing it would silently convert a working command into an exit 2. An unimplemented flag carries no such risk, because it never did anything — refusing it cannot break a caller who was relying on its effect. The bound is asserted by a test, not just commented, so re-tightening it has to be a decision someone makes on purpose.

**The guard is deliberately NOT harmonised with the validator's parser, and each copy says so in place.** `validate-entry.sh`'s `_ve_parse_args` ignores unknown flags **on purpose**, so that a stray argument can never crash a fail-safe validation path. That reasoning is right for a validator and wrong for a writer, and the asymmetry is the whole point: a validator that refuses costs a diagnostic, a writer that silently retargets costs the store. The contrast is stated in a comment at all three guards, because the two parsers sit next to each other in the same vocabulary and unifying them would reopen this. **No `--repo` flag was added.** Rejecting unknown arguments and adding a real store-root flag are separate decisions, and adding one here would make the ignored-argument class invisible again — so: reject first. The other three writers already refused, but **no suite asserted it**, so those guards could have been deleted silently; all six now carry a test. **Two mutation controls caught defects in the tests themselves rather than in the code.** Stripping only the `--*)` arm from `write-lessons.sh` left the mutant still refusing — the dropped flag's *value* fell through to the positional arm and was rejected there — so the test would have passed against a writer with no flag guard at all, and the mutation now removes both halves. In the two positional writers a softened guard **drops** the stray flag rather than promoting it, so the stray must follow a complete positional set or the mutant dies on the usage check instead of writing. Both are recorded where the mutation is built, not worked around. Counts unchanged.

**v15.32.0 — the citation-drift guard stops policing one line in one file and polices every live `file.ext:<N>` in the repo:** v15.31.0 hit the "absolute line-ref drift in prose edits" defect class three times in a single change, and the guard that landed with it — `(j4b)` in `test-read-rules.sh` — was scoped to exactly one shape: `session-resume.sh:<N>` citations under `loomwright/scripts` that also said "no-arg" on the same line. The sibling instance in that same PR (`add-rule.sh`'s `--applies-to` comment citing the `category` traversal reject at `:245` when the guard was at `:274`) was invisible to it, which is precisely why that one survived two follow-up commits that were hunting this defect class. `(j4b)` is retired here and replaced by `scripts/test-citation-drift.sh`, which runs on every live surface. **It found twelve already-rotted citations on its first run**, all corrected in this change: `emit-token-ledger.sh:94→:128`, `read-postmortem.sh:124→:159` (three citing sites), `RESULT_SCHEMAS.md:249→:789`, `context-keeper.md:15→:26`, `brain-context/SKILL.md:42→:100`, `supervisor.md:140`→ a descriptive anchor, `write-lessons.sh:125→:46`, `setup-memory.sh:920→:949`, `async-orchestration/SKILL.md:576→:557` and `:120-122→:564-566`, `hooks.json:88→:112`, plus two off-by-one frontmatter references in `IMPROVEMENTS_ROADMAP.md` converted to descriptive anchors.

**What is and is not mechanically decidable, stated rather than over-claimed.** "Line N holds what the surrounding prose claims" needs the semantics of the prose, so the gate does not pretend to decide it. It runs three checks of increasing strength and asserts nothing beyond them. **(A) A line-count floor** — the cited file must resolve and must have line N. This is a floor, not a proof: it caught **none** of the three motivating defects, all of which cited a line that existed and merely said something else. It is kept because it is free and false-positive-proof, but a green (A) means "the line exists", never "the citation is right". **(B) Pinned anchors**, the check with teeth — a citation may carry `[pins: <literal>]`, and that literal must appear within ±3 lines of N. Auto-deriving the anchor from the prose was prototyped against this repo's real corpus first and measured at 1 hit / 11 false alarms / 8 no-signal, so it was rejected: the author chooses the literal, which makes (B) false-positive-free by construction. The ±3 window is deliberately tighter than the 8-line insertion that caused the original defect. **(C) A bare-citation ratchet** — every unpinned citation must be in an allowlist, so a *new* bare `file:N` fails the gate and authors are pushed toward `[pins:]` or a descriptive anchor ("the `category` guard in §1 above"), the style v15.31.0 converged on and the only one that cannot rot at all. The allowlist is itself checked for stale entries, so it can only ratchet down. Frozen historical surfaces — `CHANGELOG.md` and `docs/SPIKES/` — are excluded by design: they legitimately cite lines as they were, and a sweep without that exclusion fails immediately on correct history. Unresolvable citations SKIP rather than fail, because agent prompts carry illustrative output (`path/to/file.ts:67`) and some real references point into gitignored `.supervisor/` artifacts.

**The guard is mutation-tested, because the one it replaces shipped vacuous.** `(j4b)` reported success over an empty set on first write — its citation and its keyword sat on two different lines, so its `git grep` matched nothing. Eight synthetic mutation controls now run through the *same* `scan_surface()` code path as the real scan and are each required to catch a deliberate corruption; three further mutations were run against the real repo, including re-inserting eight lines above a cited line, which is the exact v15.31.0 defect.
