Two scanner bugs were caught this way and would otherwise have shipped silently: a pin written on the wrapped continuation line was not seen (the identical two-line footgun that made `(j4b)` vacuous — the scanner now takes a one-line lookahead, and refuses to borrow a pin across a line carrying its own citation), and a partial-path citation like `async-orchestration/SKILL.md` resolved to nothing because 41 skills share the basename `SKILL.md` (resolution is now a unique *suffix* match). A third bug was found in the guard itself: reading a zero-byte file into an array under `set -u` killed the subshell and **silently truncated** the result set from 34 citations to 14 — the scan would have reported fewer citations and passed. Review then found a fourth and a coverage gap, both fixed: a pin literal containing `]` (a JSON snippet, a `[0-9]` character class) was truncated at the bracket rather than at its closing backtick, which could yield a spurious PINMISS or a false OK on the truncated fragment; and the SKIP verdict had **no** mutation control, so a regression in the resolution chain could have routed a real, drifted citation into SKIP and silently exempted it from all three checks — vacuity by the back door. SKIP is now pinned from both sides (M9 unresolvable, M10 ambiguous, against M1/M8 proving a resolvable citation does *not* skip), and forcing total resolution failure is confirmed to trip 11 assertions rather than passing quietly. A later round caught two more of the same shape: both ratchet membership tests were substring matches, so as the allowlist grows one entry could silently exempt a *different* citation (`a.md|b.sh:1` is a substring of `xa.md|b.sh:12`) — now exact whole-line comparisons; and the `(A)` floor used `grep -c '' f || echo 0`, which on a zero-byte file prints `0` **and** exits 1, so the `||` appends a second `0` and the comparison errors out into a silently-false `if` — a citation into an empty file bypassed the check entirely. It now uses `awk 'END{print NR}'`, chosen over the obvious `wc -l` because `wc -l` counts newlines and under-reports by one on a file with no trailing newline (measured: 1 for a 2-line unterminated file). Both are covered by controls that were confirmed to fail against the old code. A final round closed the last two vacuity gaps in the guard's own bar — the anti-vacuity floor and `(C2)`'s stale-entry detector both sat *outside* the per-citation loop and had no control proving they could fire, the floor being the one check that exists specifically to catch what killed `(j4b)` — and replaced a `grep -e "/$cpath\$"` suffix match, where the cited path was interpolated as a **regex** (so the `.` in `regex.probe/` also matched `regexXprobe/`), with a literal `case` glob. Writing that last control was itself instructive: the first version used a decoy differing in the *basename*, which `find -name` already filters exactly, so the control passed against the very implementation it was meant to condemn — vacuous, caught only by running it against the old code, which is the discipline this whole file argues for. A last note turned a documented constraint into an enforced one: the header claimed one pin per line, but nothing checked it, so a line carrying two citations would have had both silently checked against the same literal — a false OK if it sat near both targets, a false PINMISS if it named only one. A pinned line with more than one citation is now an error telling the author to split it. Review also widened the scanned set: `.claude/agent-memory/` is un-ignored in `.gitignore` precisely so agent memory persists in git, which makes it committed, long-lived prose of exactly this class — and a rotted pointer there is worse than in a skill doc, because it misdirects the reviewing agent's own institutional memory on a later session with no human in the loop. It was unscanned, and three notes were carrying eleven bare citations; several had already rotted (a pointer into a now-blank line, another into a section enumeration that had moved, and two into `CHANGELOG.md:7`, which every release shifts by construction — including this one). The stable ones are pinned, the re-check lists now name their surfaces instead of line numbers. One more latent hole went with it: the range/list normaliser anchored a single match, so `:249,251,260` resolved to 251 rather than 260 and a past-EOF citation in a 3+ element list would have passed the floor. Two last ones came from review. Retiring `(j4b)` left two live comments still describing it as the active mechanism ("keep the word and the token together on ONE line or the guard stops seeing it") — the same defect class in prose rather than in a line number, and outside `CITE_RE` by construction, so the gate can never catch it. And suffix resolution walked the WORKING TREE rather than the tracked set, so a stray untracked file sharing a cited basename could flip a unique match to ambiguous and silently SKIP a citation locally while CI's clean checkout still checked it — a gate whose verdict depends on local junk. Not hypothetical: an untracked scratch file in this worktree was accidentally swept into a commit while this very PR was being written. Resolution is now `git ls-files` when the root is a checkout. The suite ends at 26 assertions, 20 of them mutation controls, each confirmed to fail against the code it rejects — two of which were themselves vacuous on first write and only exposed by running them against the old implementation, which is the entire argument this file makes.

**The convention surfaces were reconciled, including one that was missed on the first pass.** `CLAUDE.md`'s own "cite exact `file:line`" instruction in §"Adding or Modifying Agents" would otherwise have contradicted the gate it introduces. Review then caught the second-order case: `AGENT_GUIDELINES.md` — the shared preamble *every* agent follows — still said "always cite exact file:line" unconditionally, and its worked example walked an agent through proposing a `## Proposed CLAUDE.md Update` carrying a bare `(lines 23-67)` range, i.e. teaching the retired convention straight into the one file this change is about. Both now carry the committed-vs-ephemeral carve-out: a `file:line` in a live review finding is read once and discarded, so exactness is the most useful thing an agent can give; a line number in text that gets **committed** is falsified by the next insertion above it, so it must be a descriptive anchor or a pin. The gate only ever polices the latter. No `.github/workflows/` edit was needed; CI's existing `loomwright/scripts/test-*.sh` glob picks the suite up automatically, which also avoids making `claude-code-action` self-skip this PR's own review.

**v15.31.0 — `applies_to` stops being a comment and becomes a path filter:** The house-rules substrate shipped `applies_to` in the schema in slice #3b-i and then wired advisory enforcement in #3b-ii without ever activating it. `read-rules.sh` accepted the touched paths as positional args and **discarded them** — every rule reached every agent on every task, which makes a rule that fires everywhere indistinguishable from a rule that fires nowhere. Invisible so far only because the store holds exactly ONE rule; the next queue item harvests ~104 `convention_mismatch` findings into rules, at which point fifty unrouted rules injected at three seams is both token bloat and the advisory-prose-gets-ignored effect the queue exists to fix. The reader now routes: a new shell function **`rule_applies`** matches each touched path against a rule's globs with a native bash `case`, and `add-rule.sh` gains a repeatable **`--applies-to <glob>`** so the scope can actually be authored.

**Everything ambiguous fails OPEN, and one of those cases is load-bearing.** A malformed `applies_to` — non-array, array holding a non-string, empty array — emits the rule repo-wide with a diagnostic to stderr + `memory.log`, never to stdout. So does **a call with zero touched paths**, and that one is not symmetry for its own sake: `session-resume.sh`'s `rules_nudge()` calls the reader with **no arguments** and fires its "no house rules found" SessionStart nudge when stdout is EMPTY. Treating an empty path set as "nothing matches" would make every repo that HAS rules start nudging as if it had none — a silent regression in a firing surface CLAUDE.md calls pinned. Zero args is an absence of scope, not a negative match. (The source requirement's own evidence was wrong here: it cited `session-resume.sh:197`, an unrelated observability-probe comment, and asserted all three seams already pass paths. Two of three do.)

**Order of operations between the two filters was unspecified, and it decides whether retired rules can come back.** Fixed at `validate/dedup → supersession → routing`. Supersession answers "which rule is CURRENT?" — a property of the store, identical on every call — while routing answers "which current rules are RELEVANT to the paths this caller touched?" — a property of the call. Run routing first and a retired rule RESURRECTS on any call whose path set happens to route its replacement away, making the store's own curation decision depend on the caller's diff. Pinned by a test, and the rationale lives in the reader's header docstring rather than only in this entry.

**Matching is a `case` glob in the SHELL, never in `jq`** — portable by construction, no `shopt -s globstar`, no `extglob`, no GNU/BSD flavour divergence. `jq` only NORMALIZES `applies_to` into a route cell. The cost is stated in both author-facing surfaces rather than discovered later: **`*` and `**` are equivalent and BOTH cross `/`**, there is no `!` negation and no leading-`/` anchoring, so `"loomwright/scripts/*"` matches `loomwright/scripts/a/b/c.sh` — explicitly NOT `.gitignore` semantics. `.gitignore` behaviour is not emulated. Two implementation notes worth keeping: the route cell is US-delimited (0x1F) because TAB is an IFS *whitespace* character, so an empty route cell would have folded with its neighbour and shifted every later field left by one — the cell is therefore stripped before rendering; and `--applies-to` accumulates through a newline-delimited string rather than a bash array, because `"${arr[@]}"` on an empty array is an unbound-variable error under `set -u` in the macOS bash 3.2 dev shell.

**A test that only asserts "the rule IS emitted" passes identically against the old no-op reader,** so emission assertions are not evidence here. The non-match case asserts **absence from stdout**, and a **mutation control** proves that assertion is load-bearing: the reader is copied with its routing predicate replaced by `true`, the same fixture re-run, and the absence assertion observed to fail. The control also guards itself — if the `sed` matched nothing the mutant would be byte-identical and the control vacuous, so that is checked too. The three prose seams reduce to exactly TWO executable shapes (`read-rules.sh <paths…>` and `read-rules.sh` with no args); both are now traced dynamically against a fixture store in `test-rules-seams.sh`, with the limit stated rather than oversold — a dynamic trace cannot prove agent/skill *markdown* still hands the reader the right paths, which is why the static grep assertions stay.

**`rules-check.sh` deliberately does NOT follow routing.** It accepts no path scope at all (its arg loop warns-and-ignores unknown args), and the parity it promises with the reader is over per-object validation + `LC_ALL=C` first-seen-id dedup — *which objects are well-formed* — not over applicability. Routing is an emission filter for advisory injection; `/rules check` is a repo-wide audit, and a rule routed out of some worker's advisory block has not stopped being true. A test asserts a routed-out rule is still selected and executed, and proves the premise (that the reader really does route it out) first so the assertion cannot be vacuous. Eleven stale prose sites claiming `applies_to` is inert/reserved were swept; the `skills/rules/SKILL.md` quality-gate checkbox asserting the opposite of the new behaviour was rewritten, not deleted. Counts unchanged at 14 / 21 / 41 / 24 — no new file of any counted kind.

**v15.30.0 — the readiness count, the consumption window, and the record now speak one unit:** v15.29.0 shipped the curation cadence with three different units for one quantity, in three different files, with nothing asserting a relationship between them. Readiness counted *log files newer than a wall-clock stamp*; consumption took *the N most-recent files* (`--sessions`, default 5); `record` stored *wall-clock now*. Every conversion between them lost information, and three rounds of plan review missed it because no single file contained two of the three. Measured on this repo 2026-08-09 (62 logs), the probe reported `/dreaming pending=61 ready=yes` for a run whose default window reads 5.

**A watermark cannot fix this — the record had to become a set.** `/dreaming` consumes the *most recent* N logs, so what a run leaves behind is the *older* tail, and a watermark ("pending = newer than T") cannot count an older tail at any value of T: `T = now` collapses pending to ~0 and permanently retires every unread older log (the shipped bug — 56 of them here); `T = newest consumed` is identical; `T = oldest consumed` re-counts the batch just read and *still* never counts the tail. Consumption is a set selection from the top of a sort and pending is its complement, so `record dreaming` now takes the session ids actually fed to reflection and `pending` is the set complement. Pending falls by exactly what a run read, the remainder survives for later runs, and targeting the older remainder becomes expressible for the first time. The set is keyed under `.dreaming.consumed.logs` so twin-loop item 05's second intake source becomes a sibling key rather than a migration. Ids reach jq via `--args`, never interpolated into the filter. A zero-id `record` still stamps `last_run` — a run that read nothing is a real run — but WARNS, because a run that read logs and forgot to name them otherwise looks exactly like a stuck probe. Unreadable or unparseable state yields `unknown`, never a confident backlog manufactured from an input we failed to read.

**The count names the window that will drain it,** and `test-curation-status.sh` group (o) greps `commands/dreaming.md`'s Parameters table and fails if it drifts from the probe's `DREAMING_DEFAULT_WINDOW` — the cross-file assertion whose absence let the gate and the window ship as unrelated numbers. The `--json` unknown-body interpolates that same constant rather than repeating the literal.

**Pending counts reflection signal, not files.** Measured: 29 of 62 logs held ONLY `token_ledger`/`subtask_complete` events — 1.64 MB of a 1.70 MB corpus, ~96% of the bytes carrying 0% of the signal, one log alone holding 5108 events all of them noise — so a raw file count overstated the backlog by 1.9×. The two predicates deliberately differ because the two consumers do, verified in the consumers rather than assumed: `/dreaming` reflects broadly, so a denylist; `/insights` consumes exactly one event type (`session_end`, one record per log), so a log without one contributes nothing (27 of 62 qualify). Both fail OPEN — the denylist is anchored at `^{"event":"` precisely so that a writer-format change makes a line count as signal rather than vanish, and grep's exit >1 (unreadable) counts as signal too. Over-counting can only make the nudge fire needlessly; under-counting would silently suppress it on a log we never read. `/insights` keeps its watermark, which is *exact* for it: it re-reads the whole corpus every run.

*Two process notes recorded because both cost real time.* The first census of the signal figures came out exactly inverted (29 signal / 33 noise) because it used `jq … | grep -qv` under the agent shell's zsh, where that pipeline reports the producer's SIGPIPE rather than grep's verdict — recount under `bash file.sh`, or without `producer | grep -q`. And mutation testing ended at 14 of 14 applicable mutants killed, by two different routes. One apparent survivor exposed not a missing test but *dead code* — a `[ -r "$f" ]` pre-guard made grep's fail-open rc>1 arm unreachable, so the guard was deleted, one mechanism now decides, and the behaviour became testable. The last survivor was found by the PR reviewer rather than by me, and it was a genuinely untested branch: an unreadable `curation-state.json` (`consumed_log_ids`'s `[ -r ]` guard) could be relaxed to "empty consumed set" — reporting every log as unreflected — with the whole suite still green. That test is added here, which is what takes the run to 14 of 14.

**v15.29.0 — curation cadence: one recorded last-run, two derived siblings, and a SessionStart nudge that actually fires on a fresh session:** The plugin wrote continuously (session logs, worker summaries, PR churn) but only *checked* what it wrote when a human personally typed `/dreaming`, `/insights` or `/pr-postmortem`. None of the three left a last-run trace, so running one over 3 new findings and running it over 47 were indistinguishable from outside. This release adds one uncounted plain script, **`scripts/curation-status.sh`** (`status [--json]` · `record dreaming` · `nudge`), whose every exit path is `exit 0` — a runtime advisory emitter under the bimodal invariant, never a gate.

**The seam is `SessionStart`, not `Stop`.** The source requirement called this a "session-end reminder". Verified against the real files: `hooks.json`'s `Stop` hook is `type: "prompt"`, contracted to answer `{"ok": true}`/`{"ok": false}` to the code-reviewer result validator — its output is never shown as text. `SessionStart` is the `type: "command"` hook running `session-resume.sh`. Honest consequence, stated in the docs rather than papered over: the user sees the cadence line at the *start of the next* session, and it arrives as `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` — **model-context injection the agent then surfaces**, not a directly-rendered notification. `notify-desktop.sh` was weighed and deliberately rejected: it is reserved for an exceptional fault (stack down), and a recurring cadence reminder is not a fault.

**The nudge needed a DEDICATED `startup)` branch, because the obvious implementation provably never fires.** `session-resume.sh`'s gate is `case "$SOURCE" in resume|clear|compact) ;; *) exit 0 ;; esac`, and every existing nudge sits far below it — so `source=startup`, a fresh session, is exactly the case a cadence reminder matters most for and exactly the case that never reaches one. Worse, every nudge test still passes if it only ever feeds `{"source":"resume"}`. **Widening the shared gate was tried and rejected**, and the rejection is recorded so it is not re-proposed: everything below that gate becomes reachable on startup, and that set includes `observability_probe`, which runs `curl --connect-timeout 1` and fires a **desktop notification** when the stack is down — i.e. widening would put a network call and a desktop notification on every fresh session start, plus the prior-session header, Sections 1–5 and the unconditional recovery hints. The dedicated branch avoids all of it structurally instead of guarding sections one at a time. Because that branch `exit`s from *inside* the case, `curation_nudge_startup_only` must do three things for itself, each pinned by its own test: its OWN `[ -d ".supervisor" ]` check (the shared bail sits below the case — without it the nudge fires in every repo the user opens), its OWN `jq -Rs` + `iconv -c` `hookSpecificOutput` envelope (a bare `printf` is dropped or shown as raw JSON, so it would pass "the nudge fires" while never reaching the model), and **definition above the case** (every other helper in the file is defined below it, so the call would be rc 127 — swallowed by the following `exit 0` and hidden by the hook's `|| true`). `rules_nudge`'s firing surface is explicitly UNCHANGED and pinned by test: still silent on startup, still firing on resume.

**One stored last-run, two derived.** `.supervisor/curation-state.json` holds `/dreaming` only — a derived value cannot go stale, and `/dreaming`'s case is genuinely different: it writes only through the memory writers, so `.supervisor/memory/` mtime reports the last run *that accepted something* and is structurally blind to the ran-but-accepted-nothing case, which is precisely what the record exists to capture. `/insights` derives from `.supervisor/insights/dashboard.md`'s mtime; `/pr-postmortem` derives from the ledger via **`select(.source != "automate_drain")` then `max`**. That filter is load-bearing, not defensive: since v15.28.0 `results.jsonl` is a unified corpus with two producers, and on the live 81-record ledger `automate_drain` accounts for 26 records that no human ever "ran" — with drain and command records interleaving chronologically, so a bare `map(.ts)|max` reads an engine append as "you ran /pr-postmortem". The fixture pinning it has an `automate_drain` record as its **newest** line, so a naive implementation fails red.

**`/pr-postmortem` gets readiness reporting but NO declining gate** — both of its invocation forms target one named PR, and a gate answering "only 3 PRs since your last run, come back later" would refuse a direct question the user just asked. The declining gate is for the corpus-wide passes: `/dreaming` and `/insights` gain a `--force` flag and decline (advisory, exit 0, never an error) below a threshold that is labelled an **UNVALIDATED starting guess** in the script header, in both command docs and in the decline message itself (defaults 15/10, overridable at `.curation.thresholds.{dreaming,insights}` in `.supervisor/config.json`, which the probe reads and never rewrites). `build-insights.sh` was re-measured on this repo at **6.29 s** (not the requirement's quoted 4.8 s) — fine for an explicit run, far too slow for a blocking `SessionStart`, which is why the nudge is local-only: no `gh`, no `curl`, asserted by test rather than by inspection.

**Two fail-silent traps this repo has been bitten by are pinned, not just avoided.** mtime reads try `stat -c %Y` **first** with `-f %m` only as a fallback and validate the result numeric before any arithmetic — BSD's `-f` means `--file-system` on GNU coreutils, so it *succeeds with garbage* there, and an empty value inside `$(( ))` under `set -u` silently empties the probe (macOS-green, Linux-CI-red, already shipped once). A stat shim asserts both the call ORDER and that a non-numeric result yields `unknown`. And any unreadable input yields the string **`unknown`, never a fabricated `0`** — a zero would *suppress* the nudge, i.e. fail silent on exactly the input that could not be read; an input that is genuinely absent answers `never`/`0`, which is a different and honest answer. The unreadable-ledger fixture **skips with a visible message when `EUID` is 0**, because `chmod 000` does not block reads for uid 0 and would otherwise pass for the wrong reason.

Also fixed in passing: `session-resume.sh:36` claimed "Adds NO new hook — hooks stay 21" against an authoritative 24 — the count is now de-numbered rather than re-stated (the new header restates no count at all), and `commands/agent-help.md`'s `/dreaming` flag mirror was updated in the same change, since `check-command-sync.sh` covers only `code-reviewer.md` and `check-doc-currency.sh` does not scan flags, so that drift class passes every gate. **No `hooks.json` entry was added** and agent/command/skill/hook counts are unchanged at 14/21/41/24. Test surface: new `scripts/test-curation-status.sh` at **122 assertions**, `test-session-resume.sh` **24 → 67**, repo-wide `test-*.sh` suites **54 → 55**. One incidental correctness fix inside the tests themselves: `run_hook_ctx` stashed its exit code in a shell variable assigned inside a command-substitution subshell, so every `rc` assertion in that file was silently vacuous — the code is now stashed in a file and read back.

**Six review findings closed before merge, and three of them are about a check that was not checking what it claimed.** (1) **A latency regression on two commands that never read the value:** `status` unconditionally ran `derive_postmortem_pending`, whose un-timed `gh pr list --state merged --limit 50` feeds ONLY the `/pr-postmortem` column — yet `/dreaming` and `/insights`, which read only their own rows, both invoked plain `status` and paid the round-trip on every run (measured **1.01 s vs 0.29 s**). Both Step 0 snippets now set the existing `LOOMWRIGHT_CURATION_REMOTE=0` valve; `/pr-postmortem`, the one caller that consumes the count, leaves it unset, and the script header now documents that `status` may make one `gh` call. (2) **A vacuous guard test.** `test-session-resume.sh`'s "startup is silent outside a plugin repo" case was satisfied by `curation-status.sh`'s own `[ -d "$SUP_DIR" ]` — a THIRD copy of the predicate in a separate process — before the hook-side guard mattered. The guard *is* reachable, because the two resolve `.supervisor/` by different rules: the hook's is cwd-relative, the probe walks up to the git toplevel. So a **subdirectory of a plugin-active repo** discriminates them, and that is the new case (with a root-level control proving the fixture does nudge, so silence cannot be blamed on an empty corpus). Mutation control: removing the cwd-relative guard scores **66 passed / 1 failed**, naming only the new subdirectory case; the old case stays green under the mutant, which is the vacuity itself. A comment now records that the debounce marker path obeys the same cwd-relative rule and must be relaxed in the same edit if the gate ever is. (3) A **duplicated** copy of that guard in `curation_nudge_startup_only` is deleted — same process, same cwd, no intervening state change as `curation_nudge_line`'s own gate; the probe's third copy is deliberately kept. (4) `: > "$marker" 2>/dev/null || true` **did not suppress the error it targeted** — the shell reports the failed redirection before the `2>/dev/null` on that same simple command applies, so a read-only `.supervisor/` printed `Permission denied` and broke the file's silent-pass invariant; fixed as `{ : > "$marker"; } 2>/dev/null || true` at both marker writes. (5) `derive_dreaming_last_run` answered **`never` for a present-but-unparseable `curation-state.json`**, and `never` is a *claim*: it made every log count, so a corrupt state file could produce a decline citing "N new session log(s) since its last run" for a value the script never parsed. It now returns **`unknown`** for the unparseable cases (parse-checked up front, so a well-formed object carrying no record still answers the honest `never`), which readiness maps to `unknown` and therefore never declines. (6) The exit-0 invariant grep matched `exit[[:space:]]+`, which **cannot match a bare `exit`** — the exact previous-status-returning form the invariant forbids; anchored as `exit([[:space:]]|$)` with the `exit 0` exclusion kept. Mutation control: a bare `exit` appended to the script scores **121 passed / 1 failed** under the new pattern (re-measured against the final suite) and is invisible to the old one.

**A second review round closed seven more findings, and the headline one is the v15.28.0 fail-open class again — a check that could not tell *examined* from *failed to examine*.** (1) `derive_postmortem_pending` read `merged="$(gh pr list … || true)"` and then keyed on emptiness: `[ -n "$merged" ] || unknown`. The `|| true` **destroys gh's exit status**, so a SUCCESSFUL call on a repo with genuinely zero merged PRs — which prints nothing — was reported as `unknown`, identical to gh missing, no auth, or a network failure. That is precisely the conflation this script's OWN header forbids at the `UNREADABLE INPUT ⇒ unknown, NEVER A FABRICATED 0` note ("an input that is genuinely ABSENT is a different answer"). The status is now captured separately (`merged=$(…)` on its own line, then `gh_rc=$?` — `local merged="$(…)"` would make `$?` the status of `local`, and a comment records that), so **non-zero exit ⇒ `unknown`, zero exit with empty output ⇒ an honest `0`**, and a failing-but-noisy gh (non-zero exit that still printed rows) is `unknown` too: the status wins over the bytes. Every path still exits 0. (2) The set-diff half is factored out as **`pending_from_merged <merged-list>`** — pure, no network, taking the list as an argument — because `derive_postmortem_pending` had **zero** coverage: all four call sites in the test file set `LOOMWRIGHT_CURATION_REMOTE=0` and short-circuited it before the first line. New group **(l)** drives the whole thing through a `gh` **stub** on PATH (rc and stdout both scripted): the two exit-status cases above, three set-diff cases (3 merged / 1 in the ledger ⇒ 2 · all present ⇒ 0 · a non-numeric row skipped rather than counted missing), an assertion on the stub's real argv that the window is `--limit 50`, and a control that `LOOMWRIGHT_CURATION_REMOTE=0` still consults no gh at all. (3) `count_logs_newer_than`'s unreadable-directory branch was likewise untested, and it decides whether `/dreaming` and `/insights` report a real count or never decline — with the guard removed the glob simply fails to expand on an unsearchable dir and the count comes back a fabricated `0`. New group **(m)** pins `pending`/`ready` = `unknown`, an empty decline message, and that the nudge still FIRES carrying `unknown`, with a readable-fixture control and the same visible **uid-0 skip** the unreadable-ledger fixture uses (`chmod` does not block root). (4) The **`--limit 50` window is now documented** in the script header and in `commands/pr-postmortem.md`: the "merged PRs absent from the ledger" count covers only the 50 most recent merged PRs, so it is a **lower bound**, not a total — a live condition on this repo, already past PR #134 — and the cap is a deliberate one-bounded-round-trip choice, not an oversight. (5) `commands/agent-help.md`'s `/insights` entry carried no hint that the command can now decline or that `--force` exists; that was mirrored only as a trailing clause inside the `/dreaming` entry, so anyone scanning `/insights` got a stale picture. The entry now has its own Parameters row and usage block, and group (k) pins it **section-scoped** (`/insights` heading to `/obsidian` heading) so the `/dreaming` entry's mention cannot satisfy it vacuously. (6) `commands/pr-postmortem.md` claimed its pending count "is reported as `unknown` to the SessionStart nudge" — in the real `cmd_nudge`, `/pr-postmortem` is **not mentioned in the nudge at all**; `parts` is built from the `/dreaming` and `/insights` rows only. Corrected to say the command contributes to the cadence *record*, not to the nudge text. (7) `read_threshold` silently replaced a configured **`0`** with the in-script default. Of the two options, the meaningful one was taken: **`0` is now honoured** and means "always ready — report the cadence, never decline" (readiness compares `pending >= threshold`), the standing counterpart to the per-run `--force`; negative and non-integer values still fall back, since they express no coherent threshold. Silently overriding a value the user explicitly wrote is the same class of dishonesty the script forbids everywhere else, and the rationale is recorded in a comment above the function plus both command docs. Mutation control, each applied and reverted: restoring the `|| true` + emptiness check scores **115 passed / 2 failed**, naming only the two new exit-status cases; deleting the logs-dir readability guard scores **113 passed / 4 failed**, naming only group (m). With everything restored, `test-curation-status.sh` **94 → 117**, `test-session-resume.sh` unchanged at **67**, repo-wide **55 suites / 1716 assertions, 0 failures**; counts still 14/21/41/24.

**A third review round found the SAME examined-vs-could-not-examine conflation ONE GUARD CLAUSE LATER, and this time on the LEDGER side.** Round two fixed `derive_postmortem_pending`'s `gh` half; the very next line, `[ -r "$LEDGER_FILE" ] || unknown`, repeated the class — `[ -r ]` is false for a file that is ABSENT as well as for one that is UNREADABLE, so a repo where `/pr-postmortem` has simply **never run** reported `unknown` instead of an honest count. Two things made it a defect rather than a judgement call. Its own sibling `derive_postmortem_last_run` already draws the line correctly (`[ -e ] || never` **then** `[ -r ] || unknown`, absent and unreadable as distinct answers). And the honest first-run answer was **already computable**: `pending_from_merged` degrades correctly on an absent ledger — `jq` fails, `|| true` swallows it, `have` is empty, and every merged PR number then counts as missing through the `grep -qx` loop — so "12 merged PRs, a ledger that does not exist yet ⇒ pending=12" was there all along and the caller short-circuited before reaching it. The guard now mirrors the sibling's shape: **absent ⇒ fall through to the real count; present-but-unreadable ⇒ `unknown`**, every path still `exit 0`, and `pending_from_merged` itself unchanged. **The combination was untested in either direction**, and the two existing fixtures explain why: group (d)'s absent-ledger fixture runs through `run()`, which hardcodes `LOOMWRIGHT_CURATION_REMOTE=0` and therefore never reaches this guard at all, while group (l)'s `RL` — the only fixture with a git `origin` remote — always pre-creates the ledger before adding the remote. Group (l) gains both halves, each needing a working `gh` stub AND a controlled ledger state: an **absent** ledger with three merged PRs ⇒ `pending=3` (with a control asserting the fixture really has no `results.jsonl`, so the case cannot pass for the wrong reason), and a **present-but-unreadable** ledger with the same stub ⇒ `unknown`, carrying the same visible **uid-0 skip** the (d) and (m) unreadable fixtures use, since `chmod 000` does not block reads for root. Mutation control: reverting to the single `[ -r ]` guard scores **121 passed / 1 failed**, naming only the new absent-ledger case; restored, **122 / 0**. Suites: `test-curation-status.sh` **117 → 122**, repo-wide **55 suites / 1721 assertions, 0 failures** (1716 → 1721 by the same counting method the round-two paragraph used — the `RESULT: N passed` form only; counting the `pass=`/`passed=` suites too gives 2573). `check-doc-currency.sh` and `check-command-sync.sh` both pass; counts unchanged at 14/21/41/24.

**v15.28.0 — the findings ledger becomes a committed, GATED third store; `apply` fails closed on foreign records:** The Twin's churn corpus (`.supervisor/postmortem/results.jsonl`) was gitignored, so it never travelled with the repo and every fresh clone, worktree and CI run started blind. It is now the **third store** `/setup memory` manages — but it could not simply be un-ignored, because it is **structurally cross-repo**: a `/pr-postmortem` append lands in the CURRENT working `.supervisor/`, never in the analysed repo's, so a ledger accumulates records belonging to OTHER repos (this plugin's own held **7 records from a private downstream repo among 88**). A naive addition would have generalised that contamination into every user's repo and published another repo's churn analysis. So `setup-memory.sh` gains `ledger_gate_blocks_foreign_records()`: `apply` emits the ledger negation **only** while every record's `.repo` sits inside the resolved repo allowlist, and otherwise **refuses to write it**, names the offending slugs, and **still exits 0** — fail-closed in the WRITE dimension, never in the exit status, because this helper's `FAIL-SAFE CONTRACT` makes status lines and not exit codes carry the outcome. **The negation had to be conditional AND get its own verdict class, and the two are one fix.** `render_report` sets `intended_ok=no` for any INTENDED path probing `ignored`, which yields `not configured`, whose remediation copy tells the user to comment out "the surviving exclude" — which for a withheld ledger is the module's OWN `.supervisor/*` line, and `commands/setup.md` mandates relaying that copy verbatim. An unconditional add would therefore have made a **correct refusal permanently self-report as failure with destructive guidance**. Following the script's own recorded rule that *"the two failure modes are opposites and must not share copy"*, a **third** class `gated` is added (`REPORT_GATED`), the ledger joins the probed intended set only when the gate passes (`LEDGER_INTENDED_PATH`, held separately from `INTENDED_PATHS`), and `warn_if_not_configured` gains a `gated*)` arm **ahead of** the under-inclusion fall-through that names the offending slugs and the `filter-ledger` / extend-the-allowlist remedy and never mentions a surviving exclude. Two more consequences of the same conditionality are closed rather than hidden: the byte-compare no-op branch would have printed the exact `apply: no-op — already configured` headline the `gated` class exists to prevent (it now names the gated state), and the clean→contaminated transition legitimately rewrites `.gitignore` to WITHDRAW the negation — which is now **announced** with the slugs, never a bare `apply: applied`, and states the honest limit that withdrawal does **not** un-track an already-committed ledger. Degenerate inputs are decided, not guessed: an **an UNREADABLE ledger REFUSES** ("could not examine" is never "examined and clean" — the distinction a per-line fallback silently loses when it reads "produced no output" as "found nothing wrong"); **absent ledger PASSES** (every fresh repo; also keeps `test-committed-twin-scrub.sh`'s fixture assertion green), and an **absent `jq` leaves the gate UNEVALUATED** — verdict unchanged, negation not emitted, exit 0 — failing neither toward `gated` (which would permanently mis-report for every jq-less user) nor toward emitting something unchecked. **The gate is jq-only, and that is load-bearing, not stylistic.** This ledger mixes compact and spaced JSON — one record among 88 was spaced — so `grep -c '\"repo\":\"vikashruhilgit/loomwright\"'` returned **38** where jq returned **39**, and a foreign record appended in the spaced form would have been **invisible** to a compact grep, making a grep-based gate a guard that cannot fire. Every ledger assertion in `test-setup-memory.sh` is now jq (the pre-existing group (d) greps included), the spaced form is a named fixture case (`test_gate_blocks_foreign_spaced_form`) that proves the evasion (grep=0, jq=1) *inside* the fixture before exercising the gate, and the simulated foreign append is done in the spaced form specifically. Both wrong emission shapes are pinned as negative controls (`test_negation_wrong_position_still_ignored`): the naive one-liner `!.supervisor/postmortem/results.jsonl` does nothing (its parent directory is excluded), and the correct three lines placed BEFORE `.supervisor/*` do nothing either (git applies the LAST matching rule) — with a positive control on the same fixture so neither proves a broken probe. **Applied to this repo:** the ledger was filtered (re-derived with jq at implementation time: **42** `vikashruhilgit/ai-agent-manager` + **39** `vikashruhilgit/loomwright` = **81** retained, **7** foreign records removed, 88 → 81) and committed. **A latent defect in `test-committed-twin-scrub.sh` surfaced and is fixed:** its `ignore_is_in()` probed with `git check-ignore --verbose -n`, whose exit status is 0 whenever a pattern MATCHED — **including a negated `!` pattern**, i.e. exactly when the path is committable. It was invisible for the two memory stores because they are re-included by DIRECTORY patterns that never match the FILE itself (winning record `::`, rc 1, right answer for the wrong reason), and surfaced the instant a store was re-included by a FILE pattern. The verdict now comes from `-q` (the authoritative form, as `setup-memory.sh`'s own `ignore_status()` already used); `--verbose -n` is used only to print the winning rule in failure messages. That suite also moves the ledger from its `assert_ignored` heredoc into `assert_committable` (the deferral comment said the ledger must stay ignored "until a later item filters it" — this IS that item), adds a jq census asserting ZERO out-of-allowlist records in the committed ledger with a spaced-form injected negative control, and asserts the allowlist resolves to a non-empty list first so the census cannot be vacuous. **Explicit non-goal:** the gate keys on `.repo` ONLY — a record whose `.repo` is allowlisted but whose finding TEXT cites a foreign org is not caught, and `EXPLICIT_STORE_ROOTS` is deliberately NOT widened to `.supervisor/postmortem`; that is a separate item. Consent surfaces updated in lockstep so none still enumerates two stores: `print_consent_disclosure()`, the script header, the managed block's inline comment, `do_remove()`'s tracked-count and `git rm --cached` copy, all `commands/setup.md` mirror sites (dashboard cell, Check enumeration, Offer, the apply headline list, the WARNING prose, Verify, remove, and the idempotency note — restated as "a pure function of (contents, gate outcome)"), `commands/agent-help.md`'s `/setup` entry, and `skills/setup/SKILL.md` §Pattern 2 + §Pattern 8. Suites: `test-setup-memory.sh` **198 → 295** assertions, `test-committed-twin-scrub.sh` **32 → 37**. **One remaining unpinned branch is closed by test group (o):** `do_remove()` gained a THIRD `tracked_count` and a three-path `git rm -r --cached` hint when the ledger became a managed store, but every ledger group drives `apply`/`check` only, so the remove half was untested. Group (o) applies on a clean-ledger fixture, really `git add`s the ledger (a count of 0 would pass whether or not the code looks at it), then asserts remove's own warning reports the ledger count, that the ledger is IGNORED again by a real `git check-ignore` probe, and that the remediation names all three stores. Two idioms are load-bearing: the text assertions are scoped to the half of the output BEFORE `== verify ==`, because `do_remove` tails into `render_report`, whose own "tracked today" line names all three stores and kept an unscoped assertion green under mutation; and the ignore probe uses `--no-index`, since plain `check-ignore` reports a TRACKED path as not-ignored regardless of the rules — the very already-tracked-stays-tracked fact this output warns about. Suites **286 → 295**; mutation control, one break per assertion: dropping the ledger from remove's tracked-count line ⇒ 294/1 ((o1) only), from the `git rm` hint ⇒ 294/1 ((o3) only), and leaving an effective ledger negation behind after remove ⇒ 290/5 ((o2) plus the four pre-existing byte-exact round-trip assertions, as expected). **One fail-open was found by the Phase 4.5 review and closed before merge, and the CLASS is the point: the gate's per-line fallback treated *"produced no output"* as *"examined every record and found none foreign"* — it could not distinguish **examined-and-clean** from **failed-to-examine**. Two inputs exploited that one root cause: a final line with **no trailing newline** (`while IFS= read -r line` returns non-zero on an unterminated last line — and a truncated append is *exactly* the malformed record that triggers the fallback, so one interrupted `/pr-postmortem` write sufficed), and an **unreadable** ledger (`[ -f ]` passes, the whole-file `jq` fails, the fallback's redirect fails, the loop iterates zero times). Both made `apply` emit the negation with a foreign record present, breaking the verbatim consent promise on a PUBLIC repo; a *valid* foreign record as the unterminated last line was not even named in the refusal. Fixed with `|| [ -n "$line" ]` plus a nothing-was-examined marker. **The existing test hid it by one byte** — the malformed-record fixture carried a trailing newline; dropping it reddens the pre-fix suite. Suites 259 → 286; each fix reverted in turn reddens only its own case (261/2, 261/2). **A SECOND fail-open of the same shape — this time in the OPPOSITE direction — was found by PR review and reproduced before merge, and again the CLASS is the point: the EMISSION decision (`ledger_gate_permits_negation`, state must be `pass`) ALSO governed whether an EXISTING negation survived the regenerated block, so *"we could not determine the ledger's state"* was treated identically to *"we determined the ledger is contaminated"*.** On a fresh repo that is correct and documented; on an ALREADY-APPLIED repo it **silently WITHDREW a good negation and blamed foreign slugs that do not exist**. Two inputs, one root cause: **`jq` genuinely absent** (contradicting the documented promise that jq-absent leaves the pre-existing verdict unchanged — the (l4) test only covered the fresh case), and, worse, **`jq` PRESENT BUT BROKEN** — `command -v jq` tests PRESENCE, NOT FUNCTION, so a `jq` exiting non-zero made the whole-file pass AND every per-line probe fail, counting a perfectly CLEAN ledger entirely unparseable and REFUSING with `records outside the allowlist: (unparseable record at ledger line 1)`. **The fix is an ASYMMETRY, not another special case:** EMITTING a new negation still requires an affirmative examined `pass` (never publish the unverified), while WITHDRAWING an existing one now requires an affirmative `refuse` backed by REAL, EXAMINED foreign slugs (`ledger_gate_warrants_withdrawal()`); every could-not-examine outcome PRESERVES what the block already carries (`ledger_negation_in_block()`, the single decision point shared by `managed_block()`, `render_report()` and the `apply` headlines so they cannot disagree). Rationale: a withdrawal is an **unrequested state change justified by a diagnosis we did not make** — preserving publishes nothing new (the ledger is already un-ignored and possibly already committed, and `.gitignore` does not untrack tracked files), whereas withdrawing emits a FALSE contamination message. The two causes now travel in **separate channels** — `LEDGER_FOREIGN_SLUGS` (observed `.repo` values) vs. the new `LEDGER_UNEXAMINED_REASON` — which also closes a second review finding: the `gated` remedy copy offered `filter-ledger > tmp && mv tmp ledger` for the placeholder "slugs", and on an unreadable ledger that prints nothing and **truncates it to zero bytes**; the could-not-examine arm now gets its own remedy ("install/repair jq", "make the file readable", "repair the malformed record") and explicitly warns against the filter pipeline. A broken `jq` is detected functionally (`printf '{}' | jq -e .`) and treated exactly like an absent one (`not-evaluated`), never as contamination. **Everything previously green stays green**: absent ledger ⇒ PASS; a genuinely foreign record still REFUSES, WITHDRAWS and names the real slug; and the unterminated-final-line and unreadable-ledger cases still REFUSE for EMISSION purposes with the verdict still `gated` — they simply may no longer withdraw or accuse. New test group **(n)** covers all three (already-applied + jq absent ⇒ PRESERVED; already-applied + a `jq` stub exiting 127, meta-checked as present-and-non-functional ⇒ PRESERVED with no false `unparseable record` claim; already-applied + a real foreign record ⇒ still WITHDRAWN, real slug named): `test-setup-memory.sh` **266 → 286**. Mutation control: the pre-fix writer scores **278 passed / 8 failed**, and each half reverted in turn fails only its own assertions (asymmetry only: 281/5; functional-jq probe only: 285/1, the single `no false 'unparseable record' claim` case).

Mutation control (every number measured, each mutation applied and reverted in turn):** replayed against the pre-fix `setup-memory.sh` at `0276ad6`, `test-setup-memory.sh` goes **237 passed / 22 failed**; emitting the three negation lines BEFORE `.supervisor/*` → **243/16**; the naive one-line form → **244/15**; emitting the ledger lines unconditionally (verdict-only gating) → **251/8**, failing exactly the four refusal assertions (l2/l5/l6/l8); dropping the `gated*)` arm so the under-inclusion fall-through is reached → **257/2**, naming *"THE DESTRUCTIVE UNDER-INCLUSION COPY was printed for a gated repo"*; treating an absent ledger as a refusal → **248/11** (it reddens the pre-existing groups (b)/(b2)/(b4), which is the concrete cost decision (d) avoids); reverting the gated no-op headline → **258/1**; disabling the withdrawal announcement → **257/2**. In `test-committed-twin-scrub.sh`, reverting `ignore_is_in` to `--verbose -n` → **35 passed / 1 failed**, naming the ledger. With everything restored: **295/0** and **37/0**. Counts unchanged (14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 24 hooks).

**v15.27.0 — project memory gains a correction path (`--retract` / `--supersedes`), and two ways it silently corrupted the index are closed:** Ships the **correction capability** the store never had: a stored fact can turn out to be WRONG, and a raw text edit is not an option — the read gate hashes the fact text, so a hand-edited line silently stops matching its `add` and is DROPPED without a trace at the call site. `write-project-memory.sh --retract <id>` now deletes the entry line **and** appends a chain-valid `retract` provenance entry, which `read-project-memory.sh` honors by revoking that `content_hash` from the trusted set — the read-side half is load-bearing, because the original `add` lives forever in the append-only log, so a writer-only deletion would leave a known-WRONG fact's hash trusted and an out-of-band re-append would read back as VERIFIED. `--supersedes <id>` is the same mechanism paired with a replacement `--fact`: both halves are built in temps and committed by the same pair of atomic renames, so the correction and the retraction land together or not at all. Chronological semantics are preserved — a later `add` of the same text **re-trusts** it, so a retract is a point-in-time revocation, not a permanent blocklist. The stale `subagent_type` fact in this repo's own `PROJECT_MEMORY.md` is corrected through that path rather than edited by hand. **Two correctness defects in the new path are fixed in the same release, both reproduced against the real script before and after.** (1) **`--supersedes` bypassed the dedup guard entirely.** The guard read `grep -qF -- "- [$id] $fact_oneline" "$MEM" && [ -z "$RETRACT_ID" ]`; the trailing conjunct was meant to stop a supersede from short-circuiting (it still has the retraction to do) but instead disabled the **presence CHECK itself**, so the add half appended unconditionally and a correction whose replacement text already existed elsewhere in the index wrote a **duplicate line** — violating the "duplicate fact written once" invariant the suite already guarded for the bare-`--fact` path. Presence is now computed ONCE into a `fact_present` boolean that gates only the ADD half: a bare `--fact` that is already present still short-circuits with its exact prior message and exit 0, while a `--supersedes` whose replacement is already present performs the retraction and skips the duplicate append **and** its spurious `add` provenance entry (the same shape `write-lessons.sh` already used for its replacement half). Skipping the `add` cannot orphan the hash chain — `append_prov` always chains off the CURRENT tail — and the completion message no longer says "stored" for a line that was deduped away. (2) **A no-op supersede DELETED the fact, silently, with exit 0.** When `--fact` was byte-identical to the target's own text, the freshly-appended line and the pre-existing one were identical and `grep -vxF` removed **every** matching line; identical text also means one shared `content_hash`, so the `retract` entry revoked the very hash the `add` had just trusted — the fact vanished from `PROJECT_MEMORY.md` and from the reader while the script printed "stored" *and* "retracted" and exited 0. Chosen semantic: such a call is **not a correction at all**, so it now **fails CLOSED with exit 2 before any temp state exists** (the same convention as the existing unknown-id abort) — the fact stays in the file, the reader still returns it, provenance is untouched, and the caller gets a real error instead of a false "retracted". The two paths are deliberately distinct: colliding with a DIFFERENT entry is a legitimate correction (retract runs, append skipped); colliding with the TARGET's own text is a no-op (nothing runs). **Tests:** a new group 9 in `test-project-memory.sh` covers the collision case (exactly one line, exactly two `add` entries, retraction still landed, survivor still reads back verified — which is what proves the chain survived a skipped `add`), the no-op case (non-zero exit, no false "retracted" message, provenance byte-identical, fact still in the file, reader still returns it), and a no-regression pin on the bare-`--fact` short-circuit's exit code and exact message. **Three follow-up hardening items** — raised as explicitly non-blocking observations on review of the above, closed in the same release, none of them changing an existing success path: **(a) the id gate now matches its own comment.** It read `*[!0-9a-f]*|""`, which rejected non-hex and empty but accepted ANY length, so a malformed `--retract a` passed validation and only died later at the target lookup with the misleading "no memory entry with id [a]". Ids are EXACTLY 8 lowercase hex chars by construction (`cut -c1-8` of the fact's sha256), so the check is now that shape stated **positively**, and a wrong-length id fails at validation with `--retract id must be exactly 8 lowercase hex chars`. **(b) `--supersedes` with no replacement `--fact` now FAILS CLOSED.** Both spellings write the same `RETRACT_ID`, so a `--supersedes <id>` with nothing to supersede quietly degraded to a plain retraction. The **spelling** the caller used is now tracked in a separate `SUPERSEDES_USED` flag (`RETRACT_ID` is untouched), and that case **aborts with exit 2 at validation time**, state byte-identical, with a message naming the missing replacement and pointing at `--retract`. An interim version of this fix only WARNED, on the rationale that erroring "would be a breaking change" — that rationale was false (`--supersedes` ships in this same release and has **no** callers), and it left this store disagreeing with the sibling it mirrors: `write-lessons.sh:125` already rejects the identical operator mistake with *"a supersede without a replacement is an indistinguishable synonym for retract"*. **The two stores now agree deliberately**, and the abort matches the repo-wide "correctness gates fail CLOSED" invariant. `--retract`, for which a bare retraction is the honest meaning, stays byte-identical and silent at exit 0. The abort also **subsumes** a second defect the warning carried: sitting at validation time but non-fatal, it printed *"this is a plain retraction"* for `--supersedes <unknown-id>` and then aborted exit 2 at the target lookup a few lines later, having retracted nothing — a claim false on its own path. Argument-shape errors are now ordered ahead of every state lookup, so that call reports the missing replacement, and being fatal there means the abort precedes every `mktemp`. **(c) the unknown-id abort gains its ADD-half branch.** It was only covered for a bare `--retract`; group 8 now also pins `--fact "<new>" --supersedes <unknown-id>` — the branch where the add half and the target resolution share a path — asserting exit 2, the replacement fact **not** written, `PROJECT_MEMORY.md` **and** `.provenance.jsonl` byte-identical, no `.mtmp`/`.ptmp` left behind, and the reader still returning exactly the pre-existing entries. **One more class, closed at the root:** every match on the structured entry shape `- [<id>] <text>` in `write-project-memory.sh` was an **unanchored `grep -F`**, so a match could land in the MIDDLE of another entry's free text — and fact text is arbitrary human prose that quotes bracketed ids inline (this repo's own `PROJECT_MEMORY.md` already stores such entries), so the defect was reachable, not theoretical. Both reachable sites shared the one cause and both were reproduced before and after. (1) **The dedup guard discarded brand-new facts.** With a decoy stored as `docs say the format is - [7f2a740c] victim entry and that matters`, writing the genuinely new fact `victim entry` printed `fact already present ([7f2a740c]) — skipping` and exited 0 without ever storing it — silent data loss on a success status, because the rendered line was a *substring* of another line. The guard is an exact whole-line comparison by construction, so it now uses **`grep -qxF`**. (2) **The retract lookup deleted the wrong line.** `grep -m1 -F -- "- [$RETRACT_ID] "` matched a decoy such as `see - [7f2a740c] for details`; ordered before the real target, the decoy won `-m1`, the `grep -vxF "$retract_line"` deletion then removed the **decoy** while the real entry survived — with provenance recording the real id as retracted, so state and provenance disagreed and the reader kept serving the "retracted" fact. That site is a line-start PREFIX match with an arbitrary suffix, so `-x` cannot apply; it is now an **anchored ERE** (`grep -m1 -E -- "^- \[$RETRACT_ID\] "`), which is safe **only because** the id gate above guarantees exactly 8 chars from `[0-9a-f]` — a set with no regex metacharacter. That dependency is now stated at the gate, together with the instruction to convert the lookup to a literal prefix test if the gate is ever loosened. Every remaining match on the entry shape in the writer (`grep -vxF` deletion, the `^- \[` eviction counters, the bracket-strip `sed`) was audited and is already anchored; `read-project-memory.sh`'s two trusted-set matches were re-checked and are already `-x` whole-line, so the class does not reach the reader. Two smaller review observations are folded in: **`SUPERSEDES_USED` is now cleared by both `--retract` arms**, so last-flag-wins applies to the *spelling* exactly as it already did to the *value* (`--supersedes <a> --retract <b>` used to warn `--supersedes [<b>]`, naming an id never passed under that spelling — now higher-stakes than a mislabelled warning, since a stale flag would REJECT an honest bare `--retract` with exit 2); and the id-gate rationale comment no longer claims the id reaches a `sed` address — it does not, and the comment now describes the anchored-regex interlock it actually earns. Suite: **22 → 47 → 64 → 79 assertions**. **Known limit — the allowlist does NOT travel with the repo, and CI proved it.** `.supervisor/config.json` is gitignored (the managed block re-includes the memory stores and the ledger, never the config), so on a fresh clone the allowlist falls back to the live git remote ALONE — one entry. Every pre-rename `vikashruhilgit/ai-agent-manager` record in the committed ledger is then judged FOREIGN. This is not hypothetical: the committed-ledger census passed on the author's machine (2-entry config) and failed in CI (1-entry default) on the very commit that introduced it. The census now declares its owned slugs in COMMITTED code and treats the machine-local allowlist as an advisory cross-check — a gate asserting a property of committed data must take its expectation from committed source, or it asserts a property of whoever runs it. The runtime `apply` gate still reads local config, which is correct for a user's own repo. 

**Mutation control:** replayed against the reconstructed pre-fix writer the suite goes **39 passed / 8 failed** with the reported symptoms (`supersede duplicated the colliding fact (have 2, want 1)`, `no-op supersede DELETED the fact (silent data loss)`); each hardening fix reverted in turn fails only its own new assertions — measured against the then-64-assertion suite: id gate **45/2**, unknown-target abort **40/7**, the bare-`--supersedes` gate **46/1** (as first landed, when it only warned), dedup anchoring **61/3**, retract anchoring **61/3**, `SUPERSEDES_USED` reset **63/1** per arm; reverting the bare-`--supersedes` abort to the interim warn-and-succeed path fails **15** assertions (**64 passed / 15 failed**); with everything restored the suite is **79 passed / 0 failed**. Counts unchanged (14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 24 hooks).

**v15.26.0 — `learning-emit`: a degraded ledger line can no longer permanently block its own correction:** Fixes a design flaw in `scripts/automate-helpers.sh`'s `learning-emit` (observed 2026-08-06 on PR #126). The subcommand was idempotent on `run_id`+item+`pr_url`+`source`, which is correct for crash-safety but meant a FIRST emit that **degraded** — the single `gh pr view --json files,…` fetch failed, or the caller omitted the fetch args — wrote a line with `changed_paths: []` and then **poisoned the key**: every later emit carrying the full, correct data was silently skipped. A `changed_paths: []` line is PERMANENTLY INVISIBLE to `read-postmortem.sh` (its overlap filter can never match an empty array), so the ledger kept a line that no consumer could ever return — the exact silent-invisibility failure the feature exists to prevent, and the second half of the same trap the SKILL already documented for `--repo`. **Fix (option (b) — the key is degradation-aware):** `automate_key` gains a trailing `complete`\|`degraded` component (`complete` iff `changed_paths` is a non-empty array), so a degraded line does NOT block a later complete one. Exactly-once now holds per (run/item/pr/source, state): an exact-key re-entry skips, a DEGRADED emit skips whenever any match exists (never regress a good line, never write a second invisible one), and a COMPLETE emit skips when a complete match exists (at most ONE good line). The single newly-permitted append is **complete-after-degraded** — the correction. **Legacy** four-component keys are matched by prefix, so a pre-existing complete line stays idempotent and a pre-existing degraded line is correctable. **Invariants preserved:** `learning-emit` still ALWAYS exits 0 and never gates, the ledger stays append-only, and `curate-postmortem.sh` remains the SOLE curator writer — no second writer was added. **Why not the alternatives** (recorded in the code comment, not just here): (a) superseding the degraded line via `curate-postmortem.sh` is unusable in this loop — its `target_key` matches a data line's `automate_key` **OR** `pr_url`, so a record aimed at the degraded line would ALSO hide the corrective line (same `pr_url`), and it is human-gated on `--confirm` so it can never run unattended; (c) refusing to emit a degraded line at all discards the honest `review_rounds`/`self_heal_misses` churn signal on every fetch failure and breaks the documented always-emit fail-safe contract. The degraded line is left in place and stays invisible to `read-postmortem.sh`, but it is **NOT** quarantined downstream — a first version of this change claimed it was, and that claim was wrong: `repo` and `number` are derived from `pr_url` INDEPENDENTLY of the fetch that degrades (`skills/automate-loop/SKILL.md` §6 derives them in the "inputs the engine already holds" bullet, before "the ONE added fetch"), so a contract-compliant caller emits the degraded line with the REAL `number`; a `number: 0` means `--number` was omitted altogether. The degraded and corrective lines therefore SHARE the `repo`#`number` key that `build-loop-evidence.sh` and `measure-heal-signal.py` join on. `measure-heal-signal.py` was already safe (floor-raising `max review_rounds`, tie-break latest `ts`); **`build-loop-evidence.sh`'s postmortem join was first-wins** (`jq select | head -1` over an append-ordered stream), so once a resume added fix cycles between the degraded attempt and the corrective one it would silently report the STALE, lower round count — the same silent-staleness class this fix closes, relocated one script downstream. That join is now floor-raising too, and `review_rounds` being a documented FLOOR makes that the correct semantic rather than a patch. Relatedly, `complete` requires at least one **non-empty** path — `changed_paths: [""]` is as invisible as `[]` (the reader drops empty query paths), so classing it complete would have re-opened the defect; the skip predicate's own completeness test was aligned to the same rule, a divergence the new test caught. **Tests:** seven new cases in `test-automate-helpers.sh` (65 → 72) asserted against the REAL `read-postmortem.sh` in a throwaway git repo with an origin remote — deliberately NOT the section's `reader_select` jq mirror, which could drift from the reader it models — covering degraded→corrective, a **mutation control** that replays the correction over a byte-accurate LEGACY degraded key (invisible BEFORE, correctable AFTER), and a no-regression case pinning both skip arms — all three FAIL against the pre-fix helper with the reported symptom — plus the REALISTIC degraded shape (`--number` passed, proving both lines share one `repo`#`number` key), a load-bearing check that a first-wins join reports the stale count where floor-raising reports the true one, degraded-after-degraded in isolation, and empty-string-only `changed_paths` classified degraded. The `build-loop-evidence.sh` join half gets its own regression net in `test-build-loop-evidence.sh` (28 → 30), driving the REAL builder over a two-line fixture that shares one `repo`#`number` key — reverting the join to first-wins turns both cases red. The `test-automate-helpers.sh` `F13b` mirror is explicitly labelled as a non-authoritative illustration pointing at that case, rather than left to imply coverage it does not provide. **Docs:** `skills/automate-loop/SKILL.md` §6 now documents the degraded-emit trap beside the existing `--repo` warning (with an explicit "prefer real fetch data on the first call — the correction is a safety net, not a licence to skip the fetch"), and `docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` records the new `automate_key` shape, the at-most-two-lines-per-prefix consequence, legacy-key handling, and "treat it as an opaque string; match by EXACT equality, never by parsing its components." **No `schema_version` bump** — `automate_key` was already an opaque optional string. Counts unchanged (14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 24 hooks).

**v15.25.0 — `/setup memory`: make the Twin committable in place (gitignore negation + repo allowlist):** Executes `.supervisor/requirements/twin-loop/01-commit-the-distilled-twin.md`, Single-Agent Path, single subtask. Adds an **8th `/setup` module** that puts a repo's Twin memory stores — `.claude/agent-memory/` and `.supervisor/memory/` — under version control **IN PLACE**: no move, no symlink, no generated copy, no bridge. The measured probe behind this decision is recorded in the requirement: the harness's agent-memory path is FIXED and a committed `.agent/` copy is never injected, so a `git mv` would silently sever every agent from its memory *while leaving the whole test suite green* — un-ignoring where the stores already sit makes that constraint disappear entirely. Bonus: committed files exist inside `git worktree` checkouts while gitignored ones do not, so this also closes the gap where workers in worktrees could not see lessons or agent memory. **New engine `loomwright/scripts/setup-memory.sh`** (`check` / `apply` / `remove` / `allowlist` / `filter-ledger`), fail-safe (always exits 0), write-contained to `<project>/.gitignore` (+ one timestamped backup) and `<project>/.supervisor/config.json`, and it NEVER runs `git add` / `git rm` / `git commit`. **The naive negation silently fails and is now asserted, not commented:** `.claude/` + `!.claude/agent-memory/` looks correct and does nothing (git cannot re-include a file whose parent DIRECTORY is excluded); the working `/*` form is what ships, any pre-existing bare `.claude/` / `.supervisor/` line is COMMENTED OUT (neutralised, not merely out-ordered — one surviving bare line kills the block) and restored verbatim by `remove`. **New `loomwright/scripts/test-setup-memory.sh`** — 198 assertions across 16 groups, every fixture its own `mktemp -d` + `git init` with `core.excludesFile` pinned, proving: the naive form FAILS and the `/*` form works; each intended path committable and each unintended path (`worktrees/`, `settings.local.json`, `logs/`) still ignored, asserted **per path** via `git check-ignore`, never as a bulk claim; the dotfile sidecars `.provenance.jsonl` / `.lessons-provenance.jsonl` commit (their own silent-failure class — losing them strips provenance from a fresh clone); apply is idempotent by byte-comparison (second run writes nothing, no second backup); every abort branch of the parse gate — absent, conflict-marked, sentinel-unbalanced, SYMLINK (link *and* target left byte-identical), non-regular file, unreadable/unwritable (skipped under root, which bypasses mode bits), and NUL/binary content — ABORTS with a reason, no partial write and no backup, the NUL fixture two-sided so it cannot pass under the `grep -q $'\0'` empty-pattern regression it exists to prevent; and `remove` round-trips the file BYTE-EXACTLY. A negative control was run — reverting the shipped block to the naive form turns 27 of those assertions red (198/0 → 171 passed, 27 failed). **The repo allowlist is a LIST, never a string, and never the live remote at read time:** a repo RENAME is the documented reason (this plugin's own ledger holds 42 records under the pre-rename slug against 35 under the current one, so a live-remote filter would silently drop the larger, older half). It defaults to the current remote's `owner/repo` on fresh install — never a hardcoded owner — stores as a JSON array, excludes records with no `repo` as unattributable, and treats an empty allowlist as retaining NOTHING (fail-closed). `filter_ledger_by_allowlist` is the reusable, fixture-proven predicate for the follow-on item. **Consent is load-bearing:** applying makes user content version-controlled and it travels wherever the repo travels, so `check` prints (and `apply` reprints, BEFORE the write) an explicit disclosure naming the publish risk — proprietary architecture, internal service names, client detail — and `remove` states plainly that **git history retains anything already pushed**; removal stops future tracking, it does not unpublish, and already-tracked files need the user's own `git rm --cached`. **Deliberately OUT of scope (stated, not silently skipped):** `.supervisor/postmortem/` is NOT negated and the findings ledger is NOT committed by this item (its sole sanctioned writer is append-only, and committing it would publish 77 findings as a side effect of a capability change); and the tracked-write risk — memory writes becoming working-tree modifications that `git add -A` can sweep into an unrelated commit, with `MEMORY.md` as a conflict surface — is recorded in the module docs and handed to the memory-writer item, **no proposal queue is built here**. **Correctness fixes landed under review (each mutation-proved — reverting it turns the suite red), all in the shipped mechanism rather than internal churn:** the sentinel presence GATE (`grep -F`, substring-anywhere) and the block STRIPPER (`index($0,b)==1`, column-anchored) used different matchers, so a merely INDENTED `BEGIN` passed the gate as one valid block, was not stripped, and made `apply` append a SECOND block — after which every later `apply` **and** `remove` aborted on `duplicated managed-block sentinels`, the tool bricking the very file it manages (one matcher is now authoritative for both, documented as an invariant); backup-before-write was **entirely unasserted** (stubbing the `cp` to a no-op left the old suite fully green) and is now asserted positively — the backup exists and its content IS the pre-write file; the neutraliser missed the recursive `.claude/**` / `**/.claude/` family; `unique_backup_path` returned an ALREADY-EXISTING path at its bound instead of failing closed, so the caller's `cp` would have destroyed the pristine original, and `seed_allowlist` swallowed a backup failure and rewrote a user's config **unbacked-up** (both now abort); a same-second-collidable backup name was replaced; the `X/*` exclusion's stated rationale was factually wrong AND comment-pinned rather than test-pinned (adding `X/*` to the forms list kept the suite green); and the `partial` verdict — **over**-inclusion, an unintended path became committable — fell through to the **under**-inclusion remediation copy, which named nothing and told the user to comment out an exclude, advice whose remedy would leak MORE (it now has its own branch naming each leaked path with the rule that actually wins for it, and a dedicated fixture reachable only via a nested `.claude/.gitignore`). On the test side, a FIFO handshake resting on `O_RDWR` on a FIFO — formally UNDEFINED in POSIX — inside a hard CI gate with no per-test timeout was retired for two bounded file polls, so exhaustion is a loud assertion failure rather than a wedged job. Wiring: `commands/setup.md` gains a `## Module: memory` flow, a dashboard row, an explicit `## Constraints` line for the new `.gitignore` write class, and a **reworked** ≤4-option `AskUserQuestion` set (already saturated at 4 with 4 modules folded in — `twin` + `memory` now fold into one "Repo knowledge stores" option that branches to a nested ≤4-option question, and every module stays reachable via its direct `/setup <module>` jump). `skills/setup/SKILL.md` 1.0.0 → 1.1.0 with the new registry row and a new Pattern 8 (`.gitignore` managed-block rules). Counts: agents 14, commands 21, skills 41, hooks 24 — all UNCHANGED (this adds a module, not a command).

**v15.24.0 — Unify the `tools:` frontmatter superset across all 14 agents (consistency-only, no cache win):** Executes `.supervisor/requirements/final-state/12-4c-unified-tools-lists.md` (item 07's scope item 3, split into its own PR per that requirement's own directive), Single-Agent Path, single subtask. **The premise this requirement started from — that a unified `tools:` block plus the shared prefix "clears the 512-token floor" for cross-agent prompt-cache sharing — is FALSE and was falsified before this change was written; it does not clear the floor, and cross-agent prompt-cache reuse remains structurally zero, exactly as `docs/shared-agent-prefix.md` and `docs/POINTER_AUDIT.md` §"HONEST CACHE EXPECTATION" already predicted.** No cache win is claimed anywhere in this diff. **What shipped instead:** all 14 `loomwright/agents/*.md` files now declare the identical, one-canonical-order superset `tools: Read, Write, Edit, Glob, Grep, Bash, Task, TaskOutput, LSP, WebSearch, WebFetch` line, with every agent's *observed effective* capability set preserved bit-for-bit via a per-agent `disallowedTools` denylist — derived as `superset − effective`, not `superset − previously-declared`. Four agents (`launch-pad`, `product-owner`, `qa-strategist`, `red-team-reviewer`) deliberately omit `Write`/`Edit` from their new denylists because the harness's `memory: project` grant gives them that capability effectively today even though their prior declared `tools:` never listed it — the discriminating control pair is `product-owner` (memory → gains it) vs. `orchestrator` (no memory, otherwise comparable → does not), confirming the grant is tied to `memory: project` and not to plugin agents generally. This is an explicit, accepted enforcement-model downgrade — recorded rather than shipped silently in `docs/ARCHITECTURE_CONTRACTS.md` §"disallowedTools (Defense-in-Depth)" — moving the allowlist half of per-agent restriction to uniform-by-construction and leaving the denylist to carry the whole restriction; `orchestrator` and `execute-manager` are the two agents with a genuine first-time reliance on it (previously 100% allowlist-enforced with no denylist at all), while `plan-reviewer`/`rubric-grader` see no change in enforcement strength since the denylist was already their surviving mechanism. `code-reviewer` is a partial case surfaced by the Phase 4.5 review and recorded rather than smoothed over: no change for `Write`/`Edit` (its pre-existing denylist covered them), but its old denylist was `Write, Edit, NotebookEdit` only, so its **subagent-spawn block was allowlist-enforced before this change and is denylist-only after** — functionally a no-op, but a real transition. `Task` moves allowlist→denylist on **9** agents in total (the 5 that already declared it — `supervisor`, `execute-manager`, `launch-pad`, `qa-executor`, `review-pr` — are unaffected), of which four (`code-reviewer`, `orchestrator`, `product-owner`, `red-team-reviewer`) carried no prior `Task` denylist at all. Both `docs/ARCHITECTURE_CONTRACTS.md` tables (the `disallowedTools` list, now 14 rows instead of 5, and the Agent Capability Matrix's `Write` column for Product Owner/Red Team Reviewer/QA Strategist) are reconciled against the observed effective sets; `docs/shared-agent-prefix.md`, `docs/POINTER_AUDIT.md`, and the requirement file itself are corrected at source so the floor-clearing premise is not left stale anywhere it is cited; `AGENT_GUIDELINES.md` §"Agent Frontmatter Conventions" drops its two now-false named examples ("Workers can't spawn subagents (no Task tool), Context-Keeper can't run Bash" — both agents now declare those tools and rely solely on the denylist). Token-budget headroom was re-derived live via `scripts/check-token-budget.sh` (not the JSON's stale `measured` field) and shows no breach — tightest row `rubric-grader` at 189 headroom, next `red-team-reviewer` at 311. All 7 CI gates (`check-command-sync`, `check-contract-parity`, `check-doc-currency`, `check-shared-prefix`, `check-skills-index-sync`, `check-token-budget`, `validate-version`) plus the deterministic self-test suite pass green. Counts unchanged (14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 24 hooks). **Open question recorded, not resolved:** `code-reviewer` carries `memory: project` yet its denylist blocks `Write`/`Edit` and it does not gain them (unlike the four agents above) — this falsifies "the four keep Write/Edit so memory writes don't break" as the rationale (the real reason is "preserve the observed effective set"), and is left unresolved by design; this change does not touch `code-reviewer`.

**v15.23.0 — Pin a single deterministic subtask-id scheme (producer rule + parser normalization + regression test):** Executes `.supervisor/requirements/final-state/11-subtask-id-determinism.md` (Single-Agent Path, single subtask). Launch Pad emitted `1, 2, 3` for some briefs and `1a, 1b, 2` for others from byte-identical input, and `loomwright/sdk-spike/src/runner.ts`'s parser retained whichever it parsed verbatim — the in-file comment above the Subtask Structure table regex records this as "how arm 3 ended up with every dependency edge missing." **What shipped:** plain numeric, 1-based, sequential subtask ids are now stated as an explicit RULE (not merely illustrated) in `skills/supervisor-readiness/SKILL.md` §"Subtask Structure", mirrored in `agents/launch-pad.md` Phase 4 DECOMPOSE and `commands/launch-pad.md`'s condensed mirror; a new exported `normalizeSubtaskIds` helper in `runner.ts` builds ONE id→id map — each subtask's 1-based position in the already natural-sorted list — after the existing `\d+[a-z]?` natural sort (never before, which would reintroduce the `10`-before-`2` bug) and rewrites both `id` and every `requires[].from` from that single map, so all **eight** id-bearing parse regexes agree — the Subtask Structure table row, the brace-item `from:`, the `### Subtask N` heading, the four contract-key forms (`subtask_N:` / `# Subtask N` / `S|ST N:` / `Subtask N`), and the indented `from:` list form (counted by `grep -c` on the id capture group, not by eye). The accepted-input regexes are unchanged — tolerance for `1a`/`S3`/`subtask_1`/etc. stays exactly as tolerant, with normalization applied on top, never as a replacement. The mapping is injective (no two source ids collide, unlike naive truncation), identity on already-sequential 1..N briefs, and order-preserving by construction.
