New fixture `sdk-spike/test/fixtures/legacy-alpha-ids-brief.md` (11 subtasks, legacy `1a`/`1b` + a real 10+ count) and 7 new normalization assertions wired into `sdk-spike/test/self-test.sh` (net +6 assertion sites — one replaces an existing check in place; 44 → 50, verified by counting `pass "` call sites against `main`, not estimated) — injectivity, plain-numeric output, a genuine title-order check independent of the renumbering itself, chain-linkage across the shared map, zero dangling `from:` edges, the gapped-id (`1,2,4`) renumbering case, and the unresolvable-`from:` branch (left verbatim so the wave scheduler blocks rather than mis-resolves); the pre-existing `launchpad-brief.md` regression block was updated in place (raw ids `1a,1b,2,10` are now asserted normalized to `1,2,3,4`, with the natural-sort claim moved onto title order so it still actually re-proves the sort, not just the renumbering). **Scope split — read this before assuming what got fixed:** the producer RULE lands on the DEFAULT path (Launch Pad authors every new brief numeric, whatever runner consumes it), but `normalizeSubtaskIds` — the repair for legacy alpha ids — lives entirely inside the **quarantined `loomwright/sdk-spike/` runner (UNCOUNTED, referenced only by the opt-in `--sdk-runner` seam, default OFF)**, exactly as in v15.8.0/v15.11.0. So the dangling-edge failure mode is closed *in the spike runner*; the default Supervisor execution path never reaches this code and is byte-identical with the flag off. No change to the `provides`/`requires` contract schema itself and no `schema_version` bump.

**v15.22.0 — Drain bounding: mechanize the shared ceiling + a termination-only severity floor:** Executes `.supervisor/requirements/final-state/13-drain-bounding-earned-checks.md` (Single-Agent Path, single subtask — `review-heal/SKILL.md` is the hub every other file references, so any split would place it in every lane). **Two corrected premises (both falsified at Launch Pad Phase 2.5, owner picked a corrected direction for each — see the requirement's D1/D2 and the brief's "Provenance" section):** (1) `max_rounds` was never enforced by two independent regimes — it was always the SAME prose-only bound in the shared `review-heal/SKILL.md` §U4 loop body, obeyed on one entry path and not the other (PR #122's 7 rounds vs. item 04's 5 was the same bound obeyed once, not an asymmetry); D2 = mechanize the ONE shared bound rather than add a second counter. (2) The severity floor was removed on purpose by Validate-Then-Fix (a real MEDIUM like #64 must be fixed, not dropped) — reinstating it as a fix-time gate would re-break #64; D1 = a **termination-only** floor: every finding at every severity is still fixed within its round (reading B, "fix-then-stop"), the floor only decides whether another round starts. **What shipped:** `loomwright/scripts/drain-rounds.sh` (new) — a mechanized per-PR round ledger (`init`/`bump`/`check`/`read`) both `/review-pr` entry paths call, replacing the prose-tracked `rounds`/`while rounds < max_rounds` counter; a `--severity-floor` flag (default `HIGH`) and the new `sub_floor_converged` terminal state — a round whose entire fixed yield was sub-floor terminates `READY` without a further all-channel re-scan, but ONLY after a SHA-bound confirming required-check pass on the pushed commit (`headRefOid`-bound, modeled on `automate-helpers.sh`'s existing `ready_sha`/`head_sha` precedent — never a false `READY` on red/unknown/stale-SHA CI); the earned-fallback gate is deliberately NOT re-run on this path, with a proof in-place that it cannot be earned there (sub-floor termination requires `bot_findings != []`, which is mutually exclusive with `no_review_lens_posted`'s empty-findings condition — the non-empty union IS evidence a lens posted), replacing an earlier cut that shipped an unreachable gate falsely advertising the guard. `REVIEW_HEAL_RESULT` v2 gains three additive fields (`termination_reason`, `severity_floor`, `sub_floor_fixed[]`), and `automate-helpers.sh gate-eval` condition 1 now fails CLOSED on `sub_floor_converged` (and on a missing/unreadable `termination_reason`) so it is never auto-merge-eligible — the honest residual is that this trades a full round's all-channel re-scan (a finding round N+1 would have *discovered* is never found) for one fewer scan+validate+fix-worker dispatch, not the "entire PR-#122 wall-clock saving" an earlier draft assumed (pushing a fix still re-triggers `ci`, which is where the wall-clock actually lives). `review-heal` 1.5.0 → 1.6.0, `automate-loop` 1.2.0 → 1.3.0; `review-pr` token budget 27955 → 31453 (measured 25413 → 28875, a real new contract in the preloaded hub skill).

**v15.21.0 — CLAUDE.md diet + a `/dreaming` curation pass to keep it that way:** Executes `.supervisor/requirements/final-state/09-claude-md-diet-dreaming.md` (Single-Agent Path, single subtask — the earlier 2-way split was withdrawn on review evidence: the version-claim surface and the `/dreaming`↔`agent-help.md` mirror both span the proposed lane boundary). **Problem:** every session paid CLAUDE.md's full 44,392-byte size as fixed context tax, and the file grew monotonically with no curation path, while three of its sections (`## The 14 Agent Roles`, `## Common Pitfalls`, `## Plugin Hooks (Quality Gates)`) alone were 63% of it. **The diet (relocate, never delete):** CLAUDE.md is now **15,618 bytes — a 64.8% cut**, well past the ≥50% bar. The per-agent invariants column plus the `/autonomous` orchestration shell, Shared Agent Contract, and Parallel Execution Model relocated to NEW `loomwright/docs/ARCHITECTURE_CONTRACTS.md` §"Agent Invariants" (CLAUDE.md keeps a compact 14-row name/type/purpose list + pointer); the full hook table relocated to NEW `loomwright/docs/HOOKS.md` §"Hook Table" (now the authoritative, always-current source — CLAUDE.md keeps only the `|| true`-convention prose + pointer); seven of ten Common Pitfalls entries relocated to NEW `loomwright/docs/PITFALLS.md` §"Common Pitfalls" (CLAUDE.md keeps the non-negotiable stale-branch trap plus two other every-session reminders); the doc-currency essay compressed into `scripts/check-doc-currency.sh`'s own header comment; the **review-lane contract** ("two review lenses, not four passes" — heal-only drain + Earned Fallback Review) condensed to a one-line invariant under `## Failure-Mode Invariants` pointing at `skills/review-heal/SKILL.md` (it was briefly cut outright before an external review caught that a standing invariant had been deleted rather than relocated); `## Structured Contracts` compressed to pointers (the version-by-version detail was already duplicated in `docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md`, the actual single source of truth). Two inbound pointers repaired in the same commit (`loomwright/docs/TELEMETRY.md` and `loomwright/scripts/stamp-requirement-status.sh`, both previously citing CLAUDE.md sections that moved) plus one the source brief's own sweep missed: `AGENT_GUIDELINES.md`'s own "authoritative hook table lives in CLAUDE.md" claim, now repointed to `HOOKS.md`. The References brace-list gains `PITFALLS` and `HOOKS`. **Gate compatibility, verified not assumed:** `bash scripts/check-doc-currency.sh` exits 0 post-diet; a seeded stale claim (`.claude-plugin/README.md`'s `24 hooks centralized` → `23 hooks centralized`) reproduces `DRIFT [hook-count]` and a non-zero exit, reverted via `git checkout --` back to a clean exit 0 — the gate is still effective, not merely quiet. No count claim was relocated to an ungated new file (the two new docs carry no scanned current-state claim), so `check-doc-currency.sh`'s `FILES` array is unchanged. **Treadmill reduction — DELETE-over-gate:** three prose version annotations DELETED outright rather than re-bumped (`CLAUDE.md`, `.claude-plugin/README.md`, `commands/agent-help.md` — each a directory-tree-diagram restatement of the plugin version that added nothing `plugin.json` doesn't already have); both manifest descriptions' stale "Full version history lives in CLAUDE.md." corrected to CHANGELOG.md. NEW `AGENT_GUIDELINES.md` §"Claim Duplication Rule" documents the resulting priority order (one authoritative machine-readable place → derive-or-drop everywhere else → a sync-checking gate is the last resort) and is now enforceable at the worker DO-side seam via a new committed house rule (see below). **`/dreaming` curation direction (D1 — proposals-only, no new writer):** `commands/dreaming.md` §4 "Proposed CLAUDE.md Updates" extended IN PLACE — a proposal may now also be a **prune** (a section no longer earns its context cost), **merge** (two sections restate each other), or **supersede** (a claim's authoritative home moved) candidate, under the SAME per-item Accept/Reject/Edit gate and the same PENDING USER APPROVAL label; the six-mandatory-report-sections contract under `dreaming.md` §"Reflection Report Sections" stays literally six, and the "CLAUDE.md … stays paste-to-apply" clause in its §"Read-Only Contract" is unchanged and re-verified consistent — there is still no CLAUDE.md writer. The `agent-help.md` mirror (its own restatement of the same read-only contract) synced in the same commit — no gate catches that drift; `check-command-sync.sh` covers only `commands/code-reviewer.md`. **Provenance retirement (D2):** the discharged advisory-surface-freeze house rule (both discharge conditions met — twin-remediation `01-prove-the-loop.md` and `02-curation-anti-rot.md` are both `## Status: done`) retracted via `add-rule.sh --retract --confirm` (never a hand-edit of `process.json`), and its replacement — the Claim Duplication Rule as an enforceable `process` house rule — authored via `add-rule.sh --confirm` in the same PR so the store does not go empty and `session-resume.sh`'s no-house-rules nudge does not start firing every session; `read-rules.sh` verified to return exactly the new rule and not the retracted one. `.supervisor/requirements/twin-remediation/04-claude-md-diet.md` stamped `## Status: superseded-by final-state/09-claude-md-diet-dreaming.md`. **Counts UNCHANGED: 14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 24 hooks** — this change adds/removes none; only the version moved (15.20.0 → 15.21.0), bumped in `plugin.json` + `marketplace.json` in lockstep.

**v15.20.0 — D6: worker shared-context digest + explicit file lanes:** Executes `.supervisor/requirements/final-state/08-worker-context-digest-lanes.md` across a 4-subtask serialized chain (`context-bound` split — >12 files AND >800 changed lines). **Problem:** every spawned worker cold-starts with an empty context and re-derives the same codebase understanding Launch Pad already computed at Phase 3/4, and siblings could silently impact each other's work with no ownership signal — the ST-1/ST-2 divergent-interface incident (conflicting flag shapes for the same concept, shipped because neither worktree could see the other) motivated this. **(1) Digest producer (ST-1).** NEW `scripts/build-context-digest.sh` — a bounded (6000-byte default, `--max-chars`/`CONTEXT_DIGEST_MAX_CHARS` override), fence-aware, fail-safe (always exits 0) extractor that pulls the File Impact Map, deduped interfaces touched, the `## Environment` + `## Skill References` conventions (measured 2026-07-31: the `**Tech Stack:**`/`**Architecture:**` bold-line grep an earlier cut of this builder used matched 0 of 73 real briefs and was dead code), the Subtask Structure table, and the verbatim Subtask Contracts YAML block out of an assembled brief into one `CONTEXT_DIGEST` markdown artifact at `.supervisor/jobs/context-digests/{basename(brief_path)}`. The bound is honored by **per-section budgeting**, not tail truncation: every heading is emitted unconditionally and a clipped section carries its own `_(truncated …)_` marker, so a consumer can always tell "the brief declared none" (`_(none found)_`) from "the cap clipped it"; budget is granted in priority order (contracts, interfaces, sibling summary, conventions, File Impact Map LAST) because tail-first truncation deleted the Cross-lane contracts section outright on 8 of 72 archived briefs — the largest, most-parallel jobs, exactly where lane ownership matters most. A whole-file `[context-digest truncated at N chars]` marker remains as a backstop so the digest is never unbounded (AC2). `docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` gains the `CONTEXT_DIGEST` artifact contract (no `schema_version`, not `SubagentStop`-validated — correctness lives in `scripts/test-context-digest.sh`); Launch Pad Phase 5 PACKAGE gains "Context digest emission" as an unconditional step run immediately after brief assembly, deliberately NOT gated on Phase 5.5 Plan Review (a stray digest from a discarded/failed-review brief is harmless — bounded, gitignored, no downstream consumer without a matching saved brief). Every subtask contract also gains a `lanes:` top-level YAML list (alongside `provides`/`requires`/`external_requires`) declaring the path globs a subtask owns — `skills/supervisor-readiness/SKILL.md` §"Lane Declaration Schema" is the authoritative rule, stated as **reachability** over the `requires` DAG's transitive closure (two subtasks may legally collide iff **neither is reachable from the other**, i.e. unordered relative to each other ("same-wave" is a shorthand for that condition, NOT a computable wave index — see `skills/supervisor-readiness/SKILL.md` §"Lane Declaration Schema")) — deliberately NOT "no direct `requires` edge", which would falsely flag every transitively-ordered pair more than one hop apart (this brief is its own counterexample: subtask 1 and subtask 4 share no direct edge yet are two waves apart via 1→2→4, and legally share `SKILLS_INDEX.md`). The schema also states, explicitly, that sequential lane sharing grants **visibility, not preservation** — nothing automates re-verifying an upstream `provides` symbol still resolves after a downstream edit to the same file; that stays a manual authoring/review discipline. **(2) Task-spawn carrier + lane gate (ST-2).** `skills/async-orchestration/SKILL.md` gains "Context digest pointer" in the Subagent Spawn Contracts: every worker spawn (Single-Agent, Sequential-path, and Execute-Manager-spawned parallel-path) carries the digest as a pointer — path + ≤200-char summary + "Read only the sections you need" — never pasted; worktree-resident workers receive the **main-checkout absolute path** with the prompt saying so explicitly (gitignored `.supervisor/` does not exist inside linked worktrees, the same rule already governing the brief pointer), and `docs/POINTER_AUDIT.md` gains a matching "### Context digest" subsection. `agents/worker.md` gains `out_of_lane` reporting: a worker that writes to a path matching no glob in its own subtask's `lanes` records that path in a NEW, **separate** `out_of_lane` `WORKER_RESULT` field — report-only, never failing the subtask, and deliberately never folded into `outputs_gap` (that field's `completed`⇔`""`/`partial`⇔non-empty biconditional, including its brief-unreadable carve-out, is preserved byte-for-byte). `scripts/validate-worker-result.py` validates `out_of_lane` as an OPTIONAL additive field at `schema_version: 2` — no version bump, following the `memory_candidates` precedent (`docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` §"WORKER_RESULT", the `memory_candidates` field row — cited by anchor, not line number, since this PR's own `out_of_lane` insertion shifts that line) — but, unlike that unvalidated precedent, `out_of_lane` **is** validated, so `scripts/check-contract-parity.sh`'s WORKER_RESULT MANIFEST row is deliberately pinned to include it (an optional-but-validated field must be pinned so the parity gate cannot go green while the validator silently drops it — pinning it is correct; making it *required* would violate the optional-field criterion). **(3) SDK-runner parity + parser hardening (ST-3).** `sdk-spike/src/runner.ts` gains `contextDigestPointer()` (same pointer shape as the Task-spawn path; `undefined`, never a placeholder, when the digest is absent) and `laneGlobs` on each parsed subtask, wired into `workerPrompt()` so SDK-spawned workers start exactly as warm and bounded as Task-spawned ones. Two real parser defects fixed, not hypothetical: the contract parser previously entered ONLY on the heading `### Subtask contracts`, so every other layout real briefs use silently skipped the whole YAML block, leaving every subtask with empty `provides`/`requires` and the wave scheduler marking all of them LAUNCHABLE in wave 1, inverting a sequential plan into unsafe concurrency. The runner now resolves contracts through a full layout ladder — three umbrella heading spellings (`Subtask contracts` / `Provides / Requires Contracts` / `Provides / Requires Schema`, matched case-insensitively at `##`/`###` depth), per-subtask `### Subtask N` headings with no umbrella, un-fenced content, a positional (id-less) fallback binding to Subtask Structure table order, and the id forms `subtask_N:` / `# Subtask N` / `S<N>:` / `ST<N>:`; `from:` references resolve across `1` / `"1a"` / `S3` / `ST1` / `subtask_1` alike (the `subtask_N` spelling is the producer's OWN canonical example in `agents/launch-pad.md`, and dropping it silently turned every dependent subtask LAUNCHABLE), and inline single-line `lanes: ["a.ts", "b.ts"]` populates `laneGlobs` instead of quietly yielding an empty lane. It **fails closed** with an explicit error when a brief has a Subtask Structure table but zero parsed contracts, rather than silently emitting an all-LAUNCHABLE wave. Separately, `lanes:` now resets `listKey` explicitly (added to the recognized list-header set alongside `provides`/`requires`) rather than relying on the accident that quoted-string lane items don't match the brace-form item regex — a brace-form lane entry authored after a non-empty `requires:` would previously have been silently appended into `requires` instead. **(4) Plan Reviewer Criterion 16 + seam tests + this lockstep (ST-4).** NEW **Criterion 16** (`agents/plan-reviewer.md`, conditional on the same `legacy_brief: true`/no-contract-blocks gate as Criterion 12): every subtask with a contract block MUST declare `lanes:` (HIGH if absent), every lane path must resolve to an existing file or an existing parent directory (MEDIUM if neither), and a same-wave lane overlap — computed by reusing Criterion 12's already-validated `requires` DAG, never re-trusting an unvalidated edge set — is a BLOCKING `lane_overlap` finding (the actual divergent-interface hazard this whole brief exists to catch). Criterion count 15 → 16 across every counting reference in `agents/plan-reviewer.md` (the "Never skip criteria" bullet, the `## 16 Review Criteria` heading, the Decision Matrix, the Output Format category enum, and the closing Quality Checklist). NEW `scripts/test-context-digest.sh` (hermetic, offline, bash-3.2-safe, auto-registered by CI's `test-*.sh` loop): exercises the real `build-context-digest.sh` builder in a `mktemp` sandbox to assert the bound + exact truncation-marker form; greps the two committed carrier surfaces for the exact "MAIN-CHECKOUT ABSOLUTE path" pointer phrasing; and — since Criterion 16 is LLM judgment with no other deterministic implementation in the repo — runs a small bash reference implementation of the Lane Declaration Schema's reachability algorithm against four fixtures, including the brief's own named regression case (a 2-wave transitive pair with no direct edge, which a naive "no `requires` edge" check would wrongly flag). **Counts UNCHANGED: 14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 24 hooks — this change adds none.** `loomwright/skills/SKILLS_INDEX.md`'s `supervisor-readiness` row moves 1.2.0 → 1.3.0. `async-orchestration` SKILL.md and its SKILLS_INDEX row are bumped 1.5.1 -> 1.6.0 together IN this PR (an earlier draft of this entry wrongly said the index row predated the branch). No `schema_version` bump anywhere (`out_of_lane` is additive-optional, per above). **Deferred by owner decision, not silently dropped:** the source requirement's re-read-volume/cost-proxy measurement against the arm-2 baseline is a ~$60 multi-session operator-run eval on an external repo, the same class `/automate` cannot drive — recorded as an expected rubric FAIL and left owed against the source requirement, not laundered into a pass. **Review round 2 (4 findings, all fixed in-PR — no version bump, this work is unmerged):** (a) the fail-closed contractless guard in `sdk-spike/src/runner.ts` `parseBrief` gained the `legacy_brief: true` carve-out `agents/plan-reviewer.md` Criterion 12 already grants (and Criterion 16 shares) — without it the guard hard-threw on a shape this repo's own plan gate declared legal, reproduced on archived `2026-07-07-stackpack-mysql-mcp-spinoff.md`, while directing the operator to fix a contracts anchor that legitimately does not exist; the carve-out is asserted in BOTH directions so it never becomes a blanket bypass. (b) per-section budgeting gained a **floor pass** — priority order now governs the surplus, not the whole pool, because the top-priority section could consume all of it and leave every later section a bare `_(truncated)_` with zero content (measured 15 of 72 archived briefs, worst case 4 of 5 sections — this feature's own brief). (c) all budget arithmetic moved from `${#var}` to a `blen()` byte count matching the unit the cap is enforced in — `${#var}` is a CHARACTER count under a UTF-8 locale and a byte count under `C`, so identical input produced different budgets per caller environment (`a — b` is 5 vs 7). **Correction to the first cut of this note:** that divergence is a latent locale-dependent defect, NOT the cause of the one observed cap overshoot — re-measured, the overshoot reproduces identically under both locales and was the fixed-overhead constant (`260`) underestimating the real header overhead, which the floor pass in (b) now leaves slack for. The original claim was asserted from the wrong locale; both defects are real and both are fixed, but the causal link between them was not. (d) the backstop no longer leaves a partial trailing line (`sed '$d'`). All three digest defects were invisible to the fixture suite and only surfaced by replaying the builder over the archived corpus — so `scripts/test-context-digest.sh` now carries a **corpus regression gate** asserting, across every brief in `.supervisor/jobs/`, that no section is contentless, no digest exceeds the cap, and the backstop never fires (0/72 on all three, down from 15/72, 1/72 and 1/72). **Review round 3 (bot review, 5 findings triaged):** two fixed as real — `extract_section` matched its heading as a bare prefix, so a decoy such as `## File Impact Mapping (draft)` appearing before the real `## File Impact Map` started extraction on the decoy and the next-heading exit then shipped the decoy body with the real section never reached (word-boundary guard added; reproduced and pinned by a fixture); and the stdin branch gated on `[ -n "$BRIEF_ARG" ] || [ ! -t 0 ]`, whose first disjunct short-circuits, so `--brief -` with no piped input ran `cat` against a terminal and hung — not a permitted outcome under the always-exit-0 fail-safe contract (tty test is now unconditional; both piped paths regression- tested). `agents/execute-manager.md`'s lane-collision gate now iterates ALL matching siblings rather than a singular `matching_sibling`, since sequentially-ordered siblings may legally share a lane and checking only the first match made escalation depend on iteration order. **One finding was verified as a false positive and is recorded rather than actioned:** `head -c` does cut at raw byte offsets and does split multi-byte characters, but the partial byte is necessarily on the trailing partial line that `sed '$d'` then deletes — all 72 corpus digests plus a 28-cap multi-byte sweep decode as valid UTF-8. That is emergent from the pipeline rather than promised by either half, so it is now asserted as an OUTCOME (every digest decodes as UTF-8) in both gates. Test gaps closed: explicit `out_of_lane: null` (present-with-None, the nullable-required trap from PR #84) now has a case; the `[123]` case the review asked for is recorded as ACCEPTED-by-contract, because `load_block` sources fields only from the markdown carrier and `parse_block` stringifies every scalar, making rule 9's `isinstance` guard unreachable by construction. The corpus gate now pins `LC_ALL=en_US.UTF-8` so a regression from `blen()` back to `${#var}` cannot pass under a C-locale CI shell. **Review round 4 (bot review #2):** the standout finding was real and worse than reported — the new lane-collision gate reused the requires-gap `EXECUTE_CHECKPOINT` shape but has no producer/consumer edge and so no `missing_outputs[]`, and `validate-execute-result.py` rule 6a REQUIRED that array whenever `adjudication_required: true`. Reproduced against the exact checkpoint the prompt emits: `{"ok": false}` — **the gate was unemittable, failing its own SubagentStop hook every time it fired.** Fixed by widening rule 6a to accept EITHER `missing_outputs` (requires gap) or a new `colliding_lanes[]` (lane collision) — a widening, never a weakening: a checkpoint carrying neither is still rejected, and orphan `colliding_lanes` without the flag is still rejected. Added a closed `adjudication_kind` discriminator (`requires_gap` | `lane_collision`; absent means `requires_gap`, so it stays additive with no schema bump) so Supervisor branches deterministically instead of pattern-matching free-text `reason` prose. `agents/supervisor.md` §"Adjudication Handling" gained a two-kind dispatch table and a lane-specific option set — the requires-gap options are defined over a producer/consumer edge that does not exist for a lane collision, and Option C's `inter_subtask_gap` is a **load-bearing control signal** that `/autonomous` EVALUATE Signal 2 keys re-planning on, so a lane collision now fails with the distinct reason `lane_collision` and is deliberately NOT an `/autonomous` re-iteration signal (a lane collision is an authoring defect Criterion 16 should have caught; silently re-planning around it would hide that). Also from this round: `POINTER_AUDIT.md`'s exception-count summary now folds in row 6b; the digest builder's untested fail-safe branches (`--brief <nonexistent>`, non-numeric / zero / negative / sub-floor `--max-chars`) got cases. **Two further defects were found by those new tests, in code added earlier in this same PR:** tier 2's overhead shrink could push POOL just above zero so tier 3 (checking only `POOL < 1`) shipped a digest with 4 of 5 sections a bare marker at `--max-chars` 700-900 — the exact output tier 3 exists to refuse; and `clip` starved any section whose first LINE exceeded its granted floor. Both fixed by deriving the refusal threshold and the per-section floor from ONE constant (`5 * _FLOOR_MIN_USEFUL`) and adding a UTF-8-safe word-boundary fallback to `clip`. A new tight-cap sweep (650..2600, 40 caps) now asserts the real invariant — a digest that gets WRITTEN must be contentless-free, within its cap, backstop-free and valid UTF-8; refusing is always an acceptable answer. **Review round 5 (bot review #3, 7 findings — all validated, all actioned):** the standout was real and, once traced, uncovered a LIVE defect the reviewer had not seen. `parseBrief` recorded a contract key as "claimed to declare items" only when a value sat on the SAME line, so a bare `requires:` followed by prose was never marked unparseable — reproduced: a brief whose table marks subtask 2 BLOCKED scheduled BOTH subtasks into wave 1. Fixing it made two archived briefs throw, which turned out NOT to be false positives but a THIRD unparsed shape: `requires:` followed by bare dash references (`- subtask_1`) rather than `- {...}` brace items. At the prior HEAD those two briefs (`2026-06-02-preflight-sync-gate.md`, `2026-06-06-auto-pr-review-heal.md`) parsed to **wave1=4/4 and 6/6 with ZERO dependency edges** — a live silent-empty-graph the fail-closed guard could not see, because their brace-form `provides` satisfied it. Both shapes are now handled (bare key + prose fails CLOSED; bare references PARSE, recovering 5 and 10 edges respectively) and both are pinned by tests. Also fixed: the `260` fixed-overhead constant in `build-context-digest.sh` was ~21 bytes short of the real frame — the actual cause of this PR's one measured cap overshoot — and is now DERIVED from the same literal heading/title strings the emit block prints, not guessed; `supervisor`'s budget raised 22651 → 24900 after the Adjudication-Handling rewrite left only **45 proxy tokens of headroom**; `execute-manager`'s `measured` reconciled (field said 26357, its own note prose said 28550, the live gate computed 29197 — three numbers for one row, which the MEASURED-mirror check added earlier in this PR could not catch because it compares the JSON field to the table cell and BOTH were consistently stale); `test-suite-helpers-defined.sh`'s `HELPER_RE` widened to catch mid-line `&&`/`||`/`;` helper calls (it previously missed 20+ real call sites in `test-add-rule.sh` alone — a blind spot over this repo's most common assertion idiom, in the very gate that exists because the first such regression escaped); plus three doc-precision fixes (the `supervisor-readiness` authoring bullet no longer restates the batch-based framing its own terminology note forbids, `RESULT_SCHEMAS`'s Launch Pad step-name cross-reference updated to "MATERIALIZE", and `check-contract-parity.sh`'s runtime messages relabelled hook-ENFORCED to match its MANIFEST comment). **Review round 6 (3 findings, all validated and fixed):** a junk file named `--run-id` was git-tracked at the wrapper root — a `pr-postmortem` record written to a file named after the FLAG because the helper's ledger path is POSITIONAL and was omitted. Third sighting of that bug, and this time a routine `git add -A` swept it into a commit. (The reviewer's stated consequence — "PR #99's learning signal never reached the ledger" — checked out FALSE: an identical record with the same pr_url/source/review_rounds is already in `.supervisor/postmortem/results.jsonl`, so re-appending would have duplicated it. The file is a stray artifact, not a lost signal.) Removed, gitignored, and — the durable fix, since no gate read filenames — a new `scripts/test-no-junk-tracked-files.sh` fails CLOSED on ANY tracked path whose basename starts with `-` (auto-registered by the existing `test-*.sh` CI glob; self-tests that it actually catches one). Two contract-doc drifts also fixed: `docs/FAILURE_ESCALATION.md` — the escalation AUTHORITY, and the doc `autonomous-loop` cites as owner of the grep-stable `inter_subtask_gap` string — still described adjudication as single-kind, so an agent reading it rather than `supervisor.md` would adjudicate a lane collision with producer/consumer options and emit `inter_subtask_gap`, triggering a spurious `/autonomous` re-plan; it now carries both raisers, their distinct evidence arrays and option sets, and the explicit non-signal status of `lane_collision`. And `skills/state-management/SKILL.md` (1.4.0 → 1.5.0) gained the `out_of_lane` slot in its `## Worker Results` template — four surfaces instruct Context-Keeper to record it there, but the schema had no field, so the stated rationale ("reaches state.md instead of being silently dropped") was unmet on both non-parallel paths. Adding the junk-file gate also immediately caught a false-positive mode in the round-5 `HELPER_RE` widening — `;`/`&&` inside QUOTED assertion messages read as separators — now closed by blanking quoted spans before the scan. **Review round 7 — the sdk-spike suite was never a CI gate at all:** `loomwright/sdk-spike/test/` is not matched by CI's `loomwright/scripts/test-*.sh` glob, so `self-test.sh` and `digest-lanes.test.sh` had **never run in CI** — while `src/runner.ts` absorbed 13 fix commits on this branch alone, every one of them verified only on a maintainer's laptop. Merely invoking them would not have fixed it: both gate their own execution on `dist/runner.js`, and `dist/` is gitignored, so an unbuilt CI run exits **0 having tested nothing** (reproduced: `mv dist dist.bak` → `digest-lanes.test.sh: 0 failure(s) (fully skipped)`, exit 0). `.github/workflows/ci.yml` gains a `setup-node` + `npm ci` + `npm run build` step that asserts `dist/runner.js` exists BEFORE running either suite and treats the whole-suite `all checks skipped` sentinel as a FAILURE — fail-closed per the bimodal invariant (`CLAUDE.md` §"Failure-Mode Invariants"), because a suite that skipped itself is UNVERIFIED, not clean. **Requirement close-out made mechanical (same round):** Supervisor's completion tail is supposed to stamp the originating requirement file in Beads-absent mode; measured across `.supervisor/requirements/`, **8 shipped requirements carried no stamp at all** (only 2 of the 6 `final-state/` items had one) — the same prompt-instructed-bookkeeping failure this repo already quantified at 560 hook-written `token_ledger` events vs 6 agent-written `phase_transition` events. NEW `scripts/stamp-requirement-status.sh` reconciles it the way `reconcile-resume-state.sh` handles resume state: key on a BYPRODUCT of the work (a brief landing in `.supervisor/jobs/done/` carrying Launch Pad's `- **Source requirement:**` provenance line), never on something an agent must remember to write. Success-only, idempotent, always exits 0, and **path-contained** — the requirement path comes out of generated brief text, so absolute paths, `..` segments, and anything outside `.supervisor/requirements/` are refused (4 traversal cases pinned in `scripts/test-stamp-requirement-status.sh`, 13 assertions). It stamps `## Status: brief-shipped`, deliberately **not** `done`: requirement ACs are prose bullets with nothing mechanical to evaluate, and the first real run surfaced two live counterexamples where `done` would have laundered an unmet requirement — `final-state/08` (measurement AC deferred by owner decision) and `twin-remediation/07` (closed by a brief that shipped the *prep* while the eval itself is still owed). Wired into TWO existing entries, no new hook (count stays **24**): the **`SessionStart`** entry is the load-bearing seam, and `SubagentStop[loomwright:supervisor-runner]` fast-paths agent-owned sessions. **Review caught that the first cut used only the SubagentStop seam and was therefore inert on every default run** — `/supervisor`, `/autonomous` and `/automate` are inline main-thread workflows that never spawn a `supervisor-runner` subagent, so the matcher could not fire on the very sessions whose 8 unstamped requirements motivated the script. With two seams the check-then-append idempotency guard can genuinely race, so runs are serialized by an `mkdir` lock (bash-3.2/BSD safe, stale-lock reclaim after 5 min, released by an EXIT trap); `--dry-run` now reports `would-be-stamped` rather than claiming `stamped` for a run that wrote nothing. Suite 13 -> 17 assertions. CLAUDE.md's hook table (both the telemetry fan-out row and the SessionStart row) updated to match, and the `CONTEXT_DIGEST` contract now states its write-once/never-pruned lifecycle explicitly (bounded at 6000 bytes/job, gitignored, deliberately outlives the job for postmortem inspection). **Review round 8 (bot review #4 — 3 Medium + 5 Low, all validated before actioning):** (a) `build-context-digest.sh` accepted a LEADING-ZERO `--max-chars`, which bash reads as OCTAL inside `$(( ))`. `--max-chars 0089` died with "value too great for base" then `POOL: unbound variable`, **exit 1** — a fatal arithmetic parse error that omitting `set -e` cannot suppress, breaking the file's own ALWAYS-exit-0 contract; and the well-formed variant `020000` silently enforced 8192 instead of 20000. Both reproduce through the DOCUMENTED `CONTEXT_DIGEST_MAX_CHARS` override, so this was rated higher than the review's Medium. Fixed by `10#` base-10 normalization, with three regression cases incl. an assertion that 020000 is honored as 20000. (b) The lane-collision gate emitted its checkpoint INSIDE the innermost sibling loop, so the first collision ended the round and every other colliding path was dropped — and which one got reported depended on iteration order, the exact order-dependence the plural `matching_siblings` had been introduced to remove. Collisions are now accumulated across both loops and emitted in ONE checkpoint, matching `colliding_lanes[]`'s documented batchable shape; the `reason` string now summarizes the set instead of naming a single path. (c) `runner.ts` REQUIRED `out_of_lane` of every worker via the forced-output schema and then never read it — paid for and discarded. Now forwarded into `subtasks_completed[]`. The cross-subtask collision gate itself remains a **recorded parity gap** (the SDK path can still merge two concurrent out-of-lane writers with no adjudication) — reporting is its precondition, not the gate. (d) An unclosed code fence left `extract_section`'s `infence` toggle stuck open, silently blanking every later section — reproduced. Fixed with a whole-file parity pre-pass that neutralizes only the final unmatched opener. **The obvious per-line heuristic was tried first and REGRESSED the primary case** (real briefs carry `# Subtask 1 — ...` at column 0 inside the contract fence, so treating a column-0 heading as proof of an unclosed fence truncated the whole Cross-lane contracts section); the suite now asserts BOTH directions, since a one-directional test would have shipped that regression. (e) `supervisor.md`'s `execute-manager.md:222` citation had drifted (the gate is at :225) — replaced with a descriptive anchor rather than a corrected number, per the standing absolute-line-ref lesson. **Two findings were validated as BY DESIGN and left unchanged, with the reasoning recorded rather than silently dismissed:** Criterion 16's HIGH vs Criterion 12's BLOCKING for a missing declaration (both FAIL the brief per the Decision Matrix, and a missing contract block is structurally broader than a missing `lanes:` list within a valid one), and `schemas.ts` marking `out_of_lane` required while the python validator treats it optional (two different carriers — the SDK's `additionalProperties: false` forced output must declare a field for it to be emittable at all, which the file's own comment already explains). Digest suite 31 -> 36 assertions. **Review round 9 (bot review #5 — 1 HIGH, 1 MEDIUM, 1 LOW, all real):** (a) **`stamp-requirement-status.sh`'s declared path containment was bypassable by a symlink** — reproduced: `.supervisor/requirements/link.md -> <root>/outside/victim.md` was reported "stamped" and the file OUTSIDE the containment root was modified. All four checks were LEXICAL and `-f` follows symlinks. The script's own header calls this input untrusted and containment "the security-relevant part", and the suite was 17/17 green because it covered traversal and absolute paths — the attacks that were thought of, not the class. Fixed with two layers: `-L` rejects a symlinked final component, and physical resolution of the parent (`pwd -P` on both sides, so a symlinked project root like macOS `/tmp` -> `/private/tmp` does not false-positive) catches a symlinked DIRECTORY component that `-L` cannot see. Three cases added, including one asserting the guard does NOT over-reach on a genuine nested requirement. (b) A comment claimed `_FRAME_HEADINGS` "is asserted against those printfs by scripts/test-context-digest.sh, so a reworded heading cannot silently desynchronize the two" — **that assertion did not exist**, a false safety claim, which is worse than none because it tells a maintainer they are covered when they are not. The assertion is now real (structural extraction of both sides) and was verified to CATCH a desync by mutation-testing a reworded heading, not merely to pass. (c) The parity MANIFEST's `EXECUTE_CHECKPOINT` row applied its own "required OR validated-when-present" definition inconsistently. **The asymmetry was older and wider than reported:** `adjudication_required`, `missing_outputs` and `adjudication_options` had gone unpinned since long before this PR, not just the new `adjudication_kind` / `colliding_lanes`. All five conditional adjudication fields are now pinned together, verified to have teeth (renaming `adjudication_kind` in the prompt fails the gate), with the self-test fixture repaired in the same change — the same fixture-drift ST-2 hit, caught here by the suite rather than by CI. Digest suite 36 -> 37, reconciler suite 17 -> 20. Additive on top of v15.19.0.

**v15.19.0 — 4f: Supervisor's frontmatter is skill-routed, not preloaded; 4g: brief staleness gets an anchor:** Executes item 07's 4f and 4g halves of `docs/SPIKES/FINAL_STATE_GOAL.md` (4c — the plugin-wide `tools:` frontmatter unification — is explicitly OUT OF SCOPE here; its deferral record already lives at `.supervisor/requirements/final-state/12-4c-unified-tools-lists.md`). **(1) 4f — skill routing.** `loomwright:supervisor-runner`'s frontmatter `skills:` list is fully routed out — all seven previously-preloaded skills (`workflow-management`, `async-orchestration`, `state-management`, `context-summarization`, `supervisor-readiness`, `commit`, `quality-checklist`) are removed and the `skills:` key is omitted entirely, matching the zero-preload convention already used by `agents/worker.md` / `agents/context-keeper.md` / `agents/plan-reviewer.md`, and matching the inline `/supervisor` path (`commands/supervisor.md`), which has always preloaded none of them. A new named subsection, `agents/supervisor.md` §"Preloaded Skill Routing (4f)", audits all seven against this file's own body and records the disposition of each: `async-orchestration` (~9,889 proxy tokens, the actual double-pay target) and `commit` (~1,134) and `state-management` (~6,885) get replacement phase-entry Reads (Phase 2 PLAN entry for the first, bundled into the existing Phase 4 FINALIZE Read for the second, and a new Phase 4.5 SELF_HEAL Read for the third — needed for the `session_end` JSONL exact-shape dependency self-heal-advisory defers to it for); `workflow-management` (~4,267), `context-summarization` (~1,792), `supervisor-readiness` (~6,926), and `quality-checklist` (~3,209) get no replacement Read at all — each audited to have zero genuine consumption point in this file's own procedure (duplicated content, zero citations, or delegated entirely to a different agent that already preloads it, per the subsection's per-skill evidence). The subsection explicitly engages the Phase 4 §"Protocol authority (read at phase entry)" "refresh guarantee for compressed contexts" rationale that used to justify the Phase 4 `async-orchestration` Read against a frontmatter preload: that specific framing no longer applies verbatim (nothing is preloaded now), but the underlying rationale is preserved in the same location — Phase 2's new Read is this session's genuine first load, and Phase 4's existing Read is now a genuine second load acting as the same compaction-refresh guard across Phase 3 EXECUTE's potentially long span. `docs/prompt-token-budgets.json`'s `supervisor` row is re-baselined 51814 → 21029 measured, with the budget set to measured + ~10% (22651, derived from the first post-routing measure of 20591; later in-PR edits moved measured to 21029, leaving a realized ~7.7% margin that is deliberately not re-raised since the gate is not breached) rather than restoring the row's prior compressed ~1.45% margin — the `note` records this is the initial-budget convention because 4f is a structural re-baseline (a large drop), not a marginal raise, unlike every prior restore on this row. `docs/ARCHITECTURE_CONTRACTS.md` §"Prompt Token Budgets" mirror table updated on all three `supervisor` cells (budget, measured, preloaded-skill count 7→0), footnote ¹ gains a fourth `supervisor` re-measure sentence, and the §-closing frozen-metadata paragraph gains an explicit carve-out for the preloaded-skill-count cell (a genuine change of the column's subject matter, not incidental drift). **Scope of the savings figure (read before quoting it):** the 51814 → 21029 re-baseline is **spawn-time frontmatter weight**, not a session total. The four skills removed outright are an unconditional per-run saving; the three ROUTED skills are a redistribution — a session still pays a routed skill when it reaches the phase that needs it, and `async-orchestration` (~9,889) is Read at BOTH Phase 2 and Phase 4, so a run reaching FINALIZE pays it roughly twice where the preload paid once (the deliberate price of the compaction-refresh guarantee). The unambiguous wins are runs that never reach a given phase, and short or aborted runs. **(2) 4g — brief staleness anchor.** `agents/launch-pad.md` Phase 5 PACKAGE step 3a now unconditionally stamps `- **Base commit:** {sha}` (the `git rev-parse HEAD` at brief-assembly time) under `## Environment`, beside the existing conditional `Source requirement` line. `skills/preflight-sync/SKILL.md` §Protocol step 4 gains a third signal, **(c)** brief-staleness churn — `git log --oneline $BRIEF_BASE_SHA..origin/$BASE_BRANCH -- $ANTICIPATED_PATHS | wc -l` over the already-computed anticipated-path set — recorded in the pre-flight summary / Decisions Log and the Output block's new `Churn since brief:` line. Signal (c) is explicitly ADVISORY ONLY: it is not a third classification input alongside (a) same-file overlap and (b) already-merged equivalent, it never flips CLEAR to OVERLAP by itself, and it degrades silently and fail-safe (no gate, no warning, no classification change) when `$BRIEF_BASE_SHA` is absent, empty, or fails `git rev-parse --verify`, covering every brief written before this change. **Counts UNCHANGED: 14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 24 hooks — this change adds none.** No `schema_version` bump anywhere. Additive on top of v15.18.0.

**v15.18.0 — Fix 7 / D4: two review lenses, not four passes — the drain becomes deterministically heal-only with an earned fallback, and the per-subtask LLM reviewer is gone above threshold:** Executes `docs/SPIKES/FINAL_STATE_GOAL.md` D4 (`docs/SPIKES/EVAL_FINDINGS_AND_FIXES.md` Fix 7) across a 5-subtask serialized chain. **(1) Counter-pressure rule — the authority.** NEW `## Review Counter-Pressure Rule` in `AGENT_GUIDELINES.md` (sibling of §"Read-Before-Write Verification Gate"): a review pass is owed only if it has information the prior pass lacked (same lens over the same diff finds the same things — review count is not review quality), ALSO owed when the pass expected to supply that information verifiably did not run (asserted on posted output, never on run conclusion — citing the #117 evidence below), and deterministic gates are never traded against it. Records the two sanctioned lenses' information advantages (Phase 4.5: working tree + brief + rubric; CI review: fully independent context). **(2) Drain heal-only + earned fallback.** Four surfaces previously disagreed on whether `--until-mergeable`'s drain re-reviews the diff (`skills/review-heal/SKILL.md`, `agents/review-pr.md` — which contradicted itself — `commands/review-pr.md`, and §U4's pseudocode); all four now state identically that the drain is heal-only by default, with ONE exception: a new "Earned Fallback Review" subsection triggers `no_review_lens_posted` (computed from §U1's EXISTING all-channel read — no new fetch — reading the issue-comment channel, never `--json reviews`) and runs exactly one bounded `Task(loomwright:code-reviewer)` diff review per drain run when no review-producing lens actually posted; fails CLOSED **toward running** the review (the opposite direction from the channel-unknown ⇒ `ESCALATED` rule, and said explicitly why). `READY`/`ESCALATED`, never-merges, `--max-rounds`, and the anti-churn guardrail stay byte-for-byte unchanged; the flag-absent diff-only loop stays byte-for-byte unchanged too. **(3) Per-subtask LLM reviewer removed above threshold.** `agents/execute-manager.md`'s per-subtask `Task(code-reviewer)` spawn and its polling arm are gone, and with them the FAIL==3/NEEDS_HUMAN `record_review` CALLS the v15.16.0 fix added — deleted, not re-pointed, because the deterministic gate produces no review decision to log (the Context-Keeper `record_review` OPERATION itself is retained in the accepted set, now with no current caller); `agents/orchestrator.md` §"Review Gate Policy" now applies the SAME rule at every threshold — no paired review subtask is ever generated, the deterministic `outputs_verified` gate plus tests/lint is the per-subtask gate, and Phase 4.5's integrated review is the sole LLM gate; `skills/async-orchestration/SKILL.md`, `skills/workflow-management/SKILL.md`, and `skills/self-heal-advisory/SKILL.md`'s ANTI-OVERLAP rule are synced to match, each citing the Counter-Pressure Rule by path rather than restating it. Side benefit recorded where the change is made: this also eliminates the worktree-isolation cross-file false-positive class (a per-subtask reviewer cannot see sibling worktrees). **(4) Supervisor-side mirrors + schema.** `agents/supervisor.md` and `commands/supervisor.md` (including the `auto_until_mergeable` default-ON description) synced to heal-only + the earned fallback without touching the dispatch paths themselves; `docs/RESULT_SCHEMAS.md` records that Fix 7 required **no** `REVIEW_HEAL_RESULT` schema change (stays `schema_version: 2`) — the fallback's diff review is a normal `CODE_REVIEW_RESULT` emission feeding the existing Validate-Then-Fix counters — and confirms `EXECUTE_RESULT.review_decision` stays required but is reinterpreted as the deterministic gate's outcome, not an LLM decision, above threshold. `skills/quality-checklist/SKILL.md` gains a citation-only gate question. **(5) Measurement recorded, not argued.** `docs/SPIKES/EVAL_FINDINGS_AND_FIXES.md` Fix 7 gains a "Baseline (pre-change)" block: the postmortem corpus (`.supervisor/postmortem/results.jsonl`, n=77 at 2026-07-29; per-source `review_rounds` means legacy 3.42/n50, `automate_drain` 2.82/n17, `manual_postmortem` 3.80/n10, overall 3.34) plus the verbatim re-measure `jq` command, explicitly labeled a TREND baseline mixing requirement difficulty and plugin version across months — NOT a controlled before/after, so a rise afterward is a prompt to investigate, not proof of causation; and the live CI-healing verification captured 2026-07-30 across four PRs (#118: 37 files, `claude-review` pass in 3m53s, 1 posted comment, 0 reviews; #117: 2 files both under `.github/workflows/`, pass in **14s**, 0 comments, 0 reviews — the self-skip; #116: 5 comments, 0 reviews; #115: 12 comments, 0 reviews) with the exact `gh pr view`/`gh pr checks` commands, noting plainly these are four observations on one repo and #117 is one confirming instance of documented upstream `claude-code-action` self-skip behavior, not a measured rate — and that `reviews` was empty on all four, so every real finding arrived as a posted issue comment. `docs/SPIKES/FINAL_STATE_GOAL.md` D4 and execution-order item 4 are marked SHIPPED with the amendment recorded: "the drain becomes heal-only" landed as heal-only **plus** the evidence-gated fallback, because a blanket cut would have left workflow-touching PRs with exactly one LLM lens. **(6) Four token-budget raises** (`docs/prompt-token-budgets.json` + the `ARCHITECTURE_CONTRACTS.md` mirror, same commit): `context-keeper` 3483 → 3818 (marking `record_review` retained-but-uncalled — the operations-table row, the `record_review` Details paragraph, and the Unknown-operation error string, all annotated to say the Phase 3 per-subtask reviewer this served was retired — moved measured 3166 → 3471, leaving only 12 proxy tokens of headroom), `orchestrator` 8665 → 9558 (Review Gate Policy rewrite, +811 measured), `review-pr` 24230 → 27955 (the new Earned Fallback Review subsection is a real new contract, +3386 measured), `supervisor` 51700 → 52568 (heal-only + fallback mirror prose, +855 measured) — each restoring the row's prior proportional margin (the ~10% authoring baseline for the latter three, and `context-keeper`'s own ~10% margin per the initial-budget convention it has held since its PR #116 restore) rather than resetting to a fresh measured+10%. `commands/orchestrator.md` (missed by the source brief's file enumeration; the agent↔command mirror-drift class `check-command-sync.sh` does not cover) synced at every site that promised a per-subtask review subtask, including its worked examples. Version 15.17.0 → 15.18.0 in both manifests (descriptions updated IN PLACE per the anti-rebloat rule). **Counts UNCHANGED: 14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 24 hooks — this change adds none; no `schema_version` bump anywhere.** Additive on top of v15.17.0.

**v15.17.0 — Fix 4 cheap batch: five mechanical prompt hooks become deterministic scripts (4a + 4b + 4e; 4d evaluated and REJECTED on a falsified premise):** Executes the three surviving sub-fixes of `docs/SPIKES/EVAL_FINDINGS_AND_FIXES.md` Fix 4. **(1) 4e — the substance.** Six `SubagentStop` matchers spent a model call to do schema validation; five of them asserted nothing but the presence and shape of a result block. Those five — `worker`, `execute-manager`, `supervisor-runner`, `qa-executor`, `plan-reviewer` — are now `type: command` scripts: a shared `loomwright/scripts/result_block_parser.py` (the `validate-launch-pad-result.py` payload-extraction ladder — `last_assistant_message` -> `result_block` -> `output` -> `agent_output` -> `agent_transcript_path` -> `transcript_path` — plus a last-block-wins nested-YAML-subset parser handling the five YAML null spellings, quoted literals, trailing `#` comments, inline flow arrays, block sequences, and sequences of mappings for the `outputs_verified` shape — a `#` is stripped only where YAML starts a comment, i.e. outside quotes and preceded by whitespace that itself follows real value text, so `status: completed  # done` yields `completed` while `summary: "a # b"` and `tag: v1#2` keep theirs) plus five thin per-schema validators transcribing all 38 numbered rules from the deleted prompts. Every one is **exit-0-by-contract**: they decide via stdout `{"ok": true}` / `{"ok": false, "reason": "..."}`, never via exit status, and any exception still routes through `emit` so a `|| true`-masked crash cannot silently validate nothing. Unparseable stdin returns ok (hook plumbing malformed, do not break the agent loop); a MISSING result block is a distinct `ok: false`. A 313-case `test-result-validators.sh` covers, per schema, valid / missing-block / malformed-payload / missing-required-key / **explicit-null-required-key** (key presence asserted, not truthiness) / cross-field-invariant violations, and asserts its own case ACCOUNTING — the parser sub-harness declares how many checks it ran and the bash side requires that trailer to be present and to match, because an exception used to abort that script mid-way and make later cases VANISH (measured: 290 → 287) while the suite still reported `fail=0`. **The `code-reviewer` prompt hook was deliberately RETAINED** — its cross-field + severity-cap logic is genuine judgement, not presence-checking — and is now the ONE remaining prompt validator on any `SubagentStop` matcher. **(2) The gate that would have died with the prompts.** `scripts/check-contract-parity.sh`'s `hook_prompt()` selected `type=="prompt"` and treated an empty result as a hard `pin-drift` error, so six of its seven MANIFEST rows would have errored out. It gained a command-type fallback that resolves the matcher's validator script and greps THAT source as the rule text. The selection rule is load-bearing and fail-CLOSED: three affected matchers carry more than one `type: command` entry, so it selects the entry whose command references `validate-<x>-result.py` and **hard-errors as `pin-drift` on zero or more than one match**, never falling through. The naive readings were both wrong — concatenating every command entry fails OPEN (`send-telemetry-core.sh` alone mentions `pr_url` / `heal_decision` / `heal_loop_ran` / `summary` / `schema_version`, so pin-drift would pass on a field the real validator dropped), and taking the first entry fails CLOSED on hook ordering nothing asserts. `test-check-contract-parity.sh` gained fixtures for all three branches (right-source selection AND pin-drift still firing when a pinned field is removed; unrelated-scripts-only errors; two validator matches errors) — its previous `make_fixture()` made every matcher prompt-type, so the new branch would otherwise have been covered only by a one-directional "real tree passes" check that a fail-open build satisfies just as happily. **(3) 4a:** `agents/worker.md` and `agents/code-reviewer.md` each state their own 40-turn budget in prose the agent actually reads, **with the honesty caveat stated** — advisory, ~90% adherence, do not expect behavior change. **(4) 4b:** an ANTI-OVERLAP rule in `skills/self-heal-advisory/SKILL.md` Part 2's review-and-fix loop — do not re-derive what a prior gate already found; a prior finding still open in the diff stays in scope. **(5) 4d was evaluated and REJECTED, not silently dropped.** Its premise (split the skill because "Phase 4.5 needs only Part 2" and "reads the whole file per heal iteration") was falsified by measurement: Part 2 invokes **all ten** Part 1 sections, and the Read is once at phase entry, not per iteration. The dependent "4f strictly after 4d" ordering was equally void — `self-heal-advisory` is not a 4f routing target (it is already read-on-demand on both paths; 4f's real double-pay is `async-orchestration` at 9,078 proxy tokens) — and was corrected at all four doc surfaces so item 07 inherits a true premise. The rubric item for 4d was left to FAIL rather than reworded to manufacture a pass. **(6) Honest measurement (AC-9).** The saving is **runtime model calls avoided, NOT prompt-inventory bytes**: five haiku calls per qualifying run, each carrying the finishing agent's transcript at a 30s timeout, become zero-token scripts. 10,409 prompt characters were deleted — 6,754 from `hooks.json`, whose total size fell 17,522 -> 10,912 bytes, plus 3,655 from the two inert `~/.claude/agents/`-compat frontmatter mirrors. **Both are DECODED prompt-string characters**; the same five hooks.json strings measure 6,908 as JSON-escaped source bytes (6,918 counting enclosing quotes), and the encoding is stated because the earlier "6,782 / 3,761 / 10,543" triple reproduced under no encoding at all and the total merely inherited the error. Against that: **5,374 lines of new script** (2,723 parser + validators, 2,651 test harness) — this change adds far more repo LOC than it removes. Prompt-inventory proxy deltas are small and mixed: `worker` -345 and `execute-manager` -472 (frontmatter deletion, and note the proxy sizes the whole `.md` including frontmatter that Claude Code ignores at runtime for plugin-distributed agents), `code-reviewer` **+88** (4a prose; its frontmatter `Stop` hook was untouched by design). **No converted hook has been observed FIRING** — the installed plugin is a copy, not a symlink (verified: different inodes), so hook edits do not take effect until reinstall; self-tests over fixtures are the in-PR evidence and live adherence is an operator step. Version 15.16.0 -> 15.17.0 in both manifests (descriptions updated IN PLACE per the anti-rebloat rule); `skills/self-heal-advisory/SKILL.md` frontmatter bumped 1.4.0 -> 1.5.0 with the `SKILLS_INDEX.md` row in lockstep per the CI-enforced parity gate. **Counts UNCHANGED: 14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 24 hooks** — these are type conversions, not additions; the TYPE MIX moved from 16 command + 8 prompt to **21 command + 3 prompt**, the three survivors being exactly `SubagentStop[loomwright:code-reviewer]`, `Stop`, and `TaskCompleted`. No `schema_version` bump. Additive on top of v15.16.0.

**v15.16.0 — Fix 3 / D5: one writer for progress state, derived `state.md`:** Executes `docs/SPIKES/FINAL_STATE_GOAL.md` D5 (`docs/SPIKES/EVAL_FINDINGS_AND_FIXES.md` Fix 3) — the Supervisor stack needs progress state it can trust because `--continue` re-executes from it, and this repo's own logs measured the miss rate: 785 hook-written `token_ledger` events vs 6 agent-written `phase_transition` events across 11+ sessions, culminating in a 2026-07-27 incident where all 5 subtasks were merged while `state.md` still read `phase: ACQUIRE` / all `PENDING`. **(1) One hook-triggered writer:** NEW `loomwright/scripts/emit-progress-event.sh`, modelled on `emit-token-ledger.sh`'s fail-safe discipline (`set -u`, no `set -e`, `trap 'exit 0' EXIT`, no-op on empty stdin), wired as a `type: command` entry under the EXISTING `loomwright:worker` `SubagentStop` matcher (hooks 22 → 23) — it appends exactly ONE `subtask_complete` JSONL event per worker completion, anchored to the MAIN worktree by name (`git worktree list --porcelain` first entry + a `--show-toplevel` cross-check, never bare `$PWD`/`git branch --show-current`/`dirname` of the git common dir, which are wrong or silently empty from inside a linked worktree). A SECOND `type: command` entry — `reproject-state-on-terminal.sh`, under the existing `PostToolUse[Bash]` matcher — was added during the PR #116 review round (hooks 23 → 24): the worker-triggered emitter can never re-run after the completion tail writes `session_end`, so the projector's terminal-status branch was unreachable on the normal path and `state.md` stayed at `running` on essentially every run. **(2) A derived projector:** NEW `loomwright/scripts/build-state.sh` reads the append-only event log and projects the canonical lowercase `## Session` block into `.supervisor/state.md` via temp-file + rename — `session_id`/`branch`/`status`/`phase` are all derived from event evidence only (never guessed), and the projector performs a targeted in-place edit that preserves `## Decisions Log`, `## Phase Flags`, and `## Checkpoint`. `emit-token-ledger.sh` gets the same worktree-safe anchoring fix (behavior byte-identical when cwd is the main checkout) so a `code-reviewer`/`qa-executor` completion with a worktree cwd still resolves to the same log file the projector reads (the AC-6 session-id join). **(3) Five prompt-instructed mechanisms deleted, not deprecated, across 11 files:** Context-Keeper `set_task`/`set_subtasks`/`update_phase`/`checkpoint`; Execute Manager `queue_ck_update`/`flush_ck_batch` (+ `record_batch`, retired with its two callers `agents/execute-manager.md` and `skills/async-orchestration/SKILL.md`); the Supervisor's inline best-effort `## Session` write on all three mirrored surfaces (`agents/supervisor.md`, `commands/supervisor.md`, `skills/self-heal-advisory/SKILL.md`) plus the progress half of the Session Logging catalog; and the terminal `- status:` flip prose (a sixth mechanism the source requirement didn't name, found during implementation) — the terminal flip becomes a projector derivation from `session_end` instead. `record_decision` is deliberately RETAINED — the Decisions Log is a different concern from progress-state bookkeeping, with 42 live call sites feeding the Phase 1.5 PRE-FLIGHT SYNC gate and the Phase 4.5 completion tail; deleting it (as the source requirement literally named) would have broken both. **(4) Honest measurement, not overstated:** committed line delta -46 across 11 files (121 insertions / 167 deletions); net ~-211 proxy tokens across the three re-measured budgets (`supervisor`, `execute-manager`, `context-keeper` in `docs/prompt-token-budgets.json`) — `execute-manager` alone INCREASES by 423 tokens because a bundled bug fix (the poll loop now records `record_review` on the FAIL==3 and NEEDS_HUMAN terminal branches, which were never recorded before) outweighs its deletion; this is a reliability change first, the token saving is modest, and the live post-change adherence re-measurement could not be produced in this PR (the installed plugin is a copy, not a symlink — edited hooks don't fire until reinstall; a written operator procedure documents the re-measurement instead). **(5) Known residual, documented not papered over:** the terminal `- status:` flip now derives from the agent-written `session_end`, which itself inherits the original miss-rate risk; the base-mismatch cleanup path never emits one and no automated check catches it — degradation is graceful (a stale `running` over-reports activity rather than hiding merged work, and `scripts/reconcile-resume-state.sh` still reports the true position on `--continue`). Version 15.15.0 → 15.16.0 in both manifests (descriptions updated IN PLACE per the anti-rebloat rule). **Counts: 14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 24 hooks (22 → 24); no `schema_version` bump.** Additive on top of v15.15.0.

**v15.15.0 — Fix 1 / D3: decomposition default inverted to single-agent, with fan-out as a stated exception:** Executes `docs/SPIKES/FINAL_STATE_GOAL.md` D3 (`docs/SPIKES/EVAL_FINDINGS_AND_FIXES.md` Fix 1 (a)(b)(c)) — three rules previously combined to guarantee fan-out with nothing opposing them (a "map each criterion to exactly one subtask" instruction, orchestrator's mandatory paired-review restated 12×, and a fast-path unreachable for any multi-criterion requirement), measured at 6.4× dollar cost and ~4× wall clock vs bare Claude for the same 0-defect outcome. **(1) Decomposition Threshold — single home:** NEW `## Decomposition Threshold` section in `skills/supervisor-readiness/SKILL.md` (preloaded by Launch Pad and Supervisor; Orchestrator cites it by path) makes **ONE subtask the default** — acceptance criteria are a checklist for one worker, not a subtask generator. A split now requires one of exactly three named reasons recorded verbatim as `- **Split reason:** <reason>` in the brief's `## Configuration`: `file-conflict` (same-file serialization), `context-bound` (> 12 files OR > 800 changed lines — an initial calibration, not a measured optimum, tuned so the `tree-and-find` corpus entry lands single-agent), `genuine-parallelism` (≥ 2 zero-overlap groups, each ≥ 3 files). Launch Pad and Orchestrator both invert their decomposition guidance to cite this section. **(2) A true Single-Agent Path (Supervisor):** below the threshold, one worker executes ALL acceptance criteria in one context and **no per-subtask Code Reviewer is spawned** — the deterministic gate (`outputs_verified`, tests/lint/LSP) plus the Phase 4.5 holistic review remain the sole review of the integrated result. The pre-existing Sequential Path (`--sequential`, >1 subtask) is unchanged and keeps its per-subtask reviewer — `--sequential` is NOT redefined as the single-agent path. Above the threshold, worktrees, Execute Manager, per-subtask workers/reviewers, and `--max-workers` all keep byte-identical behavior. **(3) Plan Reviewer Criterion 4 inverted:** a 1-subtask brief is now valid and expected; a brief with > 1 subtask and no (or an invalid) `Split reason:` FAILs HIGH; the > 7 upper bound is kept. Criterion 6's parallelism-overlap check vacuously PASSes on a single-subtask brief; Criterion 10's worker-count rule is reworded from "should not exceed the LAUNCHABLE subtasks in the first batch" to "should not exceed the width of the largest parallel batch" (a staged plan's Batch 1 may legitimately be narrower than a later batch's peak). **(4) All restating surfaces synced:** `agents/orchestrator.md`'s 12 paired-review restatements collapsed to the threshold-conditional form (net-reductive — the file has only ~300 proxy tokens of budget headroom); command mirrors (`commands/launch-pad.md`, `commands/orchestrator.md`, `commands/supervisor.md`, `commands/agent-help.md`) and root docs (`README.md`, `AGENT_GUIDELINES.md`, `CLAUDE.md`) hand-synced against the landed agent wording (`check-command-sync.sh` gates only `commands/code-reviewer.md`, so these four mirrors are otherwise unprotected by CI); two residual drift sites the brief's scope missed — a `commands/agent-help.md` walkthrough and `commands/product-owner.md` / `agents/product-owner.md` — reworded from the unconditional paired-review model to the threshold-conditional one. **(5) Stale-spike correction:** `docs/SPIKES/EVAL_FINDINGS_AND_FIXES.md` Fix 1 marked SHIPPED, and its problem-table citation corrected — the mandatory-paired-review rule was originally reported "stated 5×" at 5 line numbers; re-counted by anchor-phrase grep at plan time, the true count in the pre-change file was **12** (not 5), and the correction is recorded rather than silently rewritten. Version 15.14.0 → 15.15.0 in both manifests (descriptions updated IN PLACE per the anti-rebloat rule). **Counts UNCHANGED: 14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 22 hooks; no `schema_version` bump.** Additive on top of v15.14.0.

**v15.14.0 — curation/anti-rot: supersession + retraction across the three curated stores + whole-stack advisory budget + freeze rule (additive):** Executes twin-remediation item 02 (`.supervisor/requirements/twin-remediation/02-curation-anti-rot.md`), lifting the item-02 half of the standing advisory-surface freeze declared in `00-overview.md` §"Standing policy" (no NEW advisory reader/store/emitter may ship until (a) a real before/after measurement has run — item 01 — and (b) supersession/decay/unlearning exists for the existing stores — this item). Pinned normative encoding contract (verified against shipped code before implementation, not re-designed per subtask): three curated stores get three different `supersedes`/retraction encodings because each store's existing code shape constrains it differently. **(1) Rules store** (`.agent/rules/*.json`): a rule object MAY carry an optional `"supersedes": "<rule-id>"` member (declarative — the older rule is left on disk, `read-rules.sh` hides it at read time, single-hop non-transitive); `add-rule.sh` gains the flag on its ADD action plus a NEW `--retract --target <id> --reason <text> --confirm` action (curate-postmortem.sh shape) that removes the rule object outright and PRINTS the reason to stdout (no in-store sidecar — the commit is the durable record). **(2) Orientation store** (`.agent/orientation/*.md`): the same `--supersedes`/`--retract` shape lands in `add-orientation.sh`, and `read-orientation.sh`'s line-1 header parse is converted from a single greedy regex to a per-key extraction — fixing a latent bug where a field appended after `areas` was silently swallowed into the `areas` capture, which is then used as a git pathspec, silently disabling staleness detection; the fix is backward-compatible with the legacy 3-field header. **(3) Lessons store** (`.supervisor/memory/LESSONS.md`): a NEW `write-lessons.sh supersede` verb mirrors the file's own existing `retract` shape (positional `<category> <lesson-text>` or `--hash`, plus `--source`) and additionally REQUIRES `--replacement`; it composes PRE-CHECK → RETRACT → ADD in that load-bearing order — add-then-retract is forbidden because the add path's evict-oldest-from-front logic (the `while ((ec - start + 1) > maxc)` awk loop in `write-lessons.sh`) would silently destroy an unrelated, unsuperseded lesson in a full 3-entry category. A malformed/absent/untrusted supersede target fails loud and leaves `LESSONS.md` byte-identical. Lessons retraction and the postmortem-corpus curation tool already shipped in v15.7.0 (`curate-postmortem.sh`, `write-lessons.sh retract`) and were deliberately NOT rebuilt here. **(4) `## Corpus health` extended** (not duplicated) in `build-insights.sh` with rules/orientation decay flagging (missing/dangling head_sha or age past threshold) — flag-only, never auto-delete. **(5) Whole-stack advisory budget:** additive `advisory_total`/`advisory_total_kind` fields in `emit-token-ledger.sh` (mirrors the `orientation_source` omit-on-invalid convention — always exit 0) plus a per-run `/insights` advisory-budget subsection reporting the total against a documented target, reconciled against (not replacing) the pre-existing era-bucket `advisory_tokens` compute-spend proxy in `build-loop-evidence.sh`. **(6) `/dreaming`** gains per-item Supersede and Retract actions alongside the existing Accept/Reject/Edit (same per-item human gate, no bulk-accept, Reject never writes) plus documentation of an idempotent one-line pointer write into the repo's Claude-harness memory on Accept, executed at repo root only — `.claude/` is gitignored and workers run in worktrees, so this write is documented as a runtime-only, never-from-a-worktree operation. **(7) The freeze rule:** the standing advisory-surface freeze is committed as a `.agent/rules/` entry (category `process`, enforcement `advisory`, provenance pointing at `00-overview.md` §"Standing policy") via `add-rule.sh … --confirm`, so it survives sessions rather than living only in a requirement file. Version 15.13.0 → 15.14.0 in both manifests (descriptions updated IN PLACE per the anti-rebloat rule); `loomwright/skills/rules/SKILL.md` frontmatter bumped 1.1.0 → 1.2.0 (`SKILLS_INDEX.md` row updated in lockstep, per the CI-enforced parity gate) to reflect the substantive supersession/retraction additions ST-1/ST-5a made to its prose. **Counts UNCHANGED: 14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 22 hooks (scripts are uncounted); no `schema_version` bump.** Additive on top of v15.13.0.

**v15.13.0 — pointers-not-payloads + shared agent prefix + async downpricing (additive):** Executes token-economy item 05. **(1) Pointer audit + conversions:** NEW `loomwright/docs/POINTER_AUDIT.md` enumerates every Task-spawn / inter-agent handoff site pasting >~1k chars of file-backed content; each converted to a pointer (absolute path + bounded ≤200-char summary + "Read only the sections you need") or documented inline as a justified exception; Batch-API routing recorded as a roadmap note only — the plugin runs on the Claude Code subscription runtime with no API-key path, so no code. **(2) Shared agent prefix + fail-CLOSED CI gate:** all 14 `loomwright/agents/*.md` prompts open with a byte-identical shared block (order per file: frontmatter → shared block → role content → preloaded skills → volatile) sourced from the NEW canonical `loomwright/docs/shared-agent-prefix.md` — deliberately `docs/`-resident so `check-token-budget.sh` / `check-doc-currency.sh` don't count it as a 15th agent. NEW `scripts/check-shared-prefix.sh` proves byte-identity in CI and fails CLOSED (non-zero exit, no `|| true`); its self-test (`scripts/test-check-shared-prefix.sh`) proves failure on a 1-char drift. Budget raises driven by the block landed in `loomwright/docs/prompt-token-budgets.json` + the `ARCHITECTURE_CONTRACTS.md` mirror with one-line justifications, per the gate's raise rule. **HONEST CACHE FRAMING (normative, do not overclaim):** the shared block does NOT buy cross-agent prompt-cache hits — tools render before the system prompt, so different agent types diverge at position 0 regardless of byte-identical `.md` openings; its value is consistency, dedup, and a smaller prompt inventory. The real cache win is SAME-ROLE respawns (Phase 3 worker waves, heal-loop reviewer/fix-worker spawns) via volatile-last ordering; the ledger measurement plan targets cache-read share on those sequences and expects cross-agent reuse ≈ zero. The block applies to `agents/*.md` ONLY — inline command surfaces never load it, so bullets deduplicated out of an agent's Critical Rules stay RETAINED in the mirrored `commands/*.md` lists. **(3) Cost Profiles async-analysis rows:** `ARCHITECTURE_CONTRACTS.md` §Cost Profiles gains an "Async analysis surfaces" table — `/dreaming` reflection spawns (code-reviewer / red-team-reviewer / qa-executor, REFLECTION mode only: read-only backward log analysis) default to `model: "sonnet"` at spawn time; NEW documented `/dreaming --full-model` flag omits the override (→ `inherit`); the same agents' forward/gating/voter roles are explicitly NOT downgraded; `/pr-postmortem` (inline main-thread) and `/insights` (script-driven) verified to spawn no models today and recorded as such — no rows; rubric-grader already Haiku by frontmatter. **(4) `shared_prefix` ledger marker:** `emit-token-ledger.sh` emits the additive JSON-boolean `"shared_prefix": true` only when `LOOMWRIGHT_SHARED_PREFIX` is exactly `1`; any other/unset value omits the field entirely (fail-SAFE, always exit 0 preserved — same namespace discipline as `orientation_source`, documented beside it in `docs/TELEMETRY.md`); `test-token-ledger.sh` extended with marker-present / unset-absent / non-1-absent cases (env scrubbed via `env -u` so dev exports can't flip assertions). Version 15.12.0 → 15.13.0 in both manifests (descriptions updated IN PLACE per the anti-rebloat rule). **Counts UNCHANGED: 14 agents / 21 commands / 41 skills / 22 hooks (scripts are uncounted); no `schema_version` bump.** Additive on top of v15.12.0.

**v15.12.0 — orientation memos + owned repo map: committed per-area memo substrate, memos-first read seams, completion-tail proposals, `/dreaming` promotion, `orientation_source` ledger attribution (additive):** Executes the orientation-memos-repo-map job. **(1) Orientation-memo substrate:** NEW committed store `.agent/orientation/` (one ≤1000-char memo per area — line 1 a machine-parsed `written_at | head_sha | areas` header comment, line 2 a one-line summary, then a free-form body; documented in the store's README) + fail-safe advisory reader `loomwright/scripts/read-orientation.sh` (per-memo validation with hostile-marker fail-safe-skip, bounded `git log`-based staleness annotation that demotes-never-drops stale memos, ≤3000-char output under a subordination banner, ALWAYS exits 0) + confirm-gated sole writer `loomwright/scripts/add-orientation.sh` (slug path-containment REJECT rules, 1000-char composed-file cap, whitespace-normalized instruction-injection-marker REJECT, temp-file + atomic-mv write, read-back verify with prior-memo restore; a non-TTY run without `--confirm` is a print-only dry-run) — both with hermetic self-tests. **(2) NEW `loomwright/scripts/build-repo-map.sh`:** owned FLAT repo-map builder writing a bounded advisory `.supervisor/repo-map.md` (directory skeleton + language-agnostic exported-symbol scan; Tier B zero-dep find/grep floor, best-effort tree-sitter Tier A that silently degrades; fail-safe, always exits 0) — productizes the parked CODE_GRAPH_OWNERSHIP repo-map tier WITHOUT the PageRank ranker (which failed independent validation and stays unported). **(3) Memos-first read seams:** Launch Pad and Orchestrator consult orientation memos (via `read-orientation.sh`) as the FIRST advisory orientation source, ahead of heavier context; the shared seam is documented in `skills/brain-context/SKILL.md`. **(4) Completion-tail proposal seam:** the Supervisor Phase 4.5 completion tail (`skills/self-heal-advisory/SKILL.md` Part 2 step 2.6) MAY write per-area memo proposals to the gitignored `.supervisor/orientation-proposals/` on PASS / loop-skipped / ESCALATED outcomes only — success-only, fail-safe (any write failure is a silent logged no-op), never gating, and it NEVER writes the committed store. **(5) `/dreaming` promotion flow:** Phase 4 per-item approval lists pending proposals and on Accept promotes via `bash "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/add-orientation.sh" <area-slug> <summary-line> <body-file> --confirm` (values passed as literal argv, never shell-interpolated), deleting the promoted proposal file; Reject deletes without writing. **(6) `orientation_source` ledger attribution:** `emit-token-ledger.sh` gains the additive `orientation_source` field (`memos` | `repo_map` | `graphify` | `none`) read from `LOOMWRIGHT_ORIENTATION_SOURCE` — any invalid/unset value omits the field entirely (fail-safe); `test-token-ledger.sh` extended (valid-present / unset-absent / invalid-absent). Reader-side plumbing only: no in-repo producer sets the env var yet (the SubagentStop hook does not inherit subagent-Bash exports — see `docs/TELEMETRY.md`); a follow-up wires a state-file producer.
